# A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations

Philipp Grohs^(1,2), Fabian Hornung^(3,4), Arnulf Jentzen^(5,6,7),\
and Philippe von Wurstemberger^(8,9)\
\
¹Faculty of Mathematics and Research Platform Data Science,\
University of Vienna, Austria, e-mail: philipp.grohs@univie.ac.at\
²Johann Radon Institute of Computational and Applied Mathematics,\
Austrian Academy of Sciences, Austria, email: philipp.grohs@oeaw.ac.at\
³Institute for Analysis, Karlsruhe Institute of Technology, Germany\
⁴Seminar for Applied Mathematics, ETH Zurich,\
Switzerland, e-mail: fabianhornung89@gmail.com\
⁵School of Data Science and Shenzhen Research Institute of Big Data,\
The Chinese University of Hong Kong, Shenzhen,\
China, e-mail: ajentzen@cuhk.edu.cn\
⁶Faculty of Mathematics and Computer Science, University of Münster,\
Germany, e-mail: ajentzen@uni-muenster.de\
⁷Seminar for Applied Mathematics, ETH Zurich,\
Switzerland, e-mail: arnulf.jentzen@sam.math.ethz.ch\
⁸Seminar for Applied Mathematics, ETH Zurich,\
Switzerland, e-mail: philippe.vonwurstemberger@math.ethz.ch\
⁹School of Data Science, The Chinese University of Hong Kong,\
Shenzhen, China, e-mail: philippevw@cuhk.edu.cn

###### Abstract

Artificial neural networks (ANNs) have very successfully been used in numerical simulations for a series of computational problems ranging from image classification/image recognition, speech recognition, time series analysis, game intelligence, and computational advertising to numerical approximations of partial differential equations (PDEs). Such numerical simulations suggest that ANNs have the capacity to very efficiently approximate high-dimensional functions and, especially, indicate that ANNs seem to admit the fundamental power to overcome the curse of dimensionality when approximating the high-dimensional functions appearing in the above named computational problems. There are a series of rigorous mathematical approximation results for ANNs in the scientific literature. Some of them prove convergence without convergence rates and some even rigorously establish convergence rates but there are only a few special cases where mathematical results can rigorously explain the empirical success of ANNs when approximating high-dimensional functions. The key contribution of this article is to disclose that ANNs can efficiently approximate high-dimensional functions in the case of numerical approximations of Black-Scholes PDEs. More precisely, this work reveals that the number of required parameters of an ANN to approximate the solution of the Black-Scholes PDE grows at most polynomially in both the reciprocal of the prescribed approximation accuracy $`\varepsilon > 0`$ and the PDE dimension $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$. We thereby prove, for the first time, that ANNs do indeed overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes PDEs.

## 1 Introduction

Artificial neural networks (ANNs) (cf., e.g., Goodfellow et al. \[42\], McCulloch & Pitts \[73\], Priddy & Keller \[87\], Schmidhuber \[91\]) have very successfully been used in numerical simulations for a series of computational problems ranging from image classification/image recognition (cf., e.g., Huang et al. \[59\], Krizhevsky et al. \[68\], Simonyan & Zisserman \[96\]), speech recognition (cf., e.g., Dahl et al. \[26\], Hinton et al. \[54\], Graves et al. \[44\], Wu et al. \[101\]), time series analysis (cf., e.g., Goodfellow et al. \[42\], LeCun et al. \[71\]), game intelligence (cf., e.g., Silver et al. \[94, 95\]), and computational advertising to numerical approximations of partial differential equations (PDEs) (cf., e.g., \[6, 7, 8, 28, 30, 34, 48, 53, 66, 70, 77, 79, 88, 97\]). Such numerical simulations suggest that ANNs have the capacity to very efficiently approximate high-dimensional functions. In particular, they indicate that ANNs seem to admit the fundamental power to resolve the curse of dimensionality (cf., e.g., Bellman \[9\]) in the sense that the number of parameters of an ANN to approximate the high-dimensional functions appearing in the above named computational problems grows at most polynomially in both the reciprocal of the prescibed accuracy $`\varepsilon > 0`$ and the dimension $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$. There are a series of rigorous mathematical approximation results for ANNs in the scientific literature (cf., e.g., \[2, 3, 4, 10, 11, 12, 13, 15, 17, 24, 27, 32, 33, 35, 49, 55, 56, 57, 58, 72, 74, 75, 76, 80, 82, 83, 84, 85, 86, 92, 93, 97, 102, 103\] and the references mentioned therein). Some of them prove convergence without convergence rates and some even rigorously establish convergence rates but there are only a few special cases where mathematical results can rigorously explain the empirical success of ANNs when approximating high-dimensional functions.

The key contribution of this article is to disclose that ANNs can efficiently approximate high-dimensional functions in the case of numerical approximations of Black-Scholes PDEs. More accurately, Theorem 3.14 in Section 3 below, which is the main result of this paper, reveals that the number of required parameters of an ANN to approximate the solution of a Kolmogorov PDE with affine linear drift and affine linear diffusion functions grows at most polynomially in both the reciprocal of the prescribed approximation accuracy $`\varepsilon > 0`$ and the PDE dimension $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$. In particular, we thereby prove, for the first time, that ANNs do indeed resolve the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes PDEs. To illustrate the main result of this article (Theorem 3.14 in Subsection 3.6 below), we now present in the following theorem a special case of Theorem 3.14.

###### Theorem 1.1.

Let $`{T,{\mathfrak{c}},p} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{\alpha,\beta} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`\mathbf{a} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, let $`\mathbf{A}_{d} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{\mathbf{A}_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {({\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{1})}},{\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{2})}},\ldots,{\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{d})}})}`$, let $`\varphi_{d} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, let

``` math
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal{N} & {= \cup_{\mathcal{L} \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots\}}} \cup_{{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}})} \in {({{({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{L}})} \times {\{ 1\}}})}}\left( \times_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})} \right),}
\end{aligned}
```

let $`\mathcal{P}:{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}}`$ and $`\mathcal{R}:{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow{\cup_{d = 1}^{\infty}{C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}}}`$ be the functions which satisfy for all $`\mathcal{L} \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots\}}`$, $`{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}})} \in {({{({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{L}})} \times {\{ 1\}}})}`$, $`\Phi = {({(W_{1},B_{1})},\ldots,{(W_{\mathcal{L}},B_{\mathcal{L}})})} \in {( \times_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})})}`$, $`{x_{0} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{0}}},{{x_{1} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{1}},\ldots}},{x_{\mathcal{L} - 1} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{\mathcal{L} - 1}}}}`$ with $`{{\forall k} \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {(0,\mathcal{L})}}}:{x_{k} = {\mathbf{A}_{l_{k}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{k}\hspace{0pt}x_{k - 1}} + B_{k}})}}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{0}},{\mathbb{R}})}}},{{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{0})}} = {{W_{\mathcal{L}}\hspace{0pt}x_{\mathcal{L} - 1}} + B_{\mathcal{L}}}}},
```

and $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {\sum_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{l_{k}\hspace{0pt}{({l_{k - 1} + 1})}}}`$, for every $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, and let $`{(\phi_{d,\delta})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}\delta^{- {\mathfrak{c}}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, $`\left| {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right| \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathfrak{c}}})}}`$, and

``` math
{\left| {{\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}\delta\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathfrak{c}}})}}}.
```

Then

1.  (i)
    there exist unique continuous functions $`u_{d}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} = {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`u_{d}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    {{\left( \frac{\partial u_{d}}{\partial t} \right)\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} + \left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}{\frac{\beta^{2}\hspace{0pt}{|x_{i}|}^{2}}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( \frac{\partial^{2}u_{d}}{\partial x_{i}^{2}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}} \right\rbrack + \left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}{\alpha\hspace{0pt}x_{i}\hspace{0pt}\left( \frac{\partial u_{d}}{\partial x_{i}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}} \right\rbrack} = 0
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} = {(t,x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    there exist $`{\mathfrak{C}} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- {\mathfrak{C}}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, and

    ``` math
    {\left\lbrack {\int_{{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}{dx}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```

Theorem 1.1 is an immediate consequence of Corollary 3.16 in Subsection 3.6 below and the auxiliary results in Lemma 4.2 and Corollary 4.5 in Section 4 below. Corollary 3.16, in turn, is a consequence of Theorem 3.14, the main result of this article. We now provide some explanations and interpretations for the objects appearing in Theorem 1.1 above. The set $`\mathcal{N}`$ in (1) corresponds to the set of all fully-connected artificial neural networks. We thus describe for every $`\mathcal{L} \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots\}}`$, $`{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}})} \in {{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{L}} \times {\{ 1\}}}`$ a neural network $`\Phi = {({(W_{1},B_{1})},\ldots,{(W_{\mathcal{L}},B_{\mathcal{L}})})} \in {( \times_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})})} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ (with $`\mathcal{L} + 1`$ layers, $`\mathcal{L} - 1`$ hidden layers, $`l_{0}`$ neurons in the input layer, $`l_{1}`$ neurons in the first hidden layer, $`l_{2}`$ neurons in the second hidden layer, …, $`l_{\mathcal{L} - 1}`$ neurons in the $`({\mathcal{L} - 1})`$-th hidden layer, and $`l_{\mathcal{L}} = 1`$ neuron in the output layer) as a tuple consisting of its weight matrices $`W_{k} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}}`$, $`k \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,\mathcal{L}\}}`$, and its bias vectors $`B_{k} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}}`$, $`k \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,\mathcal{L}\}}`$. For every artificial neural network $`\Phi \in \mathcal{N}`$ in Theorem 1.1 the number $`\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}`$ consequently corresponds to the number of parameters used to describe the artificial neural network $`\Phi`$. Moreover, for every artificial neural network $`\Phi \in \mathcal{N}`$ in Theorem 1.1 the function $`\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}`$ is the realization of the artificial neural network $`\Phi`$ which is defined using the activation function $`\mathbf{a} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$. The equation in display (4) in Theorem 1.1 is usually referred to as Black-Scholes equations in the financial literature. The functions $`\varphi_{d}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, in Theorem 1.1 are usually called the payoff functions in the Black-Scholes model and are the terminal values of the PDE in (4). Roughly speaking, the conclusion in item (ii) of Theorem 1.1 states that the solutions $`u_{d} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, of the PDE in (4) can be approximated on the unit cube by ANNs without the curse of dimensionality. This can be seen as a partial theoretical justification of the performance of deep learning algorithms for the approximation of solutions of Black-Scholes PDEs, such as, e.g., the algorithm proposed in Beck et al. \[6\] (see, e.g., Section 4.4 in Beck et al. \[6\] for numerical results for the Black-Scholes PDE).

Numerical approximation algorithms for high-dimensional PDEs have been exceedingly studied in the scientific literature (cf., e.g., \[14, 16, 18, 29, 37, 39, 41, 40, 43, 61, 81, 98, 99, 100\] and the references mentioned therein). In particular, it is well-known that Monte Carlo methods (cf., e.g., \[43\] and the references mentioned therein) like the Monte Carlo Euler method (cf., e.g., \[43, Section 7.5 in Chapter 7\]) and the multilevel Monte Carlo Euler method (cf., e.g., Creutzig et al. \[23\], Giles \[38\], Heinrich \[50\], \[51\], and the references mentioned therein) do overcome the curse of dimensionality at one particular fixed point in space. A key contribution of Theorem 1.1 above is to provide an approximation result not only at a single space point but on the entire $`d`$-dimensional unit cube $`{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}^{d}`$ (cf. display (5) above and, e.g., Beck et al. \[6\]).

Our proofs of Theorem 1.1 and Theorem 3.14, respectively, construct suitable ANN approximations using probabilistic arguments. More formally, our proofs of Theorem 1.1 and Theorem 3.14, respectively, employ – besides other arguments –

1.  (i)
    the Feynman-Kac formula for viscosity solutions of Kolmogorov PDEs (cf. Proposition 2.23. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") in Subsection 2.5 below, (208) in the proof of Proposition 3.4 in Subsection 3.2 below, and, e.g., Hairer et al. \[47, Corollary 4.17\]),
2.  (ii)
    Monte-Carlo approximations for the expected value in the Feynman-Kac formula (cf. Corollary 2.6 in Subsection 2.1 below and (217) in the proof of Proposition 3.4 in Subsection 3.2 below),
3.  (iii)
    the fact that the solution of an affine linear stochastic differential equation (SDE) depends affine linearly on the initial value (cf. Proposition 2.21 in Subsection 2.4 below and (204) in the proof of Proposition 3.4 in Subsection 3.2 below), and
4.  (iv)
    an argument assuring the existence of a suitable elementary event on an artificial probability space constructed in the proof (cf. Proposition 3.3 in Subsection 3.1 below and (221) in the proof of Proposition 3.4 in Subsection 3.2 below).

The last argument, item (iv) above, is the crucial ingredient which builds the bridge between the probabilistic arguments of the proof and the deterministic conclusion.

There are several directions for further research arising from this work. After a first preprint of this work has come out, a few research articles exploring such directions and extending the results of this paper have appeared. For example, \[45, 46, 60, 64, 89\] establish results similar to Theorem 3.14 for different kinds of differential equations. More precisely, Jentzen et al. \[64\] considers PDEs with nonlinear but Lipschitz continuous drift coefficients, Hutzenthaler et al. \[60\] considers nonlinear PDEs, Reisinger at al. \[89\] considers value functions of zero-sum games, Grohs et al. \[46\] considers PDEs which can be approximated by a suitable Monte Carlo method, and Grohs et al.  \[45\] considers ordinary differential equations and measures the error on the entire space-time domain. Another direction of further research is to consider different ways to measure the error between the exact solution and the ANN approximation. In particular, Elbrächter et al. \[31\] measure the error with the supremum norm and furthermore are able to improve the speed of convergence by using additional structure on a special subclass of Black-Scholes PDEs. Moreover, Kutyniok et al. \[69\] prove that certain parametric maps can be approximated by ANNs without the curse of dimensionality.

The remainder of this article is organized as follows. In Section 2 we supply several auxiliary results on Monte Carlo approximations (Subsection 2.1), affine functions (Subsection 2.2), SDEs (Subsections 2.3–2.4), and viscosity solutions for PDEs (Subsection 2.5). These auxiliary results are then used in Section 3 to establish that ANNs can approximate the solutions of Kolmogorov PDEs with affine linear drift and diffusion functions without suffering under the curse of dimensionality. In particular, we prove in Theorem 3.14 in Section 3 the main approximation result of this article. In Section 4 we illustrate the application of Theorem 3.14 in the case of the Black-Scholes PDE with different payoff functions such as, basket call options in Subsection 4.3, basket put options in Subsection 4.4, call on max options in Subsection 4.5, and call on min options in Subsection 4.6.

## 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries

In this section we provide several basic and in parts well-known auxiliary results on Monte Carlo approximations (Subsection 2.1), affine functions (Subsection 2.2), SDEs (Subsections 2.3–2.4), and viscosity solutions for PDEs (Subsection 2.5).

### 2.1 Monte Carlo approximations

In this subsection we employ Kahane-Khintchine-type estimates from the literature (cf., e.g., Hytönen et al. \[62, Theorem 6.2.4 in Subsection 6.2b\]) to present the known $`L^{p}`$-Monte Carlo estimate in Corollary 2.6 below. Corollary 2.6 is an immediate consequence of Proposition 2.3 and Lemma 2.4 below. We include Proposition 2.3 and Lemma 2.4 for completeness but refer to the literature for their proofs.

###### Lemma 2.1.

Let $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})`$ be a probability space, and let $`X_{i}:{\Omega\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,n\}}`$, be i.i.d. random variables with $`{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {|X_{1}|} \right\rbrack} < \infty`$. Then

``` math
{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {|{{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack X_{1} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}X_{i}} \right)}}|}^{2} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/2} = {n^{- {1/2}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {|{X_{1} - {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack X_{1}\rbrack}}}|}^{2} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/2}}}.
```

###### Proof of Lemma 2.1.

Note that the hypothesis that $`X_{i}:{\Omega\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,n\}}`$, are i.i.d. random variables assures that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left| {{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack X_{1} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}X_{i}} \right)}} \right|^{2} \right\rbrack} \\
 & {= {\frac{1}{n^{2}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{{i,j} = 1}^{n}{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{({{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack X_{i} \right\rbrack} - X_{i}})}\hspace{0pt}{({{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack X_{j} \right\rbrack} - X_{j}})}} \right\rbrack}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {{= {\frac{1}{n^{2}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {n\hspace{0pt}{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {|{{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack X_{1} \right\rbrack} - X_{1}}|}^{2} \right\rbrack} \right)} = {n^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {|{X_{1} - {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack X_{1}\rbrack \right.}}|}^{2} \right\rbrack}}.}
\end{array}
```

The proof of Lemma 2.1 is thus completed. ∎

###### Definition 2.2.

Let $`{{p,q} \in {(0,\infty)}}.`$ Then we denote by $`{\mathfrak{K}}_{p,q} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty\rbrack}`$ the extended real number given by

$`{\mathfrak{K}}_{p,q} =`$$`\sup\left\{ {c \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}}:\left\lbrack \begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\exists{{\mathbb{R}}\text{-Banach space~}\left. (E,\parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{E}) \right.}}:} \\
 & {{\exists{\text{probability space~}{(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})}}}:} \\
 & {{\exists{{\mathbb{P}}\text{-Rademacher family~}{r_{j}:{{\Omega\rightarrow{\{{- 1},1\}}},{j \in {\mathbb{N}}}}}}}:} \\
 & {{{\exists k} \in {\mathbb{N}}}:{{{\exists x_{1}},x_{2},\ldots,x_{k}} \in {E\backslash{\{ 0\}}}}:} \\
 & {\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{\sum_{j = 1}^{k}{r_{j}\hspace{0pt}x_{j}}}\|}_{E}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} = {c\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{\sum_{j = 1}^{k}{r_{j}\hspace{0pt}x_{j}}}\|}_{E}^{q} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/q}}}
\end{array}\rbrack \right. \right\}`$

and we call $`{\mathfrak{K}}_{p,q}`$ the $`(p,q)`$-Kahane-Khintchine constant.

The next result, Proposition 2.3 below, is, e.g., proved as Corollary 5.12 in Cox et al. \[20\].

###### Proposition 2.3.

Let $`p \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, $`{d,n} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`{\mathfrak{K}}_{p,2} \in {(0,\infty)}`$ be the $`(p,2)`$-Kahane-Khintchine constant (cf. Definition 2.2), let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})`$ be a probability space, and let $`X_{i}:{\Omega\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,n\}}`$, be i.i.d. random variables with $`{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{1}\|} \right\rbrack} < \infty`$. Then

``` math
{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack X_{1} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}X_{i}} \right)}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \leq {\frac{2\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{K}}_{p,2}}{\sqrt{n}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{1} - {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack X_{1}\rbrack}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}}.
```

The next result, Lemma 2.4 below, is, e.g., proved as Theorem 6.2.4 in Subsection 6.2b in Hytönen et al. \[62\].

###### Lemma 2.4.

Let $`{p,q} \in {\lbrack 1,\infty)}`$ satisfy that $`q < p`$ and let $`{\mathfrak{K}}_{p,q}`$ be the $`(p,q)`$-Kahane-Khintchine constant (cf. Definition 2.2). Then

``` math
{{\mathfrak{K}}_{p,q} \leq \sqrt{\frac{p - 1}{q - 1}}}.
```

###### Corollary 2.5.

Let $`p \in {(2,\infty)}`$ and let $`{\mathfrak{K}}_{p,2}`$ be the $`(p,2)`$-Kahane-Khintchine constant (cf. Definition 2.2). Then

``` math
{{\mathfrak{K}}_{p,2} \leq \sqrt{p - 1}}.
```

###### Proof of Corollary 2.5.

Note that Lemma 2.4 assures that

``` math
{{\mathfrak{K}}_{p,2} \leq \sqrt{\frac{p - 1}{2 - 1}} = \sqrt{p - 1}}.
```

The proof of Corollary 2.5 is thus completed. ∎

###### Corollary 2.6.

Let $`p \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, $`{d,n} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})`$ be a probability space, and let $`X_{i}:{\Omega\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,n\}}`$, be i.i.d. random variables with $`{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{1}\|} \right\rbrack} < \infty`$. Then

``` math
{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack X_{1} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}X_{i}} \right)}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \leq {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \frac{p - 1}{n} \right\rbrack^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{1} - {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack X_{1}\rbrack}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}}.
```

###### Proof of Corollary 2.6.

Throughout this proof assume without loss of generality that $`p > 2`$ (cf. Lemma 2.1). Note that Proposition  2.3 and Corollary 2.5 demonstrate that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack X_{1} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}X_{i}} \right)}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} & {\leq {\frac{2\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{K}}_{p,2}}{\sqrt{n}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{1} - {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack X_{1}\rbrack}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}} \\
 & {\leq {\frac{2\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{p - 1}}{\sqrt{n}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{1} - {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack X_{1}\rbrack}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}} \\
 & {{= {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \frac{p - 1}{n} \right\rbrack^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{1} - {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack X_{1}\rbrack}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The proof of Corollary 2.6 is thus completed. ∎

### 2.2 Properties of affine functions

This subsection recalls in Lemmas 2.7–2.8 and Corollaries 2.9–2.11 a few well-known properties for affine functions. For the sake of completeness we include in this subsection also proofs for Lemmas 2.7–2.8 and Corollaries 2.9–2.11.

###### Lemma 2.7.

Let $`{d,m} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`A \in {\mathbb{R}}^{m \times d}`$, $`b \in {\mathbb{R}}^{m}`$ and let $`\varphi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}`$ be the function which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{A\hspace{0pt}x} + b}}.
```

Then it holds for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}}.
```

###### Proof of Lemma 2.7.

Observe that (15) assures that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} & {= {{A\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + b + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({{A \cdot 0} + b})}}}} \\
 & {{= {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({{A\hspace{0pt}x} + b})}} + {A\hspace{0pt}y} + b} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The proof of Lemma 2.7 is thus completed. ∎

###### Lemma 2.8.

Let $`{d,m} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{e_{1},e_{2},\ldots,e_{d}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ satisfy $`e_{1} = {(1,0,\ldots,0)}`$, $`e_{2} = {{(0,1,0,\ldots,0)},\ldots}`$, $`e_{d} = {(0,\ldots,0,1)}`$, let $`{\varphi = {(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\ldots,\varphi_{m})}}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}`$ be a function which satisfies for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

and let $`A \in {\mathbb{R}}^{m \times d}`$, $`b \in {\mathbb{R}}^{m}`$ satisfy $`b = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}`$ and

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
A & {= \begin{pmatrix}
{{\varphi_{1}\hspace{0pt}{(e_{1})}} - {\varphi_{1}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} & {{\varphi_{1}\hspace{0pt}{(e_{2})}} - {\varphi_{1}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} & \ldots & {{\varphi_{1}\hspace{0pt}{(e_{d})}} - {\varphi_{1}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} \\
{{\varphi_{2}\hspace{0pt}{(e_{1})}} - {\varphi_{2}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} & {{\varphi_{2}\hspace{0pt}{(e_{2})}} - {\varphi_{2}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} & \ldots & {{\varphi_{2}\hspace{0pt}{(e_{d})}} - {\varphi_{2}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} \\
\vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots \\
{{\varphi_{m}\hspace{0pt}{(e_{1})}} - {\varphi_{m}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} & {{\varphi_{m}\hspace{0pt}{(e_{2})}} - {\varphi_{m}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} & \ldots & {{\varphi_{m}\hspace{0pt}{(e_{d})}} - {\varphi_{m}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}}
\end{pmatrix}} \\
 & {{= \left( {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(e_{1})}} - \left. {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}\hspace{0pt}\left| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(e_{2})}} - {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} \right|\hspace{0pt}\cdots} \middle| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(e_{d})}} - {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} \right.} \right)}.}
\end{array}
```

Then it holds for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{A\hspace{0pt}x} + b}}.
```

###### Proof of Lemma 2.8.

First, note that (18) implies that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}})}} + {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}}.
```

This, induction, and (19) assure that for all $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} & {= {\varphi\hspace{0pt}\left( {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}{x_{i}\hspace{0pt}e_{i}}} \right)} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}\left( {{x_{1}\hspace{0pt}e_{1}} + {\sum\limits_{i = 2}^{d}{x_{i}\hspace{0pt}e_{i}}}} \right)}} \\
 & {= {{x_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(e_{1})}} - {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}})}} + {\varphi\hspace{0pt}\left( {\sum\limits_{i = 2}^{d}{x_{i}\hspace{0pt}e_{i}}} \right)}}} \\
 & {= {\left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{\min{\{ 1,d\}}}{x_{i}\hspace{0pt}{({{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(e_{i})}} - {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}})}}} \right\rbrack + {\varphi\hspace{0pt}\left( {\sum\limits_{i = {{\min{\{ 1,d\}}} + 1}}^{d}{x_{i}\hspace{0pt}e_{i}}} \right)}}} \\
 & {= {\left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{\min{\{ 2,d\}}}{x_{i}\hspace{0pt}{({{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(e_{i})}} - {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}})}}} \right\rbrack + {\varphi\hspace{0pt}\left( {\sum\limits_{i = {{\min{\{ 2,d\}}} + 1}}^{d}{x_{i}\hspace{0pt}e_{i}}} \right)}}} \\
 & {= \ldots} \\
 & {= {\left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{\min{\{ d,d\}}}{x_{i}\hspace{0pt}{({{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(e_{i})}} - {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}})}}} \right\rbrack + {\varphi\hspace{0pt}\left( {\sum\limits_{i = {{\min{\{ d,d\}}} + 1}}^{d}{x_{i}\hspace{0pt}e_{i}}} \right)}}} \\
 & {= {\left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}{x_{i}\hspace{0pt}{({{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(e_{i})}} - {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}})}}} \right\rbrack + {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}}} \\
 & {{= {{A\hspace{0pt}x} + b}}.}
\end{array}
```

The proof of Lemma 2.8 is thus completed. ∎

###### Corollary 2.9.

Let $`{d,m} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ and let $`\varphi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}`$ be a function. Then the following two statements are equivalent:

1.  (i)
    There exist $`A \in {\mathbb{R}}^{m \times d}`$, $`b \in {\mathbb{R}}^{m}`$ such that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

    ``` math
    {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{A\hspace{0pt}x} + b}}.
    ```
2.  (ii)
    It holds for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

    ``` math
    {{{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}}.
    ```

###### Proof of Corollary 2.9.

Note that Lemma 2.7 establishes that ((i) $`\Rightarrow`$ (ii)). In addition, observe that Lemma 2.8 demonstrates that ((ii) $`\Rightarrow`$ (i)). The proof of Corollary 2.9 is thus completed. ∎

###### Corollary 2.10.

Let $`{d,m} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, let $`\varphi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}`$ be a function which satisfies for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

and for every $`k \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{k}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`k`$-dimensional Euclidean norm. Then there exists $`c \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ such that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\left\| {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}} \leq {{c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}}\quad\text{and}}}\quad{\left\| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}} \leq {c\hspace{0pt}\left\| {x - y} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}}}.
```

###### Proof of Corollary 2.10.

Throughout this proof let $`A \in {\mathbb{R}}^{m \times d}`$, $`b \in {\mathbb{R}}^{m}`$ satisfy for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{A\hspace{0pt}x} + b}
```

(cf. Corollary 2.9) and let $`c \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ be given by

``` math
{c = {\max\left\{ \left\lbrack {\sup_{v \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\backslash{\{ 0\}}}}\frac{\left\| {A\hspace{0pt}v} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}}{\left\| v \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} \right\rbrack,\left\| b \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}} \right\}}}.
```

Note that (27) and (28) assure that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left\| {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}} & {= \left\| {{A\hspace{0pt}x} + b} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}} \leq {\left\| {A\hspace{0pt}x} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}} + \left\| b \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}}} \\
 & {{\leq {{\left\lbrack {\sup_{v \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\backslash{\{ 0\}}}}\frac{\left\| {A\hspace{0pt}v} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}}{\left\| v \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}} + \left\| b \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}} \leq {c\hspace{0pt}{({\left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} + 1})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Furthermore, observe that (27) and (28) imply that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left\| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}} & {= \left\| {{({{A\hspace{0pt}x} + b})} - {({{A\hspace{0pt}y} + b})}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}} = \left\| {A\hspace{0pt}{({x - y})}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}} \\
 & {\leq {\left\lbrack {\sup_{v \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\backslash{\{ 0\}}}}\frac{\left\| {A\hspace{0pt}v} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}}{\left\| v \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\left\| {x - y} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} \\
 & {{\leq {c\hspace{0pt}\left\| {x - y} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this and (29) establishes (26). The proof of Corollary 2.10 is thus completed. ∎

###### Corollary 2.11.

Let $`{d,k,m} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, let $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{k \times m}}`$ be a function which satisfies for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, and let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| : \right.`$ $`{\mathbb{R}}^{k \times m}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{k \times m}`$. Then there exists $`c \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ such that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|} \leq {{c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{{\|\left| {{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right|\|} \leq {c\hspace{0pt}\left\| {x - y} \right\|}}}.
```

###### Proof of Corollary 2.11.

Throughout this proof for every $`{\mathfrak{d}} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathfrak{d}}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathfrak{d}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`\mathfrak{d}`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`{e_{1},e_{2}},`$$`{\ldots,e_{m}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{m}`$ satisfy $`e_{1} = {(1,0,\ldots,0)}`$, $`e_{2} = {{(0,1,0,\ldots,0)},\ldots}`$, $`e_{m} = {(0,\ldots,0,1)}`$, and let $`\varphi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{({m\hspace{0pt}k})}}`$ be the function which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = \begin{pmatrix}
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{1}} \\
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{2}} \\
\ldots \\
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{m}}
\end{pmatrix}}.
```

Note that (31) and (33) ensure that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {\begin{pmatrix}
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{1}} \\
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{2}} \\
\ldots \\
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{m}}
\end{pmatrix} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\begin{pmatrix}
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{1}} \\
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{2}} \\
\ldots \\
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{m}}
\end{pmatrix}}}} \\
 & {= \begin{pmatrix}
{{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{1}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{1}}} \\
{{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{2}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{2}}} \\
\ldots \\
{{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{m}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{m}}}
\end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix}
{\left\lbrack {{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}e_{1}} \\
{\left\lbrack {{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}e_{2}} \\
\ldots \\
{\left\lbrack {{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}e_{m}}
\end{pmatrix}} \\
 & {{= \begin{pmatrix}
{\left\lbrack {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}e_{1}} \\
{\left\lbrack {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}e_{2}} \\
\ldots \\
{\left\lbrack {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}e_{m}}
\end{pmatrix} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\begin{pmatrix}
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{1}} \\
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{2}} \\
\ldots \\
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{m}}
\end{pmatrix}} + \begin{pmatrix}
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{1}} \\
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{2}} \\
\ldots \\
{{({\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}})}\hspace{0pt}e_{m}}
\end{pmatrix}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

This and Corollary 2.10 (with $`d = d`$, $`m = {m\hspace{0pt}k}`$, $`\varphi = \varphi`$ in the notation of Corollary 2.10) imply that there exists $`c \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ such that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\left\| {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{({m\hspace{0pt}k})}} \leq {{c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}}\quad\text{and}}}\quad{\left\| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{({m\hspace{0pt}k})}} \leq {c\hspace{0pt}\left\| {x - y} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}}}.
```

Furthermore, note that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}^{2} = {\sum\limits_{j = 1}^{m}\left\| {{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}e_{j}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}^{2}} = \left\| {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{({m\hspace{0pt}k})}}^{2}
```

and

``` math
{{\|\left| {{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right|\|}^{2} = {\sum\limits_{j = 1}^{m}\left\| {{\lbrack{{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}e_{j}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}^{2}} = \left\| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{({m\hspace{0pt}k})}}^{2}}.
```

Combining this with (35) ensures that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|} = \left\| {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{({m\hspace{0pt}k})}} \leq {c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}}
```

and

``` math
{{\|\left| {{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right|\|} = \left\| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{({m\hspace{0pt}k})}} \leq {c\hspace{0pt}\left\| {x - y} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}}.
```

The proof of Corollary 2.11 is thus completed. ∎

### 2.3 A priori estimates for solutions of stochastic differential equations

In this subsection we establish in Proposition 2.15 below an elementary a priori estimate for solutions of SDEs with at most linearly growing coefficient functions (see (44) in Proposition 2.15 below for details). Our proof of Proposition 2.15 employs the Gronwall integral inequality (see Lemma 2.12 below), a special case of Minkowksi’s integral inequality (see Lemma 2.13. ‣ 2.3 A priori estimates for solutions of stochastic differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") below), and the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy type inequality in Da Prato & Zabczyk \[25, Lemma 7.7\] (see Lemma 2.14 below). The Gronwall inequality in Lemma 2.12 is a well-known result in the scientific literature and its proof is therefore omitted (cf., e.g., Lemma 2.6 in Andersson et al. \[1\] and Lemma 7.1.1 in Henry \[52\]).

###### Lemma 2.12.

Let $`{\alpha,\beta,T} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ and let $`f:{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a $`{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\lbrack 0,T\rbrack})}}/\mathcal{B}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}})}`$-measurable function which satisfies for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ that $`{\int_{0}^{T}{{|{f\hspace{0pt}{(s)}}|}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} < \infty`$ and

``` math
{{f\hspace{0pt}{(t)}} \leq {\alpha + {\beta\hspace{0pt}{\int_{0}^{t}{f\hspace{0pt}{(s)}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}}}}}.
```

Then it holds for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ that

``` math
{{f\hspace{0pt}{(t)}} \leq {\alpha\hspace{0pt}e^{\beta\hspace{0pt}t}}}.
```

The next result, Lemma 2.13. ‣ 2.3 A priori estimates for solutions of stochastic differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") below, follows, e.g., from Garling \[36, Corollary 5.4.2\] or Jentzen & Kloeden \[63, Corollary A.1 in Appendix A\].

###### Lemma 2.13 (Moments of pathwise integrals).

Let $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`p \in {\lbrack 1,\infty)}`$, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})`$ be a probability space, and let $`X:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)}}`$ be a $`{{({{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\lbrack 0,T\rbrack})}} \otimes \mathcal{F}})}/\mathcal{B}}\hspace{0pt}{({\lbrack 0,\infty)})}`$-measurable function. Then it holds for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ that

``` math
{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left| {\int_{0}^{t}{X_{s}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right|^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \leq {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {|X_{s}|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}}}.
```

The next result, Lemma 2.14 below, is, e.g., proved as Lemma 7.7 in Da Prato & Zabczyk \[25\].

###### Lemma 2.14.

Let $`{d,m} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`p \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| :{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}`$, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})`$ be a filtered probability space which fulfils the usual conditions, let $`W:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}`$ be a standard $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-Brownian motion, and let $`X:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}}`$ be an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-predictable stochastic process which satisfies $`{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\int_{0}^{T}{{\|\left| X_{s} \right|\|}^{2}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} < \infty} \right)} = 1}.`$ Then it holds for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`s \in {\lbrack 0,t\rbrack}`$ that

``` math
{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| {\int_{s}^{t}{X_{r}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{r}}}} \right\|^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \leq {\left\lbrack \frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2} \right\rbrack^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\int_{s}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|\left| X_{r} \right|\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}{dr}}} \right\rbrack^{1/2}}}.
```

###### Proposition 2.15.

Let $`{d,m} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`p \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, $`{T,{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2},{\mathfrak{s}}_{1},{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, $`\xi \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| :{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}`$, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})`$ be a filtered probability space which fulfils the usual conditions, let $`W:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}`$ be a standard $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-Brownian motion, let $`\mu:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ be $`{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}/\mathcal{B}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}`$-measurable, let $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}}`$ be $`{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}/\mathcal{B}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m})}`$-measurable, assume for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{\left\| {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\| \leq {{{\mathfrak{m}}_{1} + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|}}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|} \leq {{\mathfrak{s}}_{1} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|}}}},
```

and let $`X:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ be an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths which satisfies that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s.  that

``` math
{X_{t} = {\xi + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}}.
```

Then it holds for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{t}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \\
 & {\leq {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\|\xi\|} + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}T}{2}}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}t} \right)}}} \\
 & {{\leq {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\|\xi\|} + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}t} \right)}}}.}
\end{array}
```

###### Proof of Proposition 2.15.

Throughout this proof assume without loss of generality that $`T > 0`$ and let $`\tau_{n}:{\Omega\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be the functions which satisfy for every $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that

``` math
{\tau_{n} = {\inf{({{\{{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}:{\left\| X_{t} \right\| > n}\}} \cup {\{ T\}}})}}}.
```

Note that the hypothesis that $`X:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths ensures that for all $`t \in {(0,T\rbrack}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\{{\tau_{n} < t}\}} & {= {\{{{\exists s} \in {\lbrack 0,t)}}:{\left\| X_{s} \right\| > n}\}}} \\
 & {= {\{{{\exists s} \in {{\lbrack 0,t)} \cap {\mathbb{Q}}}}:{\left\| X_{s} \right\| > n}\}}} \\
 & {{= \left( {\cup_{s \in {{\lbrack 0,t)} \cap {\mathbb{Q}}}}{\{{\left\| X_{s} \right\| > n}\}}} \right) \in {\mathbb{F}}_{t}}.}
\end{array}
```

This demonstrates that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T)}`$, $`r \in {(t,T\rbrack}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\{{\tau_{n} \leq t}\}} = \left( {\cap_{k \in {\mathbb{N}}}{\{{\tau_{n} < {t + \frac{1}{k}}}\}}} \right) = \left( {\cap_{{k \in {\mathbb{N}}},{{t + {1/k}} \leq r}}{\{{\tau_{n} < {t + \frac{1}{k}}}\}}} \right) \in {\mathbb{F}}_{r}}.
```

The hypothesis that $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})`$ fulfils the usual conditions hence ensures that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T)}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that $`{{\{{\tau_{n} \leq t}\}} \in {\mathbb{F}}_{t}^{+} = {\mathbb{F}}_{t}}.`$ Therefore, we obtain that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that $`\tau_{n}`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-stopping time. Moreover, observe that (45) and the triangle inequality assure that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} & {\leq {\left\| \xi \right\| + \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| {\int_{0}^{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right\|^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}} \\
 & {{+ \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| {\int_{0}^{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}} \right\|^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}.}
\end{array}
```

Next note that Lemma 2.13. ‣ 2.3 A priori estimates for solutions of stochastic differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations"), (44), and the triangle inequality demonstrate that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| {\int_{0}^{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right\|^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} & {\leq \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\left| \int_{0}^{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}} \right\|\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}\left\| {d\hspace{0pt}s} \right|^{p}} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}} \\
 & {\leq {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\|{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}}\|}^{p}\hspace{0pt}\mathbb{1}_{\{{s \leq \tau_{n}}\}}} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}}} \\
 & {\leq {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}})}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}}} \\
 & {\leq {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left( {{\mathfrak{m}}_{1} + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}{\| X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}}} \right)^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}}} \\
 & {\leq {\int_{0}^{t}{\left\lbrack {{\mathfrak{m}}_{1} + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{ds}}}} \\
 & {{= {{{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}t} + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right)}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The Cauchy-Schwarz inequality hence proves that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| {\int_{0}^{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right\|^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \\
 & {\leq {{{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\int_{0}^{t}{1^{2}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right\rbrack^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right\rbrack^{1/2}}}} \\
 & {{\leq {{{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right\rbrack^{1/2}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, note that the hypothesis that $`X:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths shows that $`X:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-predictable stochastic process. The fact that for every $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that $`({{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega} \ni {(t,\omega)}\mapsto\mathbb{1}_{\{{t \leq {\tau_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}}\}} \in {\{ 0,1\}}})`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-predictable stochastic process (cf., e.g., Kallenberg \[65, Lemma 22.1\]) and the hypothesis that $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}}`$ is a $`{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}/\mathcal{B}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m})}`$-measurable function hence ensure that for every $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

``` math
({{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega} \ni {(t,\omega)}\mapsto{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({X_{t}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}})}\hspace{0pt}\mathbb{1}_{\{{t \leq {\tau_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}}\}}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}})
```

is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-predictable stochastic process. Combining this, (44), and (47) with the hypothesis that $`X:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ has continuous sample paths demonstrates that for all $`n \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {({\|\xi\|},\infty)}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\int_{0}^{T}{{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}\mathbb{1}_{\{{s \leq \tau_{n}}\}}} \right|\|}^{2}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} & {\leq {T\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sup\limits_{s \in {\lbrack 0,\tau_{n}\rbrack}}{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}} \right|\|}^{2}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {\leq {T\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sup\limits_{s \in {\lbrack 0,\tau_{n}\rbrack}}\left\lbrack {({{\mathfrak{s}}_{1} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}{\| X_{s}\|}}})}^{2} \right\rbrack} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {{\leq {T\hspace{0pt}{({{\mathfrak{s}}_{1} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}n}})}^{2}} < \infty}.}
\end{array}
```

Lemma 2.14, (53), (44), and the triangle inequality therefore establish that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`n \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {({\|\xi\|},\infty)}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| {\int_{0}^{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}} \right\|^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \\
 & {= \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}\mathbb{1}_{\{{s \leq \tau_{n}}\}}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}} \right\|^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}} \\
 & {\leq {\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}} \right|\|}^{p}\hspace{0pt}\mathbb{1}_{\{{s \leq \tau_{n}}\}}} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right)^{1/2}}} \\
 & {\leq {\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{|{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}})}}|}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right)^{1/2}}} \\
 & {\leq {\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left( {{\mathfrak{s}}_{1} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\| X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}} \right\|}} \right)^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right)^{1/2}}} \\
 & {\leq {\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathfrak{s}}_{1} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}} \right)^{2}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right)^{1/2}}} \\
 & {\leq {\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\int_{0}^{t}{1^{2}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right\rbrack^{1/2}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right\rbrack^{1/2}}} \right)}} \\
 & {{\leq {{{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}T}{2}}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right\rbrack^{1/2}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this, (50), and (52) proves that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`n \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {({\|\xi\|},\infty)}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \\
 & {\leq {\left\| \xi \right\| + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}T}{2}}}}} \\
 & {{+ {\left( {{{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right\rbrack^{1/2}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The fact that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ it holds that $`{|{x + y}|}^{2} \leq {2\hspace{0pt}{({x^{2} + y^{2}})}}`$ therefore demonstrates that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`n \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {({\|\xi\|},\infty)}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p} \\
 & {\leq {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\left\| \xi \right\| + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}T}{2}}}} \right\rbrack^{2}}} \\
 & {{+ {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\int_{0}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \right\rbrack}}.}
\end{array}
```

Next note that (47) ensures for all $`n \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {({\|\xi\|},\infty)}}`$ that

``` math
\begin{aligned}
{\int_{0}^{T}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ s,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} & {{\leq {\int_{0}^{T}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack n^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} = {T\hspace{0pt}n^{2}} < \infty}.}
\end{aligned}
```

Combining this and (57) with Lemma 2.12 (with $`\alpha = {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\|\xi\|} + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}T}/2}}} \right\rbrack^{2}}`$, $`\beta = {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}/2}}} \right\rbrack^{2}}`$, $`T = T`$, $`f = \left( {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \ni t\mapsto\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p} \in {\mathbb{R}}} \right)`$ in the notation of Lemma 2.12) demonstrates that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`n \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {({\|\xi\|},\infty)}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p} \\
 & {{\leq {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\left\| \xi \right\| + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}T}{2}}}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}t} \right)}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Therefore, we obtain that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`n \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {({\|\xi\|},\infty)}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \\
 & {{\leq {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\left\| \xi \right\| + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}t} \right)}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Furthermore, observe that (47) and the fact that $`X:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a stochastic process with continuous sample paths ensure that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}} = t`$. Therefore, we obtain that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\| X_{t}\|} = {\| X_{({\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}})}\|} = {\|{\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}X_{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}}\|} = {\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\| X_{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}}}.
```

Fatou’s Lemma and (60) hence imply for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{t}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} = \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\| X_{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p}} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}} \\
 & {\leq \left( {\operatorname{lim\ inf}\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack}} \right)^{1/p} \leq {\sup\limits_{n \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {({\|\xi\|},\infty)}}}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{\min{\{ t,\tau_{n}\}}}\|}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}} \\
 & {{\leq {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\left\| \xi \right\| + {{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}}}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}t} \right)}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The fact that $`\sqrt{\frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2}} \leq \sqrt{p^{2} - p} \leq \sqrt{p^{2}} = p`$ therefore establishes (46). The proof of Proposition 2.15 is thus completed. ∎

### 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions

In this subsection we establish in Proposition 2.21 elementary regularity properties for SDEs with affine coefficient functions. Our proof of Proposition 2.21, roughly speaking, employs the elementary results in Lemma 2.16 and Proposition 2.18 (which are, loosely speaking, alleviated versions of Proposition 2.21), the well-known fact that modifications of continuous stoch-astic processes are indistinguishable (cf. Lemma 2.17. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") below), the well-known fact that a modification of an adapted stochastic process is an adapted stochastic process (see Lemma 2.19. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") below for details), and a version of the Kolmogorov-Chentsov theorem (see Lemma 2.20. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") below for details). Results similar to Lemma 2.20. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") can, e.g., be found in Cox et al. \[19, Theorem 3.5 in Subsection 3.1\] and Mittmann & Steinwart \[78, Theorem 2.1 in Section 2\]. For the sake of completeness we include in this subsection also proofs for Lemmas 2.17. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") and 2.19. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations").

###### Lemma 2.16.

Let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})`$ be a filtered probability space which fulfils the usual conditions, let $`W:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ be a standard $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-Brownian motion, let $`\mu:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{\mu\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}
```

and

``` math
{{{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

and let $`X^{x}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, be $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic processes with continuous sample paths which satisfy that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s.  that

``` math
{X_{t}^{x} = {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}}.
```

Then it holds for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{0}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{x}} + X_{t}^{y}}} \right)} = 1}.
```

###### Proof of Lemma 2.16.

Throughout this proof let $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ and let $`Y:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ be the stochastic process which satisfies for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ that

``` math
{Y_{t} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({X_{t}^{x} - X_{t}^{0}})}} + X_{t}^{y}}}.
```

Note that the hypothesis that for all $`z \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`X^{z}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths assures that $`Y`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths. Moreover, observe that (65) and (67) ensure that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s.  that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
Y_{t} & {= {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({X_{t}^{x} - X_{t}^{0}})}} + X_{t}^{y}}} \\
 & {= \lambda{(\left\lbrack x + \int_{0}^{t}\mu{(X_{s}^{x})}ds + \int_{0}^{t}\sigma{(X_{s}^{x})}dW_{s} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & \left. - \left\lbrack 0 + \int_{0}^{t}\mu{(X_{s}^{0})}ds + \int_{0}^{t}\sigma{(X_{s}^{0})}dW_{s}\rbrack \right. \right) \\
 & {+ \left\lbrack {y + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{y})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{y})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}} \right\rbrack} \\
 & {= {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y + {\int_{0}^{t}{\left\lbrack {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}} - {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{0})}}} \right)} + {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{y})}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{ds}}}}} \\
 & {{+ {\int_{0}^{t}{\left\lbrack {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}} - {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{0})}}} \right)} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{y})}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

In addition, note that (63) and (64) ensure that for all $`\nu \in {\{\mu,\sigma\}}`$, $`{a,b,c} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\nu\hspace{0pt}{(a)}} - {\nu\hspace{0pt}{(b)}}} \right)} + {\nu\hspace{0pt}{(c)}}} & {= {{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{(a)}} + {\nu\hspace{0pt}{(c)}}} - {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{(b)}}}} \\
 & {= {{{\nu\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}a} + c})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} - {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{(b)}}}} \\
 & {= {{{({- \lambda})}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{(b)}} + {\nu\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}a} + c})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}}} \\
 & {= {{\nu\hspace{0pt}{({{{({- \lambda})}\hspace{0pt}b} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}a} + c})}} + {{({- \lambda})}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}}} \\
 & {{= {\nu\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({a - b})}} + c})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this with (68) implies that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s.  that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
Y_{t} & {= {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({X_{s}^{x} - X_{s}^{0}})}} + X_{s}^{y}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{ds}}}}} \\
 & {+ {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({X_{s}^{x} - X_{s}^{0}})}} + X_{s}^{y}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}} \\
 & {{= {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(Y_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(Y_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The fact that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s.  that

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}},}
\end{array}
```

Corollary 2.10, Corollary 2.11, and, e.g., Da Prato & Zabczyk \[25, Item (i) in Theorem 7.4\] (cf., e.g., Klenke \[67, Theorem 26.8\]) hence demonstrate that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} = Y_{t}} \right)} = 1}.
```

This and (67) imply that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{0}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{x}} + X_{t}^{y}}} \right)} & {= {{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({X_{t}^{x} - X_{t}^{0}})}} + X_{t}^{y}}} \right)}} \\
 & {{= {{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} = Y_{t}} \right)} = 1}.}
\end{array}
```

The proof of Lemma 2.16 is thus completed. ∎

###### Lemma 2.17 (Modifications of continuous random fields are indistinguishable).

Let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, let $`(E,\delta)`$ be a separable metric space, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})`$ be a probability space, let $`{X,Y}:{{E \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ be random fields, assume for all $`\omega \in \Omega`$ that

``` math
{{{({E \ni e\mapsto{X_{e}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}})},{({E \ni e\mapsto{Y_{e}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{(E,{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}},
```

and assume for all $`e \in E`$ that $`{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X_{e} = Y_{e}})}} = 1`$. Then

1.  (i)
    it holds that $`{\{{{\forall e} \in E}:{X_{e} = Y_{e}}\}} \in \mathcal{F}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    it holds that $`{\mathbb{P}}\left( \forall e \in E:X_{e} = Y_{e} \right) = 1`$.

###### Proof of Lemma 2.17. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations").

Throughout this proof assume without loss of generality that $`E \neq \varnothing`$, let $`{(e_{n})}_{n \in {\mathbb{N}}} \subseteq E`$ satisfy that

``` math
{\overline{\{{e_{n}\in E}:{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\}} = E},
```

and let $`\mathcal{N} \subseteq \Omega`$ satisfy that

``` math
{\mathcal{N} = {\cup_{n \in {\mathbb{N}}}{\{{X_{e_{n}} \neq Y_{e_{n}}}\}}}}.
```

Note that the fact that $`X`$ and $`Y`$ are random fields assures that for all $`e \in E`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\{{X_{e} = Y_{e}}\}} = {\{{{X_{e} - Y_{e}} = 0}\}} \in \mathcal{F}}.
```

Hence, we obtain that

``` math
{{({\cap_{n \in {\mathbb{N}}}{\{{X_{e_{n}} = Y_{e_{n}}}\}}})} \in \mathcal{F}}.
```

Combining this and (76) implies that

``` math
{\mathcal{N} = \left\lbrack {\Omega\backslash\left( {\cap_{n \in {\mathbb{N}}}{\{{X_{e_{n}} = Y_{e_{n}}}\}}} \right)} \right\rbrack \in \mathcal{F}}.
```

Moreover, observe that the hypothesis that for all $`e \in E`$ it holds that $`{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X_{e} = Y_{e}})}} = 1`$ ensures that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that $`{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X_{e_{n}} \neq Y_{e_{n}}})}} = 0}.`$ Therefore, we obtain that

``` math
{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{(\mathcal{N})}} \leq {\sum\limits_{n = 1}^{\infty}{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X_{e_{n}} \neq Y_{e_{n}}})}}} = 0}.
```

Next note that (75) implies that for every $`v \in E`$ there exists a strictly increasing function $`n_{v}:{{\mathbb{N}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}}`$ such that $`{{\operatorname{lim\ sup}_{k\rightarrow\infty}{\delta\hspace{0pt}{(e_{n_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(k)}},v)}}} = 0}.`$ Combining this with (74. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")) ensures that for every $`v \in E`$ there exists a strictly increasing function $`n_{v}:{{\mathbb{N}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}}`$ such that for every $`\omega \in {\{{{\forall k} \in {\mathbb{N}}}:{X_{e_{k}} = Y_{e_{k}}}\}}`$ it holds that $`{\operatorname{lim\ sup}_{k\rightarrow\infty}{\delta\hspace{0pt}{(e_{n_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(k)}},v)}}} = 0`$ and

``` math
{{X_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} = {\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}{X_{e_{n_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(k)}}}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}} = {\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}{Y_{e_{n_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(k)}}}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}} = {Y_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}}.
```

This and (78) demonstrate that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left\{ {{\forall e} \in E}:{X_{e} = Y_{e}} \right\} & {= \left\{ {{\forall n} \in {\mathbb{N}}}:{X_{e_{n}} = Y_{e_{n}}} \right\}} \\
 & {{= {({\cap_{n \in {\mathbb{N}}}{\{{X_{e_{n}} = Y_{e_{n}}}\}}})} \in \mathcal{F}}.}
\end{array}
```
