This proves item (i. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")). Combining (79) and (80) hence implies that

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{\mathbb{P}}\left( \forall e \in E:X_{e} = Y_{e} \right) = {\mathbb{P}}\left( \cap_{n \in {\mathbb{N}}}\left\{ X_{e_{n}} = Y_{e_{n}} \right\} \right) = {\mathbb{P}}{(\Omega\backslash\mathcal{N})} = 1 - {\mathbb{P}}{(\mathcal{N})} = 1.}
\end{array}
```

This establishes item (ii. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")). The proof of Lemma 2.17. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") is thus completed. ∎

###### Proposition 2.18.

Let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})`$ be a filtered probability space which fulfils the usual conditions, let $`W:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ be a standard $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-Brownian motion, let $`\mu:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{\mu\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}
```

and

``` math
{{{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

let $`X^{x}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, be $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic processes, assume for all $`\omega \in \Omega`$ that

``` math
{\left( {{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}} \ni {(x,t)}\mapsto{X_{t}^{x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \right) \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}},
```

and assume that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s.  that

``` math
{X_{t}^{x} = {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}}.
```

Then

1.  (i)
    it holds that

    ``` math
    \left\{ \forall x,y \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d},\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}},t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}:X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} + \lambda X_{t}^{0} = \lambda X_{t}^{x} + X_{t}^{y} \right\} \in \mathcal{F}
    ```
    and
2.  (ii)
    it holds that

    ``` math
    {\mathbb{P}}\left( \forall x,y \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d},\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}},t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}:X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} + \lambda X_{t}^{0} = \lambda X_{t}^{x} + X_{t}^{y} \right) = 1.
    ```

###### Proof of Proposition 2.18.

Throughout this proof let $`{Y,Z}:{{{({{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ be the random fields which satisfy for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ that

``` math
{{Y_{(x,y,\lambda,t)} = {{X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{0}}}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{Z_{(x,y,\lambda,t)} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{x}} + X_{t}^{y}}}}.
```

Observe that Lemma 2.16 assures that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {Y_{(x,y,\lambda,t)} = Z_{(x,y,\lambda,t)}} \right)} = {{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{0}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{x}} + X_{t}^{y}}} \right)} = 1}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, note that (86) and the fact that

``` math
\begin{matrix}
\left( {{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}} \ni {(x,y,\lambda,t)}\mapsto{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y},t)} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}} \right) \\
{\in {C\hspace{0pt}{({{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}},{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})}}}
\end{matrix}
```

demonstrate that for all $`\omega \in \Omega`$ it holds that

``` math
{{{Y_{\cdot}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}},{Z_{\cdot}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}}.
```

Combining this, (91), and Lemma 2.17. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") (with $`d = d`$, $`E = {{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$, $`{(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})} = {(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})}`$, $`X = Y`$, $`Y = Z`$ in the notation of Lemma 2.17. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")) proves that

``` math
\left\{ \forall x,y \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d},\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}},t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}:Y_{(x,y,\lambda,t)} = Z_{(x,y,\lambda,t)} \right\} \in \mathcal{F}
```

and

``` math
{\mathbb{P}}\left( \forall x,y \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d},\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}},t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}:Y_{(x,y,\lambda,t)} = Z_{(x,y,\lambda,t)} \right) = 1.
```

This and (90) demonstrate that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left\{ \forall x,y \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d},\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}},t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}:X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} + \lambda X_{t}^{0} = \lambda X_{t}^{x} + X_{t}^{y} \right\} \\
 & {= \left\{ \forall x,y \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d},\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}},t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}:Y_{(x,y,\lambda,t)} = Z_{(x,y,\lambda,t)} \right\} \in \mathcal{F}}
\end{array}
```

and

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\mathbb{P}}\left( \forall x,y \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d},\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}},t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}:X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} + \lambda X_{t}^{0} = \lambda X_{t}^{x} + X_{t}^{y} \right)} \\
 & {= {\mathbb{P}}\left( \forall x,y \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d},\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}},t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}:Y_{(x,y,\lambda,t)} = Z_{(x,y,\lambda,t)} \right) = 1.}
\end{array}
```

This establishes items (i)–(ii). The proof of Proposition 2.18 is thus completed. ∎

###### Lemma 2.19 (Modifications of adapted processes are adapted).

Let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})`$ be a filtered probability space which fulfils the usual conditions, let $`{X,Y}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ be stochastic processes, assume that $`X`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process, and assume for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ that $`{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X_{t} = Y_{t}})}} = 1}.`$ Then $`Y`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process.

###### Proof of Lemma 2.19. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations").

Throughout this proof let $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$. Note that the hypothesis that $`{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X_{t} = Y_{t}})}} = 1`$ ensures that

``` math
{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X_{t} \neq Y_{t}})}} = 0}.
```

This and the hypothesis that $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})`$ is a filtered probability space which fulfils the usual conditions imply that $`{\{{X_{t} \neq Y_{t}}\}} \in {\mathbb{F}}_{0} \subseteq {\mathbb{F}}_{t}`$. Hence, we obtain that

``` math
{{\{{X_{t} = Y_{t}}\}} = {\Omega\backslash{\{{X_{t} \neq Y_{t}}\}}} \in {\mathbb{F}}_{t}}.
```

Moreover, observe that (98) demonstrates that for all $`B \in {\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\{{Y_{t} \in B}\}} \cap {\{{X_{t} \neq Y_{t}}\}}} \right)} \leq {{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {X_{t} \neq Y_{t}} \right)} = 0}.
```

The hypothesis that $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})`$ is a filtered probability space which fulfils the usual conditions therefore implies that for all $`B \in {\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{({{\{{Y_{t} \in B}\}} \cap {\{{X_{t} \neq Y_{t}}\}}})} \in {\mathbb{F}}_{0} \subseteq {\mathbb{F}}_{t}}.
```

Combining this with the hypothesis that $`X`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process and (99) demonstrates that for all $`B \in {\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\{{Y_{t} \in B}\}} & {= {\left( {{\{{Y_{t} \in B}\}} \cap {\{{X_{t} = Y_{t}}\}}} \right) \cup \left( {{\{{Y_{t} \in B}\}} \cap {\{{X_{t} \neq Y_{t}}\}}} \right)}} \\
 & {{= {\left( {{\{{X_{t} \in B}\}} \cap {\{{X_{t} = Y_{t}}\}}} \right) \cup \left( {{\{{Y_{t} \in B}\}} \cap {\{{X_{t} \neq Y_{t}}\}}} \right)} \in {\mathbb{F}}_{t}}.}
\end{array}
```

This establishes that $`Y`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process. The proof of Lemma 2.19. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") is thus completed. ∎

###### Lemma 2.20 (A version of the Kolmogorov-Chentsov theorem).

Let $`{d,k} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`p \in {(d,\infty)}`$, $`\alpha \in {({d/p},\infty)}`$, for every $`{\mathfrak{d}} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathfrak{d}}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathfrak{d}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`\mathfrak{d}`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})`$ be a probability space, let $`D \subseteq {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ be a non-empty set, and let $`X:{{D \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}`$ be a random field which satisfies for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that

``` math
\begin{matrix}
{\sup\left( \left\{ \frac{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{v} - X_{w}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}{\left\| {v - w} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\alpha}}:v,w \in D \cap {\lbrack - n,n\rbrack}^{d},v \neq w \right\} \right.} \\
{\left. \cup \left\{ \left( {\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack \parallel X_{v} \parallel_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}^{p} \right\rbrack \right)^{1/p}:v \in D \cap {\lbrack - n,n\rbrack}^{d} \right\} \cup {\{ 0\}} \right) < \infty.}
\end{matrix}
```

Then there exists a random field $`Y:{{D \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}`$ which satisfies

1.  (i)
    that for all $`\omega \in \Omega`$ it holds that $`{({D \ni v\mapsto{Y_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{k}})} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{(D,{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    that for all $`v \in D`$ it holds that $`{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X_{v} = Y_{v}})}} = 1}.`$

###### Proof of Lemma 2.20. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations").

Throughout this proof let $`g_{n}:{{D \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}}\rightarrow{L^{p}\hspace{0pt}{(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}}}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be functions which satisfy that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`v \in {D \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that $`X_{v} = {g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(v)}}`$ (cf. (103. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations"))), let $`{\mathfrak{c}} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ be a real number which satisfies that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{v,w} \in {D \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{v} - X_{w}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}\left\| {v - w} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\alpha}}
```

(cf. (103. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations"))), let $`{\mathfrak{a}} = {\min{\{\alpha,1\}}}`$, and let $`{( \cdot )}^{+}:{{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)}}`$ be the function which satisfies for all $`q \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that $`{(q)}^{+} = {\max{\{ q,0\}}}`$. Note that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{v,w} \in {D \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left\| {v - w} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{{({\alpha - 1})}^{+}} & {\leq {({{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} + \left\| w \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}^{{({\alpha - 1})}^{+}} \leq \left( {{n\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{d}} + {n\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{d}}} \right)^{{({\alpha - 1})}^{+}}} \\
 & {{= \left( {2\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{d}} \right)^{{({\alpha - 1})}^{+}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this and (104) with the fact that $`{\alpha - {\mathfrak{a}}} = {({\alpha - 1})}^{+} \geq 0`$ ensures that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{v,w} \in {D \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{v} - X_{w}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} & {\leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}\left\| {v - w} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\alpha}} = {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}\left\| {v - w} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathfrak{a}}\hspace{0pt}\left\| {v - w} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\alpha - {\mathfrak{a}}}}} \\
 & {= {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}\left\| {v - w} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathfrak{a}}\hspace{0pt}\left\| {v - w} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{{({\alpha - 1})}^{+}}}} \\
 & {{\leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}\left\| {v - w} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathfrak{a}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {2\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{d}} \right)^{{({\alpha - 1})}^{+}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

This and (103. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")) imply that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{matrix}
{\sup\left( \left\{ \frac{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{v} - X_{w}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}{\left\| {v - w} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathfrak{a}}}:v,w \in D \cap {\lbrack - n,n\rbrack}^{d},v \neq w \right\} \right.} \\
{\left. \cup \left\{ \left( {\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack \parallel X_{v} \parallel_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}^{p} \right\rbrack \right)^{1/p}:v \in D \cap {\lbrack - n,n\rbrack}^{d} \right\} \cup {\{ 0\}} \right) < \infty.}
\end{matrix}
```

Therefore, we obtain that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that $`g_{n}`$ is a globally bounded and globally $`\mathfrak{a}`$-Hölder continuous function. Mittmann & Steinwart \[78, Theorem 2.2\] hence ensures that for every $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ there is a globally bounded and globally $`\mathfrak{a}`$-Hölder continuous function $`G_{n}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{L^{p}\hspace{0pt}{(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}}}`$ which satisfies for all $`v \in {D \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}}`$ that $`{G_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(v)}} = {g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(v)}}`$. This assures that there exist random fields $`\xi_{n}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy

1.  (a)
    that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`v \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that $`{(\xi_{n})}_{v} = {G_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(v)}}`$ and
2.  (b)
    that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{cl}
     & {\sup\limits_{\substack{{{v,w} \in {\lbrack{- n},{n + 1})}^{d}}, \\ v \neq w}}\frac{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{{(\xi_{n})}_{v} - {(\xi_{n})}_{w}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}{\left\| {v - w} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathfrak{a}}}} \\
     & {\leq {\sup\limits_{\substack{{{v,w} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}, \\ v \neq w}}\frac{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{{(\xi_{n})}_{v} - {(\xi_{n})}_{w}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}{\left\| {v - w} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathfrak{a}}}} < \infty}
    \end{array}
    ```
    ``` math
    {{\text{and}\mspace{39mu}{\sup\limits_{v \in {\lbrack{- n},{n + 1})}^{d}}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{(\xi_{n})}_{v}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}} \leq {\sup\limits_{v \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{(\xi_{n})}_{v}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}} < \infty}.
    ```

Combining this and, e.g., Revuz & Yor \[90, Theorem 2.1 in Section 2 in Chapter I\] (with $`X = \xi_{n}`$, $`\gamma = p`$, $`d = d`$, $`\varepsilon = {{{\mathfrak{a}}\hspace{0pt}p} - d}`$ in the notation of \[90, Theorem 2.1 in Section 2 in Chapter I\]) ensures that there exist random fields $`Y_{n}:{{{\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy

1.  (A)
    that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\omega \in \Omega`$ it holds that

    ``` math
    \left( {{\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d} \ni v\mapsto{{(Y_{n})}_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{k}} \right) \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}}
    ```
    and
2.  (B)
    that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`v \in {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({{(Y_{n})}_{v} = {(\xi_{n})}_{v}})}} = 1}.`$

The fact that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`v \in {D \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}}`$ it holds that $`{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X_{v} = {(\xi_{n})}_{v}})}} = 1`$ therefore implies that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`v \in {D \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({{(Y_{n})}_{v} = X_{v}})}} = 1}.
```

This assures that for all $`{n \in {\mathbb{N}}},{{m \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 1,n\rbrack}}},{v \in {D \cap {\lbrack{- m},m\rbrack}^{d}}}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({{\{{{(Y_{n})}_{v} = X_{v}}\}} \cap {\{{{(Y_{m})}_{v} = X_{v}}\}}})}} = 1}.
```

The fact that for all $`{n \in {\mathbb{N}}},{{m \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 1,n\rbrack}}},{v \in {D \cap {\lbrack{- m},m\rbrack}^{d}}}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\{{{(Y_{n})}_{v} = X_{v}}\}} \cap {\{{{(Y_{m})}_{v} = X_{v}}\}}} \subseteq {\{{{(Y_{n})}_{v} = {(Y_{m})}_{v}}\}}
```

therefore demonstrates that for all $`{n \in {\mathbb{N}}},{{m \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 1,n\rbrack}}},{v \in {D \cap {\lbrack{- m},m\rbrack}^{d}}}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({{(Y_{n})}_{v} = {(Y_{m})}_{v}})}} = 1}.
```

Combining this with (110) and Lemma 2.17. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") (with $`d = k`$, $`E = {D \cap {\lbrack{- m},m\rbrack}^{d}}`$ for $`m \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 1,n\rbrack}}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ in the notation of Lemma 2.17. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")) establishes that for all $`{n \in {\mathbb{N}}},`$ $`m \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 1,n\rbrack}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{\mathbb{P}}\left( \forall v \in D \cap {\lbrack - m,m\rbrack}^{d}:{(Y_{n})}_{v} = {(Y_{m})}_{v} \right) = 1.
```

Next let $`\Pi \in \mathcal{F}`$ be the event given by

``` math
{\Pi = \left\{ {{{\forall n} \in {\mathbb{N}}},{{m \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 1,n\rbrack}}},{v \in {D \cap {\lbrack{- m},m\rbrack}^{d}}}}}:{{(Y_{n})}_{v} = {(Y_{m})}_{v}} \right\}}.
```

Observe that (116) and (115) show that

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{\mathbb{P}}{(\Pi)} = {\mathbb{P}}\left( \cap_{n = 1}^{\infty} \cap_{m = 1}^{n}\left\{ \forall v \in D \cap {\lbrack - m,m\rbrack}^{d}:{(Y_{n})}_{v} = {(Y_{m})}_{v} \right\} \right) = 1.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, note that (116) ensures that there exists a unique random field $`Z:{{D \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}`$ which satisfies

1.  (I)
    that for all $`\omega \in \Pi`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`v \in {D \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}}`$ it holds that $`{Z_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} = {{(Y_{n})}_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}`$ and
2.  (II)
    that for $`\omega \in {\Omega\backslash\Pi}`$, $`v \in D`$ it holds that $`{Z_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} = 0`$.

Observe that (I), (111), and (117) demonstrate that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`v \in {D \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({Z_{v} = X_{v}})}} & {= {{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({{\{{Z_{v} = X_{v}}\}} \cap \Pi})}}} \\
 & {{= {{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({{\{{{(Y_{n})}_{v} = X_{v}}\}} \cap \Pi})}} = {{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({{(Y_{n})}_{v} = X_{v}})}} = 1}.}
\end{array}
```

This shows that for all $`v \in D`$ it holds that

``` math
{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({Z_{v} = X_{v}})}} = 1}.
```

Moreover, observe that (110) and (I) imply that for all $`\omega \in \Pi`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left( {{D \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}} \ni v\mapsto{Z_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{k}} \right) \\
 & {{= \left( {{D \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}} \ni v\mapsto{{(Y_{n})}_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{k}} \right) \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({D \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}},{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

This ensures that for all $`\omega \in \Pi`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{\left( {D \ni v\mapsto{Z_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{k}} \right) \in {C\hspace{0pt}{(D,{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

In addition, note that (II) assures that for all $`\omega \in {\Omega\backslash\Pi}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{\left( {D \ni v\mapsto{Z_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{k}} \right) = \left( {D \ni v\mapsto 0 \in {\mathbb{R}}^{k}} \right) \in {C\hspace{0pt}{(D,{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this and (121) demonstrates that for all $`\omega \in \Omega`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{\left( {D \ni v\mapsto{Z_{v}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{k}} \right) \in {C\hspace{0pt}{(D,{\mathbb{R}}^{k})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

This and (119) complete the proof of Lemma 2.20. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations"). ∎

###### Proposition 2.21.

Let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})`$ be a filtered probability space which fulfils the usual conditions, let $`W:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ be a standard $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-Brownian motion, and let $`\mu:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{\mu\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}
```

and

``` math
{{{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}}.
```

Then there exist up to indistinguishability unique $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic processes with continuous sample paths $`X^{x}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, which satisfy

1.  (i)
    that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that

    ``` math
    X_{t}^{x} = {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}
    ```
    and
2.  (ii)
    that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`\omega \in \Omega`$ it holds that

    ``` math
    {{{X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{0}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} + {X_{t}^{y}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}}}.
    ```

###### Proof of Proposition 2.21.

Throughout this proof let $`p \in {({2\hspace{0pt}{({d + 1})}},\infty)}`$, for every $`k \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{k}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`k`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| {}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, let $`{{\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{s}}} \in {(0,\infty)}`$ satisfy for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{\left\| {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \leq {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{\left\| {{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \leq {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\left\| {x - y} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}}
```

and

``` math
{{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}} \leq {{{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}}\quad\text{and}}}\quad{{\|\left| {{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}\left\| {x - y} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}}
```

(cf. Corollary 2.10 and Corollary 2.11), and let $`C \in {(0,\infty)}`$ be given by

``` math
{C = {4\hspace{0pt}d\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}}}.
```

Note that (128), (129), e.g., Jentzen & Kloeden \[63, Theorem 5.1\] (with $`T = T`$, $`{(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})} = {(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})}`$, $`H = {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{H} = \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \right.`$, $`U = {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{U} = \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \right.`$, $`{Q\hspace{0pt}v} = v`$, $`{(W_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}} = {(W_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$, $`{D\hspace{0pt}{(A)}} = {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`{A\hspace{0pt}v} = 0`$, $`\eta = 1`$, $`\alpha = 0`$, $`\delta = 0`$, $`{F\hspace{0pt}{(v)}} = {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(v)}}`$, $`\beta = 0`$, $`{B\hspace{0pt}{(v)}\hspace{0pt}u} = {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(v)}\hspace{0pt}u}`$, $`\gamma = 0`$, $`p = 4`$, $`\xi = {({\Omega \ni \omega\mapsto x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}})}`$ for $`{u,v,x} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ in the notation of \[63, Theorem 5.1\]) (cf., e.g., Da Prato & Zabczyk \[25, Item (i) in Theorem 7.4\] and Klenke \[67, Theorem 26.8\]), and, e.g., the Kolmogorov-Chentsov type theorem in Lemma 2.20. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") (with $`d = 1`$, $`k = d`$, $`p = 4`$, $`\alpha = {1/2}`$, $`D = {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ in the notation of Lemma 2.20. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")) assure that there exist $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic processes with continuous sample paths $`X^{x}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, which satisfy that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that

``` math
{X_{t}^{x} = {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}}.
```

Observe that (131) and Lemma 2.16 prove that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that

``` math
{{X_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{0}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{x}} + X_{t}^{y}}}.
```

This implies that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`v \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\backslash{\{ 0\}}}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
X_{t}^{v} & {= X_{t}^{{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}\frac{v}{{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} = {\left( {X_{t}^{{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}\frac{v}{{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} + {{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{0}}} \right) - {{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{0}}}} \\
 & {{= {\left( {{{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{\frac{v}{{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}}} + X_{t}^{0}} \right) - {{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{0}}} = {{{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {X_{t}^{\frac{v}{{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} - X_{t}^{0}} \right)} + X_{t}^{0}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this and (132) (with $`t = t`$, $`x = y`$, $`y = x`$, $`\lambda = {- 1}`$ for $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ in the notation of (132)) implies that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ with $`x \neq y`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{t}^{x} - X_{t}^{y}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} = \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{t}^{x - y} - X_{t}^{0}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}} \\
 & {= \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| {{{{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {X_{t}^{\frac{x - y}{{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} - X_{t}^{0}} \right)} + X_{t}^{0}} - X_{t}^{0}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}} \\
 & {{= {\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| {X_{t}^{\frac{x - y}{{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} - X_{t}^{0}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}\hspace{0pt}{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

In addition, observe that (128), (129), (131), Proposition 2.15 (with $`d = d`$, $`m = d`$, $`p = p`$, $`T = T`$, $`{\mathfrak{m}}_{1} = {\mathfrak{m}}`$, $`{\mathfrak{m}}_{2} = {\mathfrak{m}}`$, $`{\mathfrak{s}}_{1} = {\mathfrak{s}}`$, $`{\mathfrak{s}}_{2} = {\mathfrak{s}}`$ in the notation of Proposition 2.15), and the triangle inequality assure that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\sup\limits_{{v \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}},{{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} = 1}}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{t}^{v} - X_{t}^{0}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}} \\
 & {\leq {\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{t}^{0}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} + {\sup\limits_{{v \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}},{{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} = 1}}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{t}^{v}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}}} \\
 & {\leq {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}}} \\
 & {+ {\sup\limits_{{v \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}},{{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} = 1}}\left\lbrack {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\| v\|} + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {{\leq {2\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}}}.}
\end{array}
```

This, (130), (134), and the fact that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})}},{y = {(y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{d})} \in {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} & {= {{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{1/2}}} \\
 & {= {\left\lbrack {\lbrack{{|{x_{1} - y_{1}}|}^{2} + \ldots + {|{x_{d} - y_{d}}|}^{2}}\rbrack}^{1/2} \right\rbrack^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{1/2}}} \\
 & {\leq {\left\lbrack {\lbrack{d\hspace{0pt}{({2\hspace{0pt}n})}^{2}}\rbrack}^{1/2} \right\rbrack^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{1/2}}} \\
 & {= {\left\lbrack {2\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{d}} \right\rbrack^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{1/2}} \leq {2\hspace{0pt}d\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{1/2}}}
\end{array}
```

assure that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}`$ with $`x \neq y`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{t}^{x} - X_{t}^{y}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} = {\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| {X_{t}^{\frac{x - y}{{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} - X_{t}^{0}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}\hspace{0pt}{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} \\
 & {\leq {\left\lbrack {\sup\limits_{{v \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}},{{\| v\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} = 1}}\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{t}^{v} - X_{t}^{0}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} \\
 & {\leq {2\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}\hspace{0pt}{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} \\
 & {\leq {2\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}\hspace{0pt}2\hspace{0pt}d\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{1/2}}} \\
 & {{= {n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{1/2}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, note that (131) and the triangle inequality imply that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`{s,t} \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ with $`s \leq t`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{t}^{x} - X_{s}^{x}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \\
 & {= \left( {\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack \parallel x + \int_{0}^{t}\mu{(X_{u}^{x})}du + \int_{0}^{t}\sigma{(X_{u}^{x})}dW_{u} \right. \right.} \\
 & {\left. \left. - \left( x + \int_{0}^{s}\mu{(X_{u}^{x})}du + \int_{0}^{s}\sigma{(X_{u}^{x})}dW_{u}) \right. \parallel_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack \right)^{1/p}} \\
 & {= \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{{\int_{s}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{u}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{du}}} + {\int_{s}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{u}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{u}}}}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}} \\
 & {{\leq {\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{\int_{s}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{u}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{du}}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} + \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{\int_{s}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{u}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{u}}}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Furthermore, observe that Lemma 2.13. ‣ 2.3 A priori estimates for solutions of stochastic differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations"), Proposition 2.15, (128), and the fact that for all $`{s,t} \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{|{t - s}|} = {{|{t - s}|}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{|{t - s}|}^{1/2}} \leq {\sqrt{T}\hspace{0pt}{|{t - s}|}^{1/2}}`$ ensure that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}`$, $`{s,t} \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ with $`s \leq t`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| {\int_{s}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{u}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{du}}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \leq \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left| {\int_{s}^{t}{\left\| {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{u}^{x})}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}{du}}} \right|^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}} \\
 & {\leq {\int_{s}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{u}^{x})}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}\hspace{0pt}{du}}} \leq {\int_{s}^{t}{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{u}^{x}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{du}}}} \\
 & {\leq {\int_{s}^{t}{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {1 + {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\| x\|} + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{du}}}} \\
 & {= {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {1 + {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{n\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{d}} + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{({t - s})}}} \\
 & {\leq {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \sqrt{2}})}\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}d\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}\hspace{0pt}{|{t - s}|}}} \\
 & {{= {\left( \frac{1 + \sqrt{2}}{4\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{2}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}{|{t - s}|}} \leq {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}\hspace{0pt}{|{t - s}|}^{1/2}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, note that the fact that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`X^{x}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths and (129) ensure that it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\int_{0}^{T}{{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}^{2}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} \leq {T\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sup\limits_{s \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}^{2}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {{\leq {T\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sup\limits_{s \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}\left\lbrack {{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| X_{s}^{x}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}^{2}} \right\rbrack} \right\rbrack} \leq {2\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + {\sup\limits_{s \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}{\| X_{s}^{x}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{2}}} \right)} < \infty}.}
\end{array}
```

Lemma 2.14, Proposition 2.15, the fact that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`X^{x}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths, and (129) hence demonstrate that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}`$, $`{s,t} \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ with $`s \leq t`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{\int_{s}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{u}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{u}}}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \\
 & {\leq {\left( \frac{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}}{2} \right)^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{s}^{t}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{u}^{x})}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}{du}}} \right)^{1/2}}} \\
 & {\leq {p\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{s}^{t}{\left\lbrack \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{u}^{x})}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}{du}}} \right)^{1/2}}} \\
 & {\leq {p\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{s}^{t}{\left\lbrack {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{u}^{x}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}} \right)} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}{du}}} \right)^{1/2}}} \\
 & {\leq {p\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\left( {\int_{s}^{t}{1^{2}\hspace{0pt}{du}}} \right)^{1/2} + \left( {\int_{s}^{t}{\left\lbrack \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{u}^{x}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}{du}}} \right)^{1/2}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {\leq p{\mathfrak{s}}\left\lbrack |t - s|^{1/2} \right.} \\
 & \left. + \left( \int_{s}^{t}\left\lbrack \sqrt{2}\left( \parallel x \parallel + {\mathfrak{m}}T + {\mathfrak{s}}p\sqrt{T} \right)\exp\left. (\left\lbrack {\mathfrak{m}}\sqrt{T} + {\mathfrak{s}}p \right\rbrack^{2}T \right) \right\rbrack^{2}du \right)^{1/2} \right\rbrack \\
 & {\leq {p\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}{|{t - s}|}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {1 + {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{n\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{d}} + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {\leq {p\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}{|{t - s}|}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \sqrt{2}})}\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{d}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}}} \\
 & {{= {p\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}{|{t - s}|}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}\left( \frac{1 + \sqrt{2}}{4\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{2\hspace{0pt}d}} \right)} \leq {p\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}{|{t - s}|}^{1/2}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this with (138) and (139) establishes that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}`$, $`{s,t} \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{t}^{x} - X_{s}^{x}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} & {\leq {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}\hspace{0pt}{|{t - s}|}^{1/2}} + {p\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}{|{t - s}|}^{1/2}}}} \\
 & {{= {n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}{({{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {p\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{s}}}})}\hspace{0pt}{|{t - s}|}^{1/2}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, observe that the fact that for all $`{a,b} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ it holds that $`{a + b} \leq {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}{({a^{2} + b^{2}})}^{1/2}}`$ ensures that for all $`{a,b} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\sqrt{a} + \sqrt{b}} \leq {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}{({a + b})}^{1/2}} \leq {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}{({a^{2} + b^{2}})}^{1/2}} \right)^{1/2}} \leq {2\hspace{0pt}\left( {({a^{2} + b^{2}})}^{1/2} \right)^{1/2}}}.
```

This, (137), and (142) demonstrate that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d}`$, $`{s,t} \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{t}^{x} - X_{s}^{y}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \\
 & {\leq {\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{t}^{x} - X_{t}^{y}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} + \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{t}^{y} - X_{s}^{y}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}} \\
 & {\leq {{n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{1/2}} + {n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}{({{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {p\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{s}}}})}\hspace{0pt}{|{t - s}|}^{1/2}}}} \\
 & {\leq {n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {p\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{s}}}})}\hspace{0pt}{({{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{1/2} + {|{t - s}|}^{1/2}})}}} \\
 & {\leq {n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {p\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{s}}}})}\hspace{0pt}2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {({{\|{x - y}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{2} + {|{t - s}|}^{2}})}^{1/2} \right\rbrack^{1/2}}} \\
 & {{= {2\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {p\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{s}}}})}\hspace{0pt}\left\| {{(x,t)} - {(y,s)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d + 1}}^{1/2}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Hence, we obtain for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\sup\limits_{\substack{{{{(x,t)},{(y,s)}} \in {{({{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})} \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d + 1}}}, \\ {(x,t)} \neq {(y,s)}}}\left( \frac{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\|{X_{t}^{x} - X_{s}^{y}}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}}{\left\| {{(x,t)} - {(y,s)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d + 1}}^{1/2}} \right)} \\
 & {{\leq {2\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {p\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{s}}}})}} < \infty}.}
\end{array}
```

In addition, note that (128), (129), and Proposition 2.15 assure that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\sup\limits_{{(x,t)} \in {{({{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})} \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d + 1}}}\left\lbrack \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{t}^{x}\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p} \right\rbrack} \\
 & {\leq {\sup\limits_{{(x,t)} \in {{({{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})} \cap {\lbrack{- n},n\rbrack}^{d + 1}}}\left\lbrack {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\| x\|} + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {{\leq {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{n\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{d}} + {{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}T} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left. ({\left\lbrack {{{\mathfrak{m}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {{\mathfrak{s}}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}} < \infty}.}
\end{array}
```

Lemma 2.20. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") (with $`d = {d + 1}`$, $`k = d`$, $`p = p`$, $`\alpha = {1/2}`$, $`{(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})} = {(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})}`$, $`D = {{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$, $`X = {({{{({{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})} \times \Omega} \ni {({(x,t)},\omega)}\mapsto{X_{t}^{x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}})}`$ in the notation of Lemma 2.20. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")) and (145) hence prove that there exist stochastic processes $`Y^{x}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, which satisfy

1.  (I)
    that for all $`\omega \in \Omega`$ it holds that

    ``` math
    \left( {{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}} \ni {(x,t)}\mapsto{Y_{t}^{x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \right) \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}
    ```
    and
2.  (II)
    that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds that

    ``` math
    {{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({Y_{t}^{x} = X_{t}^{x}})}} = 1}.
    ```

The fact that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`X^{x}`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process and Lemma 2.19. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") therefore ensure that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`Y^{x}`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process. Next note that (147), (148), and the fact that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`X^{x}`$ has continuous sample paths assure that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
{\mathbb{P}}\left( \forall t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}:Y_{t}^{x} = X_{t}^{x} \right) = {\mathbb{P}}\left( \forall t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \cap {\mathbb{Q}}:Y_{t}^{x} = X_{t}^{x} \right) = 1.
```

Moreover, observe that (148) implies that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s.  that

``` math
{{{\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} = {{\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(Y_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{{\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}} = {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(Y_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}}.
```

Combining this, (148), and (131) ensures that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s.  that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
Y_{t}^{x} & {= X_{t}^{x} = {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}} \\
 & {{= {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(Y_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(Y_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Next let $`\Pi \subseteq \Omega`$ be the set given by

``` math
\begin{aligned}
\Pi & {= \left\{ \forall x,y \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d},\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}},t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}:Y_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y} + \lambda Y_{t}^{0} = \lambda Y_{t}^{x} + Y_{t}^{y} \right\}.}
\end{aligned}
```

Combining (124), (125), (147) (151), and (152) with Proposition 2.18 demonstrates that

``` math
{{\Pi \in {\mathcal{F}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{(\Pi)}} = 1}}.
```

This proves that there exist unique stochastic processes $`Z^{x}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, which satisfy for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`\omega \in \Omega`$ that

$`Z_{t}^{x}{(\omega)} = \left\{ \begin{array}{cl}
 & {Y_{t}^{x}{(\omega)}:\mspace{23mu}\omega \in \Pi} \\
 & {{{0\mspace{51mu}:\mspace{23mu}\omega} \in {\Omega\backslash\Pi}}.}
\end{array} \right.`$

Observe that (152) and (154) imply that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`\omega \in \Pi`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{Z_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}Z_{t}^{0}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}} & {= {{Y_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}Y_{t}^{0}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}}} \\
 & {{= {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}Y_{t}^{x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} + {Y_{t}^{y}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}Z_{t}^{x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} + {Z_{t}^{y}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, note that (154) assures that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`\omega \in {\Omega\backslash\Pi}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{{Z_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}Z_{t}^{0}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}} = {0 + 0} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}Z_{t}^{x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} + {Z_{t}^{y}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this with (155) demonstrates that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`\omega \in \Omega`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{{Z_{t}^{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}Z_{t}^{0}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}Z_{t}^{x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} + {Z_{t}^{y}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Furthermore, observe that (154) ensures for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{\Pi \subseteq {\{{{\forall t} \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}:{Z_{t}^{x} = Y_{t}^{x}}\}}}.
```

This and (153) show for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{\mathbb{P}}\left( \forall t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}:Z_{t}^{x} = Y_{t}^{x} \right) = 1.
```

The fact that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`Y^{x}`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths, Lemma 2.19. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations"), and (154) therefore imply that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`Z^{x}`$ is an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths. Combining this and (151) with (159) demonstrates that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s.  that

``` math
{Z_{t}^{x} = {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(Z_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(Z_{s}^{x})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}}.
```

This, (128), (129), and, e.g., Jentzen & Kloeden \[63, Theorem 5.1\] (with $`T = T`$, $`{(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})} = {(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})}`$, $`H = {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{H} = \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \right.`$, $`U = {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{U} = \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \right.`$, $`{Q\hspace{0pt}v} = v`$, $`{(W_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}} = {(W_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$, $`{D\hspace{0pt}{(A)}} = {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`{A\hspace{0pt}v} = 0`$, $`\eta = 1`$, $`\alpha = 0`$, $`\delta = 0`$, $`{F\hspace{0pt}{(v)}} = {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(v)}}`$, $`\beta = 0`$, $`{B\hspace{0pt}{(v)}\hspace{0pt}u} = {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(v)}\hspace{0pt}u}`$, $`\gamma = 0`$, $`p = 2`$, $`\xi = {({\Omega \ni \omega\mapsto x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}})}`$ for $`{u,v,x} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ in the notation of \[63, Theorem 5.1\]) (cf., e.g., Da Prato & Zabczyk \[25, Item (i) in Theorem 7.4\] and Klenke \[67, Theorem 26.8\]) establish that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ and all $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic processes with continuous sample paths $`V:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ which satisfy that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that

``` math
V_{t} = {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(V_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(V_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}
```

it holds that $`{{\forall t} \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}:{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({V_{t} = Z_{t}^{x}})}} = 1}`$. Lemma 2.17. ‣ 2.4 Stochastic differential equations with affine coefficient functions ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") hence demonstrates that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ and all $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic processes with continuous sample paths $`V:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ which satisfy that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that

``` math
V_{t} = {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(V_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(V_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}
```

it holds that $`{\mathbb{P}}{(\forall t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}:V_{t} = Z_{t}^{x})} = 1`$. Combining this with (157) and (160) completes the proof of Proposition 2.21. ∎

### 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations

s

In this subsection we apply results on viscosity solutions for PDEs from the literature (cf., e.g., Crandall et al. \[21\], Crandall & Lions \[22\], and Hairer et al. \[47, Subsections 4.3–4.4\]) to establish in Proposition 2.23. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") and Corollary 2.24 the existence, uniqueness, and regularity results for viscosity solutions which we need for our proofs of the ANN approximation results. We recall that the notion of viscosity solutions allows to extend the classical notion of solutions of second order PDEs to functions which are merely continuous and not necessarily $`C^{2}`$. Our proof of Proposition 2.23. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") employs the following well-known result, Lemma 2.22 below, on the existence of a suitable Lyapunov-type function $`V:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ where $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ under the coercivity-type hypothesis in (163). For the sake of completeness we include in this subsection also a proof of Lemma 2.22.

###### Lemma 2.22.

Let $`{d,m} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`p \in {\lbrack 4,\infty)}`$, let $`{\langle \cdot , \cdot \rangle}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean scalar product, let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| :{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}`$, let $`\mu:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}}`$ be functions which satisfy that

``` math
{{\left\lbrack {\sup\limits_{x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\langle x,{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|^{2}})}} \right\rbrack + \left\lbrack {\sup\limits_{x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}} \right\rbrack} < \infty},
```

and let $`V:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the function which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{V\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}}`$. Then

1.  (i)
    it holds that $`V \in {C^{2}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{(0,\infty)})}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    there exists $`\rho \in {(0,\infty)}`$ such that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

    ``` math
    {{\left\langle {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}},{{({\nabla V})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\rangle + {{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}V})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right)}} \leq {\rho\hspace{0pt}V\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}}.
    ```

###### Proof of Lemma 2.22.

Throughout this proof let $`\mu_{i}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}`$, and $`\sigma_{i,j}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}`$, $`j \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,m\}}`$, satisfy for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {({\mu_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}_{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}`$ and $`{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {({\sigma_{i,j}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}_{{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}},{j \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,m\}}}}`$ and let $`c \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ satisfy for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{{\langle x,{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle} \leq {{c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|^{2}})}}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|} \leq {c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}}}}.
```

Note that the fact that for all $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}`$ it holds that $`{V\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {1 + \left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{d}{|x_{i}|}^{2}} \right\rbrack^{p/2}}`$ assures that for all $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\left( {\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\hspace{0pt}V} \right)\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\frac{p}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{d}{|x_{i}|}^{2}} \right\rbrack^{{p/2} - 1}\hspace{0pt}2\hspace{0pt}x_{i}} = {p\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}\hspace{0pt}x_{i}}}.
```

This ensures that for all $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`{i,j} \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\left( {\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial{x_{j}\hspace{0pt}{\partial x_{i}}}}\hspace{0pt}V} \right)\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = \begin{cases}
{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 2})}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 4}\hspace{0pt}x_{i}\hspace{0pt}x_{j}} & {:{i \neq j}} \\
{{p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 2})}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 4}\hspace{0pt}{|x_{i}|}^{2}} + {p\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}}} & {:{i = j}}
\end{cases}}.
```

Combining this and (166) proves item (i). Next observe that (166) and (167) demonstrate that for all $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\left\langle {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}},{{({\nabla V})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\rangle + {{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}V})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right)}} \\
 & {= {\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{d}{\mu_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\hspace{0pt}V} \right)\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right\rbrack + \left\lbrack {\sum_{{i,j} = 1}^{d}{\sum_{k = 1}^{m}{\sigma_{i,k}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{j,k}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial{x_{i}\hspace{0pt}{\partial x_{j}}}}\hspace{0pt}V} \right)\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {= {\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{d}{\mu_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}\hspace{0pt}x_{i}}} \right\rbrack + \left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{d}{\sum_{k = 1}^{m}{\sigma_{i,k}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{i,k}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}}}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {+ \left\lbrack {\sum_{{i,j} = 1}^{d}{\sum_{k = 1}^{m}{\sigma_{i,k}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{j,k}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 2})}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 4}\hspace{0pt}x_{i}\hspace{0pt}x_{j}}}} \right\rbrack} \\
 & {= {{p\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}\hspace{0pt}{\langle x,{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}} + {p\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{d}{\sum_{k = 1}^{m}{|{\sigma_{i\hspace{0pt}k}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}|}^{2}}} \right\rbrack}}} \\
 & {+ {p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 2})}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 4}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{{i,j} = 1}^{d}{\sum_{k = 1}^{m}{\sigma_{i,k}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{j,k}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}x_{i}\hspace{0pt}x_{j}}}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {= {{p\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}\hspace{0pt}{\langle x,{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}} + {p\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}\hspace{0pt}{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}^{2}}}} \\
 & {+ {p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 2})}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 4}\hspace{0pt}{({x^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \\
 & {\leq {{p\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}\hspace{0pt}{\langle x,{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}} + {p\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}\hspace{0pt}{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}^{2}} + {p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 2})}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}\hspace{0pt}{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}^{2}}}} \\
 & {{= {{p\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}\hspace{0pt}{\langle x,{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}} + {p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}\hspace{0pt}{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}^{2}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

This and (165) ensure that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\left\langle {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}},{{({\nabla V})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\rangle + {{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}V})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right)}} \\
 & {\leq {{p\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}\hspace{0pt}c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|^{2}})}} + {p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}\hspace{0pt}c^{2}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}^{2}}}} \\
 & {= {{{({{p\hspace{0pt}c} + {p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}c^{2}}})}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 2}} + {2\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}c^{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p - 1}} + {{({{p\hspace{0pt}c} + {p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}c^{2}}})}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|^{p}}}} \\
 & {\leq {{{({{p\hspace{0pt}c} + {p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}c^{2}}})}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}})}} + {2\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}c^{2}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}})}}}} \\
 & {+ {{({{p\hspace{0pt}c} + {p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}c^{2}}})}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}})}}} \\
 & {{= {{({{2\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}c} + {4\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}c^{2}}})}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}})}} = {{({{2\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}c} + {4\hspace{0pt}p\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}\hspace{0pt}c^{2}}})}\hspace{0pt}V\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}}.}
\end{array}
```

This establishes item (ii). The proof of Lemma 2.22 is thus completed. ∎

###### Proposition 2.23 (Existence and uniqueness of viscosity solutions).

Let $`{d,m} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`c \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, let $`{\langle \cdot , \cdot \rangle}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean scalar product, let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| :{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}`$, let $`\varphi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a continuous and at most polynomially growing function, and let $`\mu:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{{\langle x,{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle} \leq {c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|^{2}})}}},{{\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|} \leq {c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}}}},
```
``` math
{{\text{and}\qquad{\left\| {{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right\| + {\|\left| {{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right|\|}}} \leq {c\hspace{0pt}\left\| {x - y} \right\|}}.
```

Then

1.  (i)
    there exists a continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies for all $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$ that $`{{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty},`$ and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,\infty)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{aligned}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
    \end{aligned}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,\infty)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$,
2.  (ii)
    for all $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$ it holds that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{aligned}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
    \end{aligned}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$,
3.  (iii)
    for all $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$ and all continuous functions $`v:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfy for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{v\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfy that $`{{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty},`$ and which satisfy that $`{v|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{aligned}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
    \end{aligned}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ it holds that $`v = {u|}_{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, and
4.  (iv)
    for every $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, every filtered probability space $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})`$ which fulfils the usual conditions, every standard $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-Brownian motion $`W:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}`$, and every $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths $`X:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ which satisfies that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that

    ``` math
    X_{t} = {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}
    ```
    it holds that

    ``` math
    {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} = {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T})}} \right\rbrack}}.
    ```

###### Proof of Proposition 2.23. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations").

Throughout this proof let $`C_{n} \subseteq {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be the sets which satisfy for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`C_{n} = {\{{x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:{\left\| x \right\| > n}\}}`$ and for every $`p \in {(0,\infty)}`$ let $`V_{p}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{(0,\infty)}}`$ be the function which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{V_{p}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}}`$. Note that the hypothesis that $`\varphi`$ is a continuous and at most polynomially growing function, (170. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")), (171. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")), and Hairer et al. \[47, Corollary 4.17\] demonstrate that there exists a continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies for all $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$ that

``` math
{{\inf\limits_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty},
```

and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,\infty)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{aligned}
{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
\end{aligned}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,\infty)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$. This establishes items (i. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations"))–(ii. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")). For the next step let $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$ and let $`v:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a continuous function which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{v\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies that

``` math
{{\inf\limits_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty},
```

and which satisfies that $`{v|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

