``` math
\begin{aligned}
{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
\end{aligned}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$. Observe that (177) and (179) show that

``` math
{{{\inf\limits_{q \in {\lbrack 3,\infty)}}{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < {\infty\quad\text{and}}}\mspace{21mu}{{\inf\limits_{q \in {\lbrack 3,\infty)}}{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty}}.
```

Therefore, we obtain that there exists $`p \in {\lbrack 3,\infty)}`$ such that

``` math
{{{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}}} < {\infty\qquad\text{and}}}\mspace{39mu}{{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}}} < \infty}}.
```

Next note that the fact that there exists $`r_{0} > 0`$ such that the function $`\left( {{\lbrack r_{0},\infty)} \ni r\mapsto\frac{1 + r^{p}}{1 + r^{p + 1}}} \right)`$ is monotonically decreasing ensures that there exists $`n_{0} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ such that for all $`n \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack n_{0},\infty)}}`$, $`x \in C_{n}`$ it holds that

$`{\frac{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p + 1}} \leq \frac{1 + n^{p}}{1 + n^{p + 1}}}.`$

Therefore, we obtain that for all $`w \in {\{ u,v\}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times C_{n}}}\frac{|{w\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{V_{p + 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}}} \\
 & {= {\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times C_{n}}}\left( {\frac{|{w\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}}\hspace{0pt}\frac{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p + 1}}} \right)}}} \\
 & {\leq {\left\lbrack {\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{w\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times C_{n}}}\frac{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p + 1}}}}}} \\
 & {{\leq {\left\lbrack {\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{w\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{p}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1 + n^{p}}{1 + n^{p + 1}}}} = 0}.}
\end{array}
```

In addition, note that (170. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")), the fact that $`{p + 1} \in {\lbrack 4,\infty)}`$, and Lemma 2.22 (with $`d = d`$, $`m = m`$, $`p = {p + 1}`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = {2^{- {1/2}}\hspace{0pt}\sigma}`$, $`V = V_{p + 1}`$ in the notation of Lemma 2.22) prove that there exists $`\rho \in {(0,\infty)}`$ such that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`V_{p + 1} \in {C^{2}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{(0,\infty)})}}`$ and

``` math
{{\left\langle {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}},{{({\nabla V_{p + 1}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\rangle + {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}V_{p + 1}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right)}}} \leq {\rho\hspace{0pt}V_{p + 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}}.
```

Combining this, (171. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")), item (ii. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")), the fact that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {v\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}}`$, (180), and (183) with Hairer et al. \[47, Corollary 4.14\] (with $`T = T`$, $`d = d`$, $`m = m`$, $`\rho = \rho`$, $`O = {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\varphi = \varphi`$, $`v = 0`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = {2^{- {1/2}}\hspace{0pt}\sigma}`$, $`V = V_{p + 1}`$ in the notation of Hairer et al. \[47, Corollary 4.14\]) establishes that $`v = {u|}_{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$. This proves item (iii. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")). It thus remains to prove item (iv. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")). For this let $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})`$ be a filtered probability space which fulfils the usual conditions, let $`W:{{{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}`$ be a standard $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}}`$-Brownian motion, and let $`X:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ be an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths which satisfies that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that

``` math
{X_{t} = {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}}.
```

Observe that (171. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")) and Klenke \[67, Theorem 26.8\] assure that there exists an $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}}`$-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths $`Y:{{{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ which satisfies that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that

``` math
{Y_{t} = {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(Y_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(Y_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}}.
```

Moreover, note that, e.g., Jentzen & Kloeden \[63, Theorem 5.1\] (with $`T = T`$, $`{(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})} = {(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})}`$, $`H = {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`U = {\mathbb{R}}^{m}`$, $`{Q\hspace{0pt}u} = u`$, $`{(W_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}} = {(W_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$, $`{D\hspace{0pt}{(A)}} = {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`{A\hspace{0pt}v} = 0`$, $`\eta = 1`$, $`\alpha = 0`$, $`\delta = 0`$, $`{F\hspace{0pt}{(v)}} = {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(v)}}`$, $`\beta = 0`$, $`{B\hspace{0pt}{(v)}\hspace{0pt}u} = {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(v)}\hspace{0pt}u}`$, $`\gamma = 0`$, $`p = 2`$, $`\xi = {({\Omega \ni \omega\mapsto x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}})}`$ for $`{v,x} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`u \in {\mathbb{R}}^{m}`$ in the notation of \[63, Theorem 5.1\]) implies that for all $`s \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({Y_{s} = X_{s}})}} = 1`$. This, item (i. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")), and the Feynman-Kac formula in Hairer et al. \[47, Corollary 4.17\] demonstrate that

``` math
{{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} = {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(Y_{T})}} \right\rbrack} = {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T})}} \right\rbrack}}.
```

This implies item (iv. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")). The proof of Proposition 2.23. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") is thus completed. ∎

###### Corollary 2.24.

Let $`{d,m} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`\varphi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a continuous and at most polynomially growing function, and let $`\mu:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{\mu\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}} \\
{{{\text{and}\qquad{{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Then

1.  (i)
    there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty`$, which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{aligned}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
    \end{aligned}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    for every $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, every filtered probability space $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})`$ which fulfils the usual conditions, every standard $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-Brownian motion $`W:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}`$, and every $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths $`X:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ which satisfies that for all $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that $`X_{t} = {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}}}}}`$ it holds that

    ``` math
    {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} = {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T})}} \right\rbrack}}.
    ```

###### Proof of Corollary 2.24.

Throughout this proof let $`{\langle \cdot , \cdot \rangle}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean scalar product and let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| :{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times m}`$. Note that (188), Corollary 2.10, and Corollary 2.11 assure that there exists $`\kappa \in {(0,\infty)}`$ such that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
{\left\| {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\| + {\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}} \leq {\kappa\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}}
```

and

``` math
{{\left\| {{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right\| + {\|\left| {{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} \right|\|}} \leq {\kappa\hspace{0pt}\left\| {x - y} \right\|}}.
```

This ensures that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\langle x,{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle} & {\leq {\left\| x \right\|\hspace{0pt}\left\| {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|} \leq {\left\| x \right\|\hspace{0pt}\kappa\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}} = {\kappa\hspace{0pt}{({\left\| x \right\| + \left\| x \right\|^{2}})}}} \\
 & {{\leq {\kappa\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|^{2} + \left\| x \right\|^{2}})}} \leq {2\hspace{0pt}\kappa\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|^{2}})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this, the hypothesis that $`\varphi`$ is a continuous function, and (191)–(192) with items (i. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations"))–(iii. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")) in Proposition 2.23. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") (with $`d = d`$, $`m = m`$, $`c = {2\hspace{0pt}\kappa}`$, $`\varphi = \varphi`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = \sigma`$ in the notation of Proposition 2.23. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")) proves item (i). Moreover, note that item (iv. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations")) in Proposition 2.23. ‣ 2.5 Viscosity solutions for partial differential equations ‣ 2 Probabilistic and analytic preliminaries ‣ A proof that artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes partial differential equations") and item (i) establish item (ii). The proof of Corollary 2.24 is thus completed. ∎

## 3 Artificial neural network approximations

### 3.1 Construction of a realization on the artificial probability space

In Theorem 3.14 in Subsection 3.6 below we establish that the number of required parameters of an ANN to approximate the solution of the Black-Scholes PDE grows at most polynomially in both the reciprocal of the prescribed approximation accuracy $`\varepsilon > 0`$ and the PDE dimension $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$. An important ingredient in our proof of Theorem 3.14 is an artificial probability space on which we establish the existence of a suitable realization with the desired approximation properties. In this subsection we provide, roughly speaking, in the elementary result in Proposition 3.3 below on a very abstract level the argument for the existence of such a realization on the artificial probability space. Proposition 3.3 is an immediate consequence from the elementary result in Corollary 3.2 below. Corollary 3.2, in turn, follows from the following elementary lemma, Lemma 3.1.

###### Lemma 3.1.

Let $`\varepsilon \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})`$ be a probability space, and let $`X:{\Omega\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a random variable which satisfies that $`{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X > \varepsilon})}} = 1}.`$ Then

1.  (i)
    it holds that $`{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\max{\{{- X},0\}}} \right\rbrack} < \infty`$ and
2.  (ii)
    it holds that $`{{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\lbrack X\rbrack} > \varepsilon}.`$

###### Proof of Lemma 3.1.

Observe that the hypothesis that $`{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X > \varepsilon})}} = 1`$ establishes item (i). Next note that the hypothesis that $`{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X > \varepsilon})}} = 1`$ implies that

``` math
{0 < 1 = {{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X > \varepsilon})}} = {{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\cup_{n \in {\mathbb{N}}}\left\{ {X \geq {\varepsilon + \frac{1}{n}}} \right\}} \right)} = {\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X \geq {\varepsilon + \frac{1}{n}}})}}}}.
```

Hence, we obtain that there exists $`\delta \in {(0,\infty)}`$ such that $`{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X \geq {\varepsilon + \delta}})}} > 0`$. The hypothesis that $`{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X > \varepsilon})}} = 1`$ therefore ensures that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\lbrack X\rbrack} & {\geq {{{({\varepsilon + \delta})}\hspace{0pt}{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X \geq {\varepsilon + \delta}})}} + {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X \in {(\varepsilon,{\varepsilon + \delta})}})}}}} \\
 & {{= {{\delta\hspace{0pt}{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X \geq {\varepsilon + \delta}})}} + {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X > \varepsilon})}}} = {{\delta\hspace{0pt}{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({X \geq {\varepsilon + \delta}})}} + \varepsilon} > \varepsilon}.}
\end{array}
```

This establishes item (ii). The proof of Lemma 3.1 is thus completed. ∎

###### Corollary 3.2.

Let $`\varepsilon \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})`$ be a probability space, and let $`X:{\Omega\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a random variable which satisfies that $`{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({{|X|} > \varepsilon})}} = 1}.`$ Then

``` math
{{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {|X|} \right\rbrack} > \varepsilon}.
```

###### Proof of Corollary 3.2.

Note that item (ii) in Lemma 3.1 (with $`\varepsilon = \varepsilon`$, $`X = {|X|}`$ in the notation of Lemma 2.12) ensures that $`{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {|X|} \right\rbrack} > \varepsilon`$. The proof of Corollary 3.2 is thus completed. ∎

###### Proposition 3.3.

Let $`\varepsilon \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})`$ be a probability space, and let $`X:{\Omega\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a random variable which satisfies that

``` math
{{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {|X|} \right\rbrack} \leq \varepsilon}.
```

Then $`{{{\mathbb{P}}\hspace{0pt}{({{|X|} \leq \varepsilon})}} > 0}.`$

###### Proof of Proposition 3.3.

Combining Corollary 3.2 and (197) completes the proof of Proposition 3.3. ∎

### 3.2 Approximation error estimates

###### Proposition 3.4.

Let $`{d,n} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`p \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{c,\varepsilon,L} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, $`{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}} \in {\lbrack{1/p},\infty)}`$, let $`{\langle \cdot , \cdot \rangle}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean scalar product, let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| :{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, let $`\nu:{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$ be a probability measure, let $`\varphi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a continuous function, let $`\phi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a $`{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}/\mathcal{B}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}})}`$-measurable function which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{\left| {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right| \leq {{c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{\left| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| \leq {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{w}}})}}}},
```

and let $`\mu:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mu\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```
``` math
{{{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

and $`{{\left\| {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\| + {\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}} \leq {L\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}}}.`$ Then

1.  (i)
    there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies that $`{{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty},`$ which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{aligned}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
    \end{aligned}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    there exist $`{A_{1},A_{2},\ldots,A_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, $`{b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ such that

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{cl}
     & \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \\
     & {\leq \varepsilon\left( 1 + 2^{\mathbf{w}/2}\exp\left( \left\lbrack \sqrt{T} + \max{\{ 2,\mathbf{w}\}} \right\rbrack^{2}L^{2}T\mathbf{w} \right) \right.} \\
     & \left. \cdot \left\lbrack L\left( T + \max{\{ 2,\mathbf{w}\}}\sqrt{T} \right) + \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \parallel x \parallel^{\mathbf{w}\hspace{0pt}p}\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{w}\hspace{0pt}p})}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{w}} \right) \\
     & {+ n^{- {1/2}}4c{(p - 1)}^{1/2}\left( 1 + 2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\exp\left( \left\lbrack \sqrt{T} + \max{\{ 2,\mathbf{v}p\}} \right\rbrack^{2}L^{2}T\mathbf{v} \right) \right.} \\
     & {\left. \cdot \left\lbrack L\left( T + \max{\{ 2,\mathbf{v}p\}}\sqrt{T} \right) + \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \parallel x \parallel^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}} \right).}
    \end{array}
    ```

###### Proof of Proposition 3.4.

Throughout this proof let $`e_{j} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`j \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}`$, be given by $`{e_{1} = {(1,0,\ldots,0)}},{{e_{2} = {{(0,1,0,\ldots,0)},\ldots}},{e_{d} = {(0,\ldots,0,1)}}}`$, let $`m:{{(0,\infty)}\rightarrow{\lbrack 2,\infty)}}`$ be the function which satisfies for all $`z \in {(0,\infty)}`$ that $`{m\hspace{0pt}{(z)}} = {\max{\{ 2,z\}}}`$, let $`(\Omega,\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}},{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}})`$ be a filtered probability space which fulfils the usual conditions, let $`W^{i}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be independent standard $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-Brownian motions, let $`X^{i,x}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, be $`{({\mathbb{F}}_{t})}_{t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}}`$-adapted stochastic processes with continuous sample paths which satisfy that for all $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$ it holds $`\mathbb{P}`$-a.s. that

``` math
X_{t}^{i,x} = {x + {\int_{0}^{t}{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{i,x})}\hspace{0pt}{ds}}} + {\int_{0}^{t}{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(X_{s}^{i,x})}\hspace{0pt}{dW_{s}^{i}}}}}
```

and that for all $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`\omega \in \Omega`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{X_{t}^{i,{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y}}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{i,0}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}X_{t}^{i,x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} + {X_{t}^{i,y}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}}
```

(cf. Proposition 2.21), and let $`\mathcal{A}_{i}:{\Omega\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$, $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, and $`\mathcal{B}_{i}:{\Omega\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be the random variables which satisfy that for all $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\omega \in \Omega`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{B}_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} = {X_{T}^{i,0}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}`$ and

``` math
\begin{matrix}
{\mathcal{A}_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \\
{{= \left( {{X_{T}^{i,e_{1}}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} - \left. {X_{T}^{i,0}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}\hspace{0pt}\left| {{X_{T}^{i,e_{2}}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} - {X_{T}^{i,0}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}} \right|\hspace{0pt}\cdots} \middle| {{X_{T}^{i,e_{d}}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} - {X_{T}^{i,0}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}} \right.} \right)}.}
\end{matrix}
```

Observe that (198) assures for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{|{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}|} & {\leq {\left| {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right| + \left| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|} \leq {{c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}} + {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{w}}})}}}} \\
 & {\leq {{c\hspace{0pt}{({2 + {\| x\|}^{\max{\{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}\}}}})}} + {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{({2 + {\| x\|}^{\max{\{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}\}}}})}}}} \\
 & {{\leq {2\hspace{0pt}{({c + \varepsilon})}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\max{\{\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}\}}}})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Therefore, we obtain that $`\varphi`$ is an at most polynomially growing function. This, (199), (200), and item (i) in Corollary 2.24 (with $`d = d`$, $`m = d`$, $`T = T`$, $`\varphi = \varphi`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = \sigma`$ in the notation of Corollary 2.24) demonstrate that there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{aligned}
{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
\end{aligned}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$. This proves item (i). Next note that item (ii) in Corollary 2.24, item (i), and (203) establish that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} = {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack}}.
```

Moreover, observe that (204), (205), and Lemma 2.8 (with $`d = d`$, $`m = d`$, $`e_{j} = e_{j}`$, $`{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {X_{T}^{i,x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}`$ for $`j \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}`$, $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\omega \in \Omega`$ in the notation of Lemma 2.8) demonstrate that for all $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\omega \in \Omega`$ it holds that

``` math
{{X_{T}^{i,x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} = {{\mathcal{A}_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}\hspace{0pt}x} + {\mathcal{B}_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}}}.
```

This ensures that for all $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\omega \in \Omega`$ it holds that the function

``` math
{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \ni x\mapsto{X_{T}^{i,x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}
```

is continuous. Combining this and the fact that for all $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`X_{T}^{i,x}:{\Omega\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is $`{\mathcal{F}/\mathcal{B}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}`$-measurable with Beck et al. \[5, Lemma 2.4\] (with $`E = {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\mathcal{E} = {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\Omega = \Omega`$, $`{X\hspace{0pt}{(x,\omega)}} = {X_{T}^{i,x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}}`$ for $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\omega \in \Omega`$ in the notation of \[5, Lemma 2.4\]) establishes that for all $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that the function

``` math
{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times \Omega} \ni {(x,\omega)}\mapsto{X_{T}^{i,x}\hspace{0pt}{(\omega)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}
```

is $`{{({{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}} \otimes \mathcal{F}})}/\mathcal{B}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}`$-measurable. This, (198), and the triangle inequality assure that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack} - {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \\
 & {= \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} - {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}}} \right\rbrack} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \\
 & {\leq \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} - {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}}} \right| \right\rbrack} \right)^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \\
 & {\leq \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\| X_{T}^{1,x} \right\|^{\mathbf{w}}} \right)} \right\rbrack} \right)^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \\
 & {= {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left( {1 + {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| X_{T}^{1,x} \right\|^{\mathbf{w}} \right\rbrack}} \right)^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}}} \\
 & {{\leq {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| X_{T}^{1,x} \right\|^{\mathbf{w}} \right\rbrack} \right)^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)}}.}
\end{array}
```

Furthermore, observe that Jensen’s inequality, the hypothesis that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{\left\| {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\| + {\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}} \leq {L\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}}`$, (203), and Proposition 2.15 (with $`d = d`$, $`m = d`$, $`p = {m\hspace{0pt}{(z)}}`$, $`{\mathfrak{m}}_{1} = {\mathfrak{m}}_{2} = {\mathfrak{s}}_{1} = {\mathfrak{s}}_{2} = L`$, $`T = T`$, $`\xi = x`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = \sigma`$ for $`z \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ in the notation of Proposition 2.15) prove that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`z \in {(0,\infty)}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| X_{T}^{1,x} \right\|^{z} \right\rbrack} = {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\lbrack \left\| X_{T}^{1,x} \right\|^{m\hspace{0pt}{(z)}} \right\rbrack^{z/{m\hspace{0pt}{(z)}}} \right\rbrack} \leq \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| X_{T}^{1,x} \right\|^{m\hspace{0pt}{(z)}} \right\rbrack} \right)^{z/{m\hspace{0pt}{(z)}}}} \\
 & {\leq \left\lbrack {\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\| x\|} + {L\hspace{0pt}T} + {L\hspace{0pt}m\hspace{0pt}{(z)}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\left\lbrack {{L\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}} + {L\hspace{0pt}m\hspace{0pt}{(z)}}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}T} \right)}} \right\rbrack^{z}} \\
 & {{= {2^{z/2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\| x\|} + {L\hspace{0pt}\left( {T + {m\hspace{0pt}{(z)}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)}} \right\rbrack^{z}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\left\lbrack {\sqrt{T} + {m\hspace{0pt}{(z)}}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}L^{2}\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}z} \right)}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The fact that $`{\mathbf{w}\hspace{0pt}p} \geq 1`$ and the triangle inequality hence prove that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| X_{T}^{1,x} \right\|^{\mathbf{w}} \right\rbrack} \right)^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \\
 & {\leq \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left\lbrack 2^{\mathbf{w}/2}\left\lbrack \parallel x \parallel + L\left( T + m{(\mathbf{w})}\sqrt{T} \right) \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{w}} \right. \right.} \\
 & {\left. \left. \cdot \exp\left( \left\lbrack \sqrt{T} + m{(\mathbf{w})} \right\rbrack^{2}L^{2}T\mathbf{w} \right) \right\rbrack^{p}\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \\
 & {= {2^{\mathbf{w}/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\left\lbrack {\sqrt{T} + {m\hspace{0pt}{(\mathbf{w})}}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}L^{2}\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{w}} \right)}}} \\
 & {\cdot \left\lbrack \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left\lbrack {{\| x\|} + {L\hspace{0pt}\left( {T + {m\hspace{0pt}{(\mathbf{w})}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{w}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{w}\hspace{0pt}p})}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{w}}} \\
 & {\leq {2^{\mathbf{w}/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\left\lbrack {\sqrt{T} + {m\hspace{0pt}{(\mathbf{w})}}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}L^{2}\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{w}} \right)}}} \\
 & {{\cdot \left\lbrack {\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{w}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{w}\hspace{0pt}p})}} + {L\hspace{0pt}\left( {T + {m\hspace{0pt}{(\mathbf{w})}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{w}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this and (212) demonstrates that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack} - {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \\
 & {\leq \varepsilon\left( 1 + 2^{\mathbf{w}/2}\exp\left( \left\lbrack \sqrt{T} + m{(\mathbf{w})} \right\rbrack^{2}L^{2}T\mathbf{w} \right) \right.} \\
 & {\left. \cdot \left\lbrack \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \parallel x \parallel^{\mathbf{w}\hspace{0pt}p}\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{w}\hspace{0pt}p})}} + L\left( T + m{(\mathbf{w})}\sqrt{T} \right) \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{w}} \right).}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, observe that the fact that $`W^{i}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times \Omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, are independent Brownian motions ensures for every $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`X_{T}^{i,x}:{\Omega\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`i \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, are i.i.d. random variables (cf., e.g., Beck et al. \[5, Theorem 2.8\] and Klenke \[67, Theorem 15.8\]). Combining this, (198), (213), and the hypothesis that $`\phi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ is a $`{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}/\mathcal{B}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}})}`$-measurable function proves for every $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{i,x})}}:{\Omega\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,n\}}`$, are i.i.d. random variables which satisfy for every $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {|{\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}}|} \right\rbrack} \leq {c\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\| X_{T}^{1,x} \right\|^{\mathbf{v}} \right\rbrack}} \right)} < \infty}.
```

This, Hölder’s inequality, Fubini’s theorem, and Corollary 2.6 (with $`p = p`$, $`d = 1`$, $`n = n`$, $`X_{i} = {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{i,x})}}`$ for $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,n\}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ in the notation of Corollary 2.6) demonstrate that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{i,x})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \right\rbrack} \\
 & {\leq \left( {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{i,x})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack} \right)^{1/p}} \\
 & {= \left( {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left| {{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{i,x})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right)^{1/p}} \\
 & {\leq \left( {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}} \right\rbrack^{p}\hspace{0pt}{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left| {{\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} - {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right)^{1/p}} \\
 & {{= {\frac{2\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left| {{\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} - {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right)^{1/p}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, observe that Hölder’s inequality demonstrates that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left| {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack} \right|^{p} \right\rbrack} = \left| {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack} \right|^{p} \leq {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left| {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right|^{p} \right\rbrack}}.
```

The triangle inequality, Hölder’s inequality, (198), and (217) hence imply that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{i,x})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \right\rbrack} \\
 & {\leq \frac{2\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}}\left\lbrack \left( \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack \middle| \phi{(X_{T}^{1,x})} \middle| {}_{p} \right\rbrack\nu{(dx)} \right)^{1/p} \right.} \\
 & \left. + \left( \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack \middle| {\mathbb{E}}\left\lbrack \phi{(X_{T}^{1,x})} \right\rbrack \middle| {}_{p} \right\rbrack\nu{(dx)} \right)^{1/p} \right\rbrack \\
 & {\leq {\frac{4\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left| {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right|^{p} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right)^{1/p}}} \\
 & {\leq {\frac{4\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left| {c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| X_{T}^{1,x}\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}} \right|^{p} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right)^{1/p}}} \\
 & {= {\frac{4\hspace{0pt}c\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left( {1 + {\| X_{T}^{1,x}\|}^{\mathbf{v}}} \right)^{p} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right)^{1/p}}} \\
 & {{\leq {\frac{4\hspace{0pt}c\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\| X_{T}^{1,x}\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)}}.}
\end{array}
```

This, (213), the triangle inequality, and the fact that $`{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p} \geq 1`$ assure that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{i,x})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \right\rbrack} \\
 & {\leq \frac{4\hspace{0pt}c\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}}\left( 1 + \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}2^{{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}/2}\left\lbrack \parallel x \parallel + L\left( T + m{(\mathbf{v}p)}\sqrt{T} \right) \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p} \right. \right.} \\
 & \left. \left. \cdot \exp\left( \left\lbrack \sqrt{T} + m{(\mathbf{v}p)} \right\rbrack^{2}L^{2}T\mathbf{v}p \right)\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \right) \\
 & {= \frac{4\hspace{0pt}c\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}}\left( 1 + 2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\exp\left( \left\lbrack \sqrt{T} + m{(\mathbf{v}p)} \right\rbrack^{2}L^{2}T\mathbf{v} \right) \right.} \\
 & \left. \cdot \left\lbrack \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left\lbrack \parallel x \parallel + L\left( T + m{(\mathbf{v}p)}\sqrt{T} \right) \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}} \right) \\
 & {\leq \frac{4\hspace{0pt}c\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}}\left( 1 + 2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\exp\left( \left\lbrack \sqrt{T} + m{(\mathbf{v}p)} \right\rbrack^{2}L^{2}T\mathbf{v} \right) \right.} \\
 & {\left. \cdot \left\lbrack \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \parallel x \parallel^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}} + L\left( T + m{(\mathbf{v}p)}\sqrt{T} \right) \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}} \right).}
\end{array}
```

Proposition 3.3 hence demonstrates that there exists $`{\mathbf{ω}} \in \Omega`$ such that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{({X_{T}^{i,x}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbf{ω}})}})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \\
 & {\leq \frac{4\hspace{0pt}c\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}}\left( 1 + 2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\exp\left( \left\lbrack \sqrt{T} + m{(\mathbf{v}p)} \right\rbrack^{2}L^{2}T\mathbf{v} \right) \right.} \\
 & {\left. \cdot \left\lbrack \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \parallel x \parallel^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}} + L\left( T + m{(\mathbf{v}p)}\sqrt{T} \right) \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}} \right).}
\end{array}
```

Combining this, the triangle inequality, (208), (209), and (215) establishes that there exists $`{\mathbf{ω}} \in \Omega`$ such that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{({{\mathcal{A}_{i}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbf{ω}})}\hspace{0pt}x} + {\mathcal{B}_{i}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbf{ω}})}}})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \\
 & {= \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{({X_{T}^{i,x}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbf{ω}})}})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \\
 & {\leq \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack} - {{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \\
 & {+ \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{{\mathbb{E}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(X_{T}^{1,x})}} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{({X_{T}^{i,x}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbf{ω}})}})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \\
 & {\leq \varepsilon\left( 1 + 2^{\mathbf{w}/2}\exp\left( \left\lbrack \sqrt{T} + m{(\mathbf{w})} \right\rbrack^{2}L^{2}T\mathbf{w} \right) \right.} \\
 & \left. \cdot \left\lbrack \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \parallel x \parallel^{\mathbf{w}\hspace{0pt}p}\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{w}\hspace{0pt}p})}} + L\left( T + m{(\mathbf{w})}\sqrt{T} \right) \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{w}} \right) \\
 & {+ \frac{4\hspace{0pt}c\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}}\left( 1 + 2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\exp\left( \left\lbrack \sqrt{T} + m{(\mathbf{v}p)} \right\rbrack^{2}L^{2}T\mathbf{v} \right) \right.} \\
 & {\left. \cdot \left\lbrack \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \parallel x \parallel^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}} + L\left( T + m{(\mathbf{v}p)}\sqrt{T} \right) \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}} \right).}
\end{array}
```

The proof of Proposition 3.4 is thus completed. ∎

###### Corollary 3.5.

Let $`{d,n} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{\varepsilon,c,L,C} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, $`{\mathbf{v},p} \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, let $`{\langle \cdot , \cdot \rangle}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean scalar product, let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| :{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, let $`\nu:{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$ be a probability measure, assume that

``` math
{C = {{({p - 1})}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {3\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {L^{2}\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}{({\sqrt{T} + {\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}})}^{2}}})}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)}},
```

let $`\varphi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a continuous function, let $`\phi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a $`{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}/\mathcal{B}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}})}`$-measurable function which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{\left| {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right| \leq {{c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{\left| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| \leq {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}}},
```

and let $`\mu:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mu\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```
``` math
{{{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

and $`{\left\| {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\| + {\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}} \leq {L\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}}`$. Then

1.  (i)
    there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty`$, which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{aligned}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
    \end{aligned}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    there exist $`{A_{1},A_{2},\ldots,A_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, $`{b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ such that

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{cl}
     & {{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq {{({\varepsilon + {n^{- {1/2}}\hspace{0pt}c}})}\hspace{0pt}C}}.}
    \end{array}
    ```

###### Proof of Corollary 3.5.

Throughout this proof let $`r \in {(0,\infty)}`$ be given by $`r = {L\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}\hspace{0pt}{({\sqrt{T} + {\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}})}}`$. Note that (224) implies that $`\varphi`$ is an at most polynomially growing function. Combining this, (225), and (226) with item (i) in Corollary 2.24 (with $`d = d`$, $`m = d`$, $`T = T`$, $`\varphi = \varphi`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = \sigma`$ in the notation of Corollary 2.24) demonstrates that there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{aligned}
{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
\end{aligned}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$. This proves item (i). Furthermore, note that item (ii) in Proposition 3.4 (with $`d = d`$, $`n = n`$, $`p = p`$, $`T = T`$, $`c = c`$, $`\varepsilon = \varepsilon`$, $`L = L`$, $`\mathbf{v} = \mathbf{v}`$, $`\mathbf{w} = \mathbf{v}`$, $`\nu = \nu`$, $`\varphi = \varphi`$, $`\phi = \phi`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = \sigma`$ in the notation of Proposition 3.4) assures that there exist $`{A_{1},A_{2},\ldots,A_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, $`{b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ such that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \\
 & {\leq \varepsilon\left( 1 + 2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\exp\left( \left\lbrack \sqrt{T} + \max{\{ 2,\mathbf{v}\}} \right\rbrack^{2}L^{2}T\mathbf{v} \right) \right.} \\
 & \left. \cdot \left\lbrack L\left( T + \max{\{ 2,\mathbf{v}\}}\sqrt{T} \right) + \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \parallel x \parallel^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}} \right) \\
 & {+ n^{- {1/2}}\, 4c{(p - 1)}^{1/2}\left( 1 + 2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\exp\left( \left\lbrack \sqrt{T} + \max{\{ 2,\mathbf{v}p\}} \right\rbrack^{2}L^{2}T\mathbf{v} \right) \right.} \\
 & {\left. \cdot \left\lbrack L\left( T + \max{\{ 2,\mathbf{v}p\}}\sqrt{T} \right) + \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \parallel x \parallel^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}} \right).}
\end{array}
```

Next note that the fact that $`{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p} \geq 2`$ and the fact that $`p \geq 1`$ imply that

``` math
{{\max{\{ 2,\mathbf{v}\}}} \leq {\max{\{ 2,{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\}}} = {\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}}.
```

This demonstrates that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {1 + {2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\left\lbrack {\sqrt{T} + {\max{\{ 2,\mathbf{v}\}}}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}L^{2}\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}}} \\
 & {\cdot \left\lbrack {{L\hspace{0pt}\left( {T + {{\max{\{ 2,\mathbf{v}\}}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)} + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}}} \\
 & {\leq {1 + {2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\left\lbrack {\sqrt{T} + {\max{\{ 2,{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\}}}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}L^{2}\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}}}} \\
 & {\cdot \left\lbrack {{L\hspace{0pt}\left( {T + {{\max{\{ 2,{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\}}}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}}} \right)} + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}}} \\
 & {= {1 + {2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {L^{2}\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sqrt{T} + {\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right\rbrack^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}}}} \\
 & {\cdot \left\lbrack {{L\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{T}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\sqrt{T} + {\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}} \right)} + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}}} \\
 & {{= {1 + {2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {r^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {r + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

In addition, note that the fact that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ it holds that $`{1 + x} \leq {\exp{(x)}}`$ and the fact that for all $`y \in {(0,\infty)}`$ it holds that $`{1 + y + y^{2}} \leq {\frac{3}{2}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + y^{2}})}}`$ ensure that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {1 + {2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {r^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {r + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}}}} \\
 & {\leq {1 + {{\exp{({\mathbf{v}/2})}}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {r^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{({1 + r})}\hspace{0pt}{\max\left\{ 1,\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}} \right\}}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}}}}} \\
 & {\leq {1 + {{\exp{({\mathbf{v}/2})}}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {r^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\exp{(r)}}\hspace{0pt}{\max\left\{ 1,\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}} \right\}}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}}}}} \\
 & {\leq {2\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\mathbf{v} + {r^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}{\exp{({r\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}})}}\hspace{0pt}{\max\left\{ 1,\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \right\}}}} \\
 & {\leq {2\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {{({1 + r + r^{2}})}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)}} \\
 & {{\leq {2\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\frac{3}{2}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + r^{2}})}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, note that the fact that $`\mathbf{v} \geq 2`$ implies that $`{8 = 2^{3} \leq {\exp{(3)}} \leq {\exp\left( {\frac{3}{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)} \leq {\exp\left( {\frac{3}{2}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + r^{2}})}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}}.`$ Hence, we obtain that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{{8\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\frac{3}{2}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + r^{2}})}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}} \leq {{\exp\left( {\frac{3}{2}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + r^{2}})}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\frac{3}{2}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + r^{2}})}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}} = {\exp\left( {3\hspace{0pt}{({1 + r^{2}})}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this, (223), (230), (232), and (233) establishes that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \\
 & {\leq {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + {2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {r^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {r + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}}}} \right)}} \\
 & {+ {n^{- {1/2}}\hspace{0pt}\, 4\hspace{0pt}c\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + {2^{\mathbf{v}/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {r^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {r + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/{({\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p})}}} \right\rbrack^{\mathbf{v}}}} \right)}} \\
 & {\leq {\left\lbrack {\varepsilon + {n^{- {1/2}}\hspace{0pt}\, 4\hspace{0pt}c\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\, 2\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\frac{3}{2}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + r^{2}})}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)}} \\
 & {\leq {{({\varepsilon + {n^{- {1/2}}\hspace{0pt}c}})}\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}\, 8\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {\frac{3}{2}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + r^{2}})}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)}} \\
 & {\leq {{({\varepsilon + {n^{- {1/2}}\hspace{0pt}c}})}\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {3\hspace{0pt}{({1 + r^{2}})}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)}} \\
 & {{= {{({\varepsilon + {n^{- {1/2}}\hspace{0pt}c}})}\hspace{0pt}C}}.}
\end{array}
```

The proof of Corollary 3.5 is thus completed. ∎

### 3.3 Cost estimates

###### Proposition 3.6.

Let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{T,\varepsilon} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{c,L,C} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, $`{\mathbf{v},p} \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, $`n \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack{c^{2}\hspace{0pt}C^{2}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}},\infty)}}`$, let $`{\langle \cdot , \cdot \rangle}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean scalar product, let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| :{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, let $`\nu:{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$ be a probability measure, assume that

``` math
{C = {2\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {3\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {L^{2}\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}{({\sqrt{T} + {\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}})}^{2}}})}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)}},
```

let $`\varphi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a continuous function, let $`\phi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a $`{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}/\mathcal{B}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}})}`$-measurable function which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{\left| {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right| \leq {{c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{\left| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| \leq {C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}}
```

and let $`\mu:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mu\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```
``` math
{{{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

and $`{{\left\| {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\| + {\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}} \leq {L\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}}}.`$ Then

1.  (i)
    there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty`$, which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{aligned}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
    \end{aligned}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    there exist $`{A_{1},A_{2},\ldots,A_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, $`{b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ such that

    ``` math
    {\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```

###### Proof of Proposition 3.6.

Note that (237) implies that $`\varphi`$ is an at most polynomially growing function. Combining this, (238), and (239) with item (i) in Corollary 2.24 (with $`d = d`$, $`m = d`$, $`T = T`$, $`\varphi = \varphi`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = \sigma`$ in the notation of Corollary 2.24) establishes that there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{aligned}
{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
\end{aligned}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$. This proves item (i). Next note that Corollary 3.5 (with $`d = d`$, $`n = n`$, $`T = T`$, $`\varepsilon = {C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon}`$, $`c = c`$, $`L = L`$, $`\mathbf{v} = \mathbf{v}`$, $`p = p`$, $`\nu = \nu`$, $`\phi = \phi`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = \sigma`$ in the notation of Corollary 3.5) assures that there exist $`{A_{1},A_{2},\ldots,A_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, $`{b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ such that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq {\left( {{C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon} + {n^{- {1/2}}\hspace{0pt}c}} \right)\hspace{0pt}\frac{C}{2}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The hypothesis that $`n \geq {c^{2}\hspace{0pt}C^{2}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}}`$ hence assures that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{\phi\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \\
 & {\leq {\left( {{C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon} + {{({c^{2}\hspace{0pt}C^{2}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}})}^{- {1/2}}\hspace{0pt}c}} \right)\hspace{0pt}\frac{C}{2}}} \\
 & {{= {\left( {{C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon} + {C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon}} \right)\hspace{0pt}\frac{C}{2}} = \varepsilon}.}
\end{array}
```

This establishes item (ii). The proof of Proposition 3.6 is thus completed. ∎

### 3.4 Representation properties for artificial neural networks

###### Setting 3.7.

For every $`l \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\mathcal{M}_{l}`$ be the set of all Borel measurable functions from $`{\mathbb{R}}^{l}`$ to $`\mathbb{R}`$, let

``` math
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal{N} & {= \cup_{\mathcal{L} \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots\}}} \cup_{{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}})} \in {({{({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{L}})} \times {\{ 1\}}})}}\left( \times_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})} \right),}
\end{aligned}
```

let $`\mathbf{A}_{l}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{l}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{l}}`$, $`l \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, and $`\mathbf{a} \in \mathcal{M}_{1}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`l \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{l})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l}`$ that

``` math
{{\mathbf{A}_{l}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {({\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{1})}},{\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{2})}},\ldots,{\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{l})}})}},
```

and let $`{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}}:{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}}`$ and $`\mathcal{R}:{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow{\cup_{l = 1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}_{l}}}`$ be the functions which satisfy for all $`\mathcal{L} \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots\}}`$, $`{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}})} \in {({{({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{L}})} \times {\{ 1\}}})}`$, $`\Phi = {({(W_{1},B_{1})},\ldots,{(W_{\mathcal{L}},B_{\mathcal{L}})})} = {({(W_{k}^{(i,j)})}_{{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,l_{k}\}}},{j \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,l_{k - 1}\}}}},{(B_{k}^{(i)})}_{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,l_{k}\}}})}_{k \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,\mathcal{L}\}}} \in {( \times_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})})}`$, $`{x_{0} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{0}}},{{x_{1} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{1}},\ldots}},{x_{\mathcal{L} - 1} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{\mathcal{L} - 1}}}}`$ with $`{{\forall k} \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {(0,\mathcal{L})}}}:{x_{k} = {\mathbf{A}_{l_{k}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{k}\hspace{0pt}x_{k - 1}} + B_{k}})}}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \in \mathcal{M}_{l_{0}}},{{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{0})}} = {{W_{\mathcal{L}}\hspace{0pt}x_{\mathcal{L} - 1}} + B_{\mathcal{L}}}}},
```
``` math
{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{l_{k}}\left( {{\mathbb{1}_{{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\{ 0\}}}\hspace{0pt}{(B_{k}^{(i)})}} + {\sum\limits_{j = 1}^{l_{k - 1}}{\mathbb{1}_{{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\{ 0\}}}\hspace{0pt}{(W_{k}^{(i,j)})}}}} \right)}}},
```

and $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {\sum_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{l_{k}\hspace{0pt}{({l_{k - 1} + 1})}}}`$.

###### Lemma 3.8.

Assume Setting 3.7 and let $`{d,n} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{A_{1},A_{2},\ldots,A_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, $`{b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\phi \in \mathcal{N}`$ satisfy that $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}} \in \mathcal{M}_{d}`$. Then there exists $`\psi \in \mathcal{N}`$ which satisfies that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \leq {n^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}}`$, $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \leq {n\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}}`$, and $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \in \mathcal{M}_{d}`$ and which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}}} \right\rbrack}}.
```

###### Proof of Lemma 3.8.

Throughout this proof let $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, for all $`\mathcal{L} \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots\}}`$, $`{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}})} \in {({{({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{L}})} \times {\{ 1\}}})}`$, $`\Phi \in {( \times_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})})}`$ let $`W_{1}^{(\Phi)} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{1} \times l_{0}}`$, $`{W_{2}^{(\Phi)} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{2} \times l_{1}},\ldots}},`$ $`W_{\mathcal{L}}^{(\Phi)} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{\mathcal{L}} \times l_{\mathcal{L} - 1}}`$, $`B_{1}^{(\Phi)} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{1}}`$, $`B_{2}^{(\Phi)} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{2}},\ldots}`$, $`B_{\mathcal{L}}^{(\Phi)} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{\mathcal{L}}}`$ satisfy that

``` math
{\Phi = \left( {(W_{1}^{(\Phi)},B_{1}^{(\Phi)})},{(W_{2}^{(\Phi)},B_{2}^{(\Phi)})},\ldots,{(W_{\mathcal{L}}^{(\Phi)},B_{\mathcal{L}}^{(\Phi)})} \right)},
```

let $`N \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots\}}`$, $`{(u_{0},u_{1},\ldots,u_{N})} \in {({{\{ d\}} \times {({\mathbb{N}}^{N - 1})} \times {\{ 1\}}})}`$ satisfy that $`\phi \in \times_{k = 1}^{N}{({\mathbb{R}}^{u_{k} \times u_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{u_{k}})}`$ (i.e., $`\phi`$ corresponds to a fully connected feedforward artificial neural network with $`N + 1`$ layers with dimensions $`(u_{0},u_{1},\ldots,u_{N})`$), let $`\psi \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{{({n\hspace{0pt}u_{1}})} \times u_{0}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{n\hspace{0pt}u_{1}})} \times {( \times_{k = 2}^{N - 1}{({\mathbb{R}}^{{({n\hspace{0pt}u_{k}})} \times {({n\hspace{0pt}u_{k - 1}})}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{n\hspace{0pt}u_{k}})})} \times {({\mathbb{R}}^{u_{N} \times {({n\hspace{0pt}u_{N - 1}})}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{u_{N}})} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ (i.e., $`\psi`$ corresponds to a fully connected feedforward artificial neural network with $`N + 1`$ layers with dimensions $`(u_{0},{n\hspace{0pt}u_{1}},{n\hspace{0pt}u_{2}},\ldots,{n\hspace{0pt}u_{N - 1}},u_{N})`$) satisfy for all $`k \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots,{N - 1}\}}`$ that

``` math
{W_{1}^{(\psi)} = \begin{pmatrix}
{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}A_{1}} \\
{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}A_{2}} \\
\vdots \\
{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}A_{n}}
\end{pmatrix} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{{({n\hspace{0pt}u_{1}})} \times u_{0}} = {\mathbb{R}}^{{({n\hspace{0pt}u_{1}})} \times d}},
```
``` math
{{B_{1}^{(\psi)} = \begin{pmatrix}
{{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}b_{1}} + B_{1}^{(\phi)}} \\
{{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}b_{2}} + B_{1}^{(\phi)}} \\
\vdots \\
{{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}b_{n}} + B_{1}^{(\phi)}}
\end{pmatrix} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{n\hspace{0pt}u_{1}}},{B_{k}^{(\psi)} = \begin{pmatrix}
B_{k}^{(\phi)} \\
B_{k}^{(\phi)} \\
\vdots \\
B_{k}^{(\phi)}
\end{pmatrix} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{n\hspace{0pt}u_{k}}}},
```
``` math
{W_{k}^{(\psi)} = \begin{pmatrix}
W_{k}^{(\phi)} & 0 & \cdots & 0 \\
0 & W_{k}^{(\phi)} & \cdots & \vdots \\
\vdots & \vdots & \ddots & 0 \\
0 & \cdots & 0 & W_{k}^{(\phi)}
\end{pmatrix} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{{({n\hspace{0pt}u_{k}})} \times {({n\hspace{0pt}u_{k - 1}})}}},
```
``` math
{W_{N}^{(\psi)} = \begin{pmatrix}
{\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}W_{N}^{(\phi)}} & {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}W_{N}^{(\phi)}} & \cdots & {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}W_{N}^{(\phi)}}
\end{pmatrix} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{u_{N} \times {({n\hspace{0pt}u_{N - 1}})}} = {\mathbb{R}}^{1 \times {({n\hspace{0pt}u_{N - 1}})}}},
```
``` math
{{\text{and}\qquad B_{N}^{(\psi)}} = B_{N}^{(\phi)} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{u_{N}} = {\mathbb{R}}},
```

let $`y_{i,k} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{u_{k}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,n\}}`$, $`k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,N\}}`$, satisfy for all $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,n\}}`$, $`k \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,{N - 1}\}}`$ that

``` math
{{y_{i,0} = {{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}}},{y_{i,k} = {\mathbf{A}_{u_{k}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{k}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}y_{i,{k - 1}}} + B_{k}^{(\phi)}})}}}},
```
``` math
{{\text{and}\qquad y_{i,N}} = {{W_{N}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}y_{i,{N - 1}}} + B_{N}^{(\phi)}}},
```

and let $`{z_{0} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{u_{0}}},{{z_{1} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{n\hspace{0pt}u_{1}}},{{z_{2} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{n\hspace{0pt}u_{2}},\ldots}},{{z_{N - 1} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{n\hspace{0pt}u_{N - 1}}},{z_{N} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{u_{N}}}}}}`$ satisfy that

``` math
{{z_{0} = x},{z_{k} = {\mathbf{A}_{n\hspace{0pt}u_{k}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{k}^{(\psi)}\hspace{0pt}z_{k - 1}} + B_{k}^{(\psi)}})}}}},
```
``` math
{{\text{and}\qquad z_{N}} = {{W_{N}^{(\psi)}\hspace{0pt}z_{N - 1}} + B_{N}^{(\psi)}}}.
```

Observe that (246) proves that for all $`{l,L} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`v = {(v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{L\hspace{0pt}l})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{({L\hspace{0pt}l})}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\mathbf{A}_{L\hspace{0pt}l}\hspace{0pt}{(v)}} & {= \left( {\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(v_{1})}},{\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(v_{2})}},\ldots,{\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(v_{L\hspace{0pt}l})}} \right)} \\
 & {= \left( \mathbf{A}_{l}{(v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{l})},\mathbf{A}_{l}{(v_{l + 1},v_{l + 2},\ldots,v_{2\hspace{0pt}l})},\ldots, \right.} \\
 & {\left. \mathbf{A}_{l}{(v_{{{({L - 1})}\hspace{0pt}l} + 1},v_{{{({L - 1})}\hspace{0pt}l} + 2},\ldots,v_{L\hspace{0pt}l})} \right).}
\end{array}
```

Furthermore, note that the fact that $`u_{0} = d`$ ensures that $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \in \mathcal{M}_{u_{0}} = \mathcal{M}_{d}`$. Next observe that (247), (250), (256), and (257) imply that for all $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,n\}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{y_{i,N} = {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(y_{i,0})}} = {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}}}.
```

Moreover, observe that (247), (250), (258), and (259) ensure that

``` math
{z_{N} = {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(z_{0})}} = {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}}.
```

Next we claim that for all $`k \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,{N - 1}\}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{z_{k} = \left( y_{1,k},y_{2,k},\ldots,y_{n,k} \right)}.
```

We now prove (263) by induction on $`k \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,{N - 1}\}}`$. For the base case $`k = 1`$ note that (256) assures that for all $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,n\}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
```
