``` math
y_{i,1} & {= {\mathbf{A}_{u_{1}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}y_{i,0}} + B_{1}^{(\phi)}})}}} \\
 & {= {\mathbf{A}_{u_{1}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}} + B_{1}^{(\phi)}})}}} \\
 & {{= {\mathbf{A}_{u_{1}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + {W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}b_{i}} + B_{1}^{(\phi)}})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

This, (251), (252), (258), and (260) demonstrate that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
z_{1} & {= {\mathbf{A}_{n\hspace{0pt}u_{1}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{1}^{(\psi)}\hspace{0pt}x} + B_{1}^{(\psi)}})}}} \\
 & {= {\mathbf{A}_{n\hspace{0pt}u_{1}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\begin{pmatrix}
{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}A_{1}\hspace{0pt}x} \\
{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}A_{2}\hspace{0pt}x} \\
\vdots \\
{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}A_{n}\hspace{0pt}x}
\end{pmatrix} + \begin{pmatrix}
{{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}b_{1}} + B_{1}^{(\phi)}} \\
{{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}b_{2}} + B_{1}^{(\phi)}} \\
\vdots \\
{{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}b_{n}} + B_{1}^{(\phi)}}
\end{pmatrix}} \right)}} \\
 & {{= \begin{pmatrix}
{\mathbf{A}_{u_{1}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}A_{1}\hspace{0pt}x} + {W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}b_{1}} + B_{1}^{(\phi)}})}} \\
{\mathbf{A}_{u_{1}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}A_{2}\hspace{0pt}x} + {W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}b_{2}} + B_{1}^{(\phi)}})}} \\
\vdots \\
{\mathbf{A}_{u_{1}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}A_{n}\hspace{0pt}x} + {W_{1}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}b_{n}} + B_{1}^{(\phi)}})}}
\end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix}
y_{1,1} \\
y_{2,1} \\
\vdots \\
y_{n,1}
\end{pmatrix}}.}
\end{array}
```

This establishes (263) in the base case $`k = 1`$. For the induction step $`{\{ 1,2,\ldots,{N - 2}\}} \ni {k - 1}\rightarrow k \in {\{ 2,{3\hspace{0pt}\ldots},{N - 1}\}}`$ observe that (252), (253), (256), (258), and (260) imply that for all $`k \in {\{ 2,{3\hspace{0pt}\ldots},N\}}`$ with $`z_{k - 1} = {(y_{1,{k - 1}},y_{2,{k - 1}},\ldots,y_{n,{k - 1}})}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
z_{k} & {= {\mathbf{A}_{n\hspace{0pt}u_{k}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{W_{k}^{(\psi)}\hspace{0pt}\begin{pmatrix}
y_{1,{k - 1}} \\
y_{2,{k - 1}} \\
\vdots \\
y_{n,{k - 1}}
\end{pmatrix}} + B_{k}^{(\psi)}} \right)}} \\
 & {= {\mathbf{A}_{n\hspace{0pt}u_{k}}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\begin{pmatrix}
{W_{k}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}y_{1,{k - 1}}} \\
{W_{k}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}y_{2,{k - 1}}} \\
\vdots \\
{W_{k}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}y_{n,{k - 1}}}
\end{pmatrix} + \begin{pmatrix}
B_{k}^{(\phi)} \\
B_{k}^{(\phi)} \\
\vdots \\
B_{k}^{(\phi)}
\end{pmatrix}} \right)}} \\
 & {{= \begin{pmatrix}
{\mathbf{A}_{u_{k}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{k}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}y_{1,{k - 1}}} + B_{k}^{(\phi)}})}} \\
{\mathbf{A}_{u_{k}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{k}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}y_{2,{k - 1}}} + B_{k}^{(\phi)}})}} \\
\vdots \\
{\mathbf{A}_{u_{k}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{k}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}y_{n,{k - 1}}} + B_{k}^{(\phi)}})}}
\end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix}
y_{1,k} \\
y_{2,k} \\
\vdots \\
y_{n,k}
\end{pmatrix}}.}
\end{array}
```

Induction thus proves (263). Next note that (254), (255), (257), (259), and (263) demonstrate that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
z_{N} & {= {{W_{N}^{(\psi)}\hspace{0pt}z_{N - 1}} + B_{N}^{(\psi)}} = {{\begin{pmatrix}
{\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}W_{N}^{(\phi)}} & {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}W_{N}^{(\phi)}} & \cdots & {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}W_{N}^{(\phi)}}
\end{pmatrix}\hspace{0pt}\begin{pmatrix}
y_{1,{N - 1}} \\
y_{2,{N - 1}} \\
\vdots \\
y_{n,{N - 1}}
\end{pmatrix}} + B_{N}^{(\phi)}}} \\
 & {= {{\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{W_{N}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}y_{i,{N - 1}}}} \right\rbrack} + B_{N}^{(\phi)}} = {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{W_{N}^{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}y_{i,{N - 1}}}} + B_{N}^{(\phi)}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {{= {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}y_{i,N}} \right\rbrack}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this with (261) and (262) establishes that

``` math
{{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = z_{N} = {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}y_{i,N}} \right\rbrack} = {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}}} \right\rbrack}}.
```

In addition, observe that the fact that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}} = {\sum_{k = 1}^{N}{u_{k}\hspace{0pt}{({u_{k - 1} + 1})}}}`$ assures that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} & {= {{n\hspace{0pt}u_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({u_{0} + 1})}} + \left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{k = 2}^{N - 1}{n\hspace{0pt}u_{k}\hspace{0pt}{({{n\hspace{0pt}u_{k - 1}} + 1})}}} \right\rbrack + {u_{N}\hspace{0pt}{({{n\hspace{0pt}u_{N - 1}} + 1})}}}} \\
 & {\leq {{n^{2}\hspace{0pt}u_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({u_{0} + 1})}} + \left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{k = 2}^{N - 1}{n^{2}\hspace{0pt}u_{k}\hspace{0pt}{({u_{k - 1} + 1})}}} \right\rbrack + {n^{2}\hspace{0pt}u_{N}\hspace{0pt}{({u_{N - 1} + 1})}}}} \\
 & {{\leq {n^{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{N}{u_{k}\hspace{0pt}{({u_{k - 1} + 1})}}} \right\rbrack} = {n^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Furthermore, note that (251) – (255) and (269) demonstrate that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} & {\leq {{n\hspace{0pt}u_{1}\hspace{0pt}u_{0}} + {n\hspace{0pt}u_{1}} + \left\lbrack {{\sum\limits_{k = 2}^{N - 1}{n\hspace{0pt}u_{k}\hspace{0pt}u_{k - 1}}} + {n\hspace{0pt}u_{k}}} \right\rbrack + {u_{N}\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}u_{N - 1}} + u_{N}}} \\
 & {{\leq {n\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{N}{u_{k}\hspace{0pt}{({u_{k - 1} + 1})}}} \right\rbrack} = {n\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this, (268), and (269) completes the proof of Lemma 3.8. ∎

### 3.5 Cost estimates for artificial neural networks

###### Lemma 3.9.

Assume Setting 3.7, let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{T,\varepsilon} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{L,C,\mathbf{C}} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, $`c \in {\lbrack 1,\infty)}`$, $`{\mathbf{v},p} \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, let $`{\langle \cdot , \cdot \rangle}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean scalar product, let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| :{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, let $`\nu:{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$ be a probability measure, assume that

``` math
{C = {2\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {3\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {L^{2}\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}{({\sqrt{T} + {\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}})}^{2}}})}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)}},
```

assume that $`\mathbf{C} = {4\hspace{0pt}{({\max{\{ C,\varepsilon\}}})}^{4}}`$, let $`\varphi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a continuous function, let $`\mu:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mu\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```
``` math
{{{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

and $`{{\left\| {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\| + {\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}} \leq {L\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}}},`$ and let $`\phi \in \mathcal{N}`$ satisfy for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}} \in \mathcal{M}_{d}`$, $`\left| {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right| \leq {c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}`$, and

``` math
{\left| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| \leq {C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}}.
```

Then

1.  (i)
    there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty`$, which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{aligned}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
    \end{aligned}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    there exists $`\psi \in \mathcal{N}`$ such that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \leq {c^{4}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 4}}`$, $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \leq {c^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \in \mathcal{M}_{d}`$, and

    ``` math
    {\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```

###### Proof of Lemma 3.9.

Throughout this proof let $`n = {\min{({{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack{c^{2}\hspace{0pt}C^{2}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}},\infty)}})}}`$. Note that (274) implies that $`\varphi`$ is an at most polynomially growing function. Combining this, (272), and (273) with item (i) in Corollary 2.24 (with $`d = d`$, $`m = d`$, $`T = T`$, $`\varphi = \varphi`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = \sigma`$ in the notation of Corollary 2.24) establishes that there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{aligned}
{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
\end{aligned}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$. This proves item (i). Next note that Proposition 3.6 (with $`d = d`$, $`T = T`$, $`\varepsilon = \varepsilon`$, $`L = L`$, $`c = c`$, $`\mathbf{v} = \mathbf{v}`$, $`p = p`$, $`\nu = \nu`$, $`n = n`$, $`\varphi = \varphi`$, $`\phi = {\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}}`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = \sigma`$ in the notation of Proposition 3.6) ensures that there exist $`{A_{1},A_{2},\ldots,A_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, $`{b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{n}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ such that

``` math
{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
```

Moreover, observe that Lemma 3.8 demonstrates that there exists $`\psi \in \mathcal{N}`$ which satisfies that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \leq {n^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}}`$, $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \leq {n\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}}`$, and $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \in \mathcal{M}_{d}`$ and which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}}} \right\rbrack}}.
```

This and (278) assure that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \\
 & {{= \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {\frac{1}{n}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{n}{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{({{A_{i}\hspace{0pt}x} + b_{i}})}}} \right\rbrack}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, note that the hypothesis that $`\mathbf{C} = {4\hspace{0pt}{({\max{\{ C,\varepsilon\}}})}^{4}}`$ and $`c \in {\lbrack 1,\infty)}`$ implies that $`{{c^{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\mathbf{C}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}} \geq {\sqrt{4\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{4}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}} = 2}.`$ This ensures that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
n & {\leq {{c^{2}\hspace{0pt}C^{2}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}} + 1} \leq {2\hspace{0pt}{\max{\{{c^{2}\hspace{0pt}C^{2}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}},1\}}}}} \\
 & {= {\max{\{{c^{2}\hspace{0pt}\, 2\hspace{0pt}C^{2}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}},2\}}}} \\
 & {\leq {\max{\{{c^{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\mathbf{C}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}},2\}}}} \\
 & {{= {c^{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\mathbf{C}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}}}.}
\end{array}
```

This and the fact that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \leq {n^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}}`$ imply that

``` math
\begin{aligned}
{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} & {{\leq {{({c^{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\mathbf{C}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}})}^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}} = {c^{4}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 4}}}.}
\end{aligned}
```

Furthermore, note that (281), the fact that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \leq {n\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}}`$, and the fact that $`\mathbf{C} \geq 1`$ ensure that

``` math
{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \leq {c^{2}\hspace{0pt}\sqrt{\mathbf{C}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}} \leq {c^{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi)}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}}}.
```

This, (280), (282), and the fact that $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \in \mathcal{M}_{d}`$ establish item (ii). The proof of Lemma 3.9 is thus completed. ∎

###### Proposition 3.10.

Assume Setting 3.7, let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{T,a,r,R} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{L,\mathbf{C},\mathbf{z}} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, $`{b,c} \in {\lbrack 1,\infty)}`$, $`{\mathbf{v},p} \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, let $`{\langle \cdot , \cdot \rangle}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean scalar product, let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| :{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, let $`\nu:{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$ be a probability measure, assume that

``` math
\begin{matrix}
{\mathbf{C} = 4{\lbrack\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}\max\left\{ 2{(p - 1)}^{1/2}\exp\left( 3\mathbf{v}{(1 + L^{2}T{(\sqrt{T} + \mathbf{v}p)}^{2})} \right) \right.}} \\
{\left. \left. \left( 1 + \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \parallel x \parallel^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \right),R \right\} \right\rbrack^{4 + \mathbf{z}},}
\end{matrix}
```

let $`\varphi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a continuous function, let $`\mu:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mu\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```
``` math
{{{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

and $`{{\left\| {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\| + {\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}} \leq {L\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}}},`$ and let $`{(\phi_{\delta})}_{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ satisfy for all $`\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{\delta})}} \leq {a\hspace{0pt}\delta^{- \mathbf{z}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{\delta})}} \in \mathcal{M}_{d}`$, $`\left| {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right| \leq {c\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}`$, and

``` math
{\left| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| \leq {b\hspace{0pt}\delta\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}}.
```

Then

1.  (i)
    there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty`$, which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{r}
    {{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
    \end{array}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    there exist $`{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}_{\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}} \leq {\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}a\hspace{0pt}b^{\mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}c^{4}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 4} - \mathbf{z}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}} \leq {\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}a\hspace{0pt}b^{\mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}c^{2}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 2} - \mathbf{z}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}} \in \mathcal{M}_{d}`$, and

    ``` math
    {\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```

###### Proof of Proposition 3.10.

Throughout this proof let $`\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}`$ and let $`C \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ be given by

``` math
{C = {2\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {3\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {L^{2}\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}{({\sqrt{T} + {\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}})}^{2}}})}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)}}.
```

Note that (287) implies that $`\varphi`$ is an at most polynomially growing function. Combining this, (285), and (286) with item (i) in Corollary 2.24 (with $`d = d`$, $`m = d`$, $`T = T`$, $`\varphi = \varphi`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = \sigma`$ in the notation of Corollary 2.24) establishes that there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$. This proves item (i). Next observe that (287) ensures that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\left( {\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{\min{\{{b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon},r\}}})}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| & {\leq {{\min{\{{b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon},r\}}}\hspace{0pt}b\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}} \\
 & {\leq {b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}b\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}} \\
 & {{= {C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Lemma 3.9 (with $`d = d`$, $`T = T`$, $`\varepsilon = \varepsilon`$, $`L = L`$, $`c = c`$, $`\mathbf{v} = \mathbf{v}`$, $`p = p`$, $`\nu = \nu`$, $`\varphi = \varphi`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = \sigma`$, $`\phi = \phi_{\min{\{{b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon},r\}}}`$ in the notation of Lemma 3.9) hence assures that there exists $`\psi \in \mathcal{N}`$ such that

``` math
{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \leq {c^{4}\hspace{0pt}\, 4\hspace{0pt}{({\max{\{ C,\varepsilon\}}})}^{4}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{\min{\{{b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon},r\}}})}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 4}}},
```
``` math
{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \leq {c^{2}\hspace{0pt}\, 4\hspace{0pt}{({\max{\{ C,\varepsilon\}}})}^{4}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{\min{\{{b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon},r\}}})}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}}},
```
``` math
{{{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} \in {\mathcal{M}_{d},\text{and}}}\qquad{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}}.
```

Moreover, note that the fact that $`{b,C} \geq 1`$ and the fact that $`{\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}R^{- 1}} \leq 1`$ assures that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\min{\{{b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon},r\}}} & {\geq {\min\left\{ {b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon},{r\hspace{0pt}b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}R^{- 1}} \right\}}} \\
 & {{= {{\min{\{ 1,\frac{r}{R}\}}}\hspace{0pt}b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon}}.}
\end{array}
```

This, the fact that $`C \geq 1`$, the fact that $`\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}`$, (284), and (290) ensure that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {4\hspace{0pt}{({\max{\{ C,\varepsilon\}}})}^{4}\hspace{0pt}{({\min{\{{b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon},r\}}})}^{- \mathbf{z}}} \\
 & {\leq {4\hspace{0pt}{({\max{\{ C,R\}}})}^{4}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\min{\{ 1,\frac{r}{R}\}}}\hspace{0pt}b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon} \right)^{- \mathbf{z}}}} \\
 & {= {4\hspace{0pt}{({\max{\{ C,R\}}})}^{4}\hspace{0pt}{({\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}})}^{\mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}b^{\mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}C^{\mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- \mathbf{z}}}} \\
 & {\leq {4\hspace{0pt}{({\max{\{ C,R\}}})}^{4 + \mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}{({\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}})}^{4 + \mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}b^{\mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- \mathbf{z}}}} \\
 & {{= {\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}b^{\mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- \mathbf{z}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this with the hypothesis that for all $`\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{\delta})}} \leq {a\hspace{0pt}\delta^{- \mathbf{z}}}`$ and (293) demonstrates that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} & {\leq {c^{4}\hspace{0pt}\, 4\hspace{0pt}{({\max{\{ C,\varepsilon\}}})}^{4}\hspace{0pt}a\hspace{0pt}{({\min{\{{b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon},r\}}})}^{- \mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 4}}} \\
 & {{\leq {c^{4}\hspace{0pt}a\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}b^{\mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- \mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 4}} = {\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}a\hspace{0pt}b^{\mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}c^{4}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 4} - \mathbf{z}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Furthermore, observe that the hypothesis that for all $`\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{\delta})}} \leq {a\hspace{0pt}\delta^{- \mathbf{z}}}`$, (294), and (297) demonstrate that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}} & {\leq {c^{2}\hspace{0pt}\, 4\hspace{0pt}{({\max{\{ C,\varepsilon\}}})}^{4}\hspace{0pt}a\hspace{0pt}{({\min{\{{b^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}C^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon},r\}}})}^{- \mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}}} \\
 & {{\leq {c^{2}\hspace{0pt}a\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}b^{\mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- \mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}} = {\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}a\hspace{0pt}b^{\mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}c^{2}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 2} - \mathbf{z}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this, (295), and (298) establishes item (ii). The proof of Proposition 3.10 is thus completed. ∎

###### Corollary 3.11.

Assume Setting 3.7, let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{T,r,R} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{L,{\mathfrak{C}},v,w,z,\mathbf{z}} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, $`{\mathfrak{c}} \in {\lbrack 1,\infty)}`$, $`{\mathbf{v},p} \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, let $`{\langle \cdot , \cdot \rangle}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean scalar product, let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| :{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, let $`\nu:{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$ be a probability measure, assume that

``` math
\begin{matrix}
{{\mathfrak{C}} = \left\lbrack {\mathfrak{c}}\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}\max\left\{ 2{(p - 1)}^{1/2}\exp\left( 3\mathbf{v}{(1 + L^{2}T{(\sqrt{T} + \mathbf{v}p)}^{2})} \right) \right. \right.} \\
{\left. \left. \left( 1 + \left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \parallel x \parallel^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\nu{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \right),R \right\} \right\rbrack^{5 + \mathbf{z}},}
\end{matrix}
```

let $`\varphi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a continuous function, let $`\mu:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and $`\sigma:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mu\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```
``` math
{{{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

and $`{{\left\| {\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\| + {\|\left| {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}} \leq {L\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|})}}},`$ and let $`{(\phi_{\delta})}_{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ satisfy for all $`\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{\delta})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{z}\hspace{0pt}\delta^{- \mathbf{z}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{\delta})}} \in \mathcal{M}_{d}`$, $`\left| {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right| \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{v}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}`$, and

``` math
{\left| {{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{w}\hspace{0pt}\delta\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}}.
```

Then

1.  (i)
    there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty`$, which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{r}
    {{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
    \end{array}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    there exist $`{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}_{\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {4\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 4} - \mathbf{z}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {2\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 2} - \mathbf{z}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}} \in \mathcal{M}_{d}`$, and

    ``` math
    {\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```

###### Proof of Corollary 3.11.

Throughout this proof let $`{C,\mathbf{C}} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ be given by

``` math
C = {2\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {3\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {L^{2}\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}{({\sqrt{T} + {\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}})}^{2}}})}} \right)}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)}
```

and

``` math
{\mathbf{C} = {4\hspace{0pt}\left. \lbrack{{\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}}\hspace{0pt}{\max{\{ C,R\}}}} \right\rbrack^{4 + \mathbf{z}}}}.
```

Note that (303) implies that $`\varphi`$ is an at most polynomially growing function. Combining this, (301), and (302) with item (i) in Corollary 2.24 (with $`d = d`$, $`m = d`$, $`T = T`$, $`\varphi = \varphi`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = \sigma`$ in the notation of Corollary 2.24) establishes that there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|^{q}}}} < \infty`$, which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$. This proves item (i). Next observe that Proposition 3.10 (with $`d = d`$, $`T = T`$, $`a = {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{z}}`$, $`r = r`$, $`R = R`$, $`L = L`$, $`\mathbf{z} = \mathbf{z}`$, $`b = {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{w}}`$, $`c = {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{v}}`$, $`\mathbf{v} = \mathbf{v}`$, $`p = p`$, $`\nu = \nu`$, $`\varphi = \varphi`$, $`\mu = \mu`$, $`\sigma = \sigma`$, $`{(\phi_{\delta})}_{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}} = {(\phi_{\delta})}_{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}}`$ in the notation of Proposition 3.10) proves that there exist $`{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}_{\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}} \leq {\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{z}\hspace{0pt}{({{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{w}})}^{\mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}{({{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{v}})}^{4}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 4} - \mathbf{z}}}},
```
``` math
{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}} \leq {\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{z}\hspace{0pt}{({{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{w}})}^{\mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}{({{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{v}})}^{2}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 2} - \mathbf{z}}}},
```
``` math
{{{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}} \in {\mathcal{M}_{d},\text{and}}}\qquad{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}}.
```

Furthermore, note that the fact that $`{p,\mathbf{v}} \geq 2`$ assures that

``` math
{C \geq {2\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {3\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {L^{2}\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}{({\sqrt{T} + {\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}})}^{2}}})}} \right)}} \geq {2\hspace{0pt}{\exp{(6)}}} \geq 4}.
```

This implies that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{\mathfrak{c}}^{5 + \mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{C}} & {= {{\mathfrak{c}}^{5 + \mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}\, 4\hspace{0pt}\left. \lbrack{{\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}}\hspace{0pt}{\max{\{ C,R\}}}} \right\rbrack^{4 + \mathbf{z}}}} \\
 & {\leq {{\mathfrak{c}}^{5 + \mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}C\hspace{0pt}\left. \lbrack{{\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}}\hspace{0pt}{\max{\{ C,R\}}}} \right\rbrack^{4 + \mathbf{z}}}} \\
 & {\leq {{\mathfrak{c}}^{5 + \mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}\left. \lbrack{{\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}}\hspace{0pt}{\max{\{ C,R\}}}} \right\rbrack^{5 + \mathbf{z}}}} \\
 & {{= {\mathfrak{C}}}.}
\end{array}
```

This and (309) demonstrate that for all $`\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}} \leq {\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{c}}^{5 + \mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}d^{z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {4\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 4} - \mathbf{z}}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {4\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 4} - \mathbf{z}}}}.
```

Moreover, observe that (310), (313), and the fact that $`{\mathfrak{c}} \geq 1`$ assure that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{\varepsilon})}} & {\leq {\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{c}}^{3 + \mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}d^{z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {2\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 2} - \mathbf{z}}}} \\
 & {{\leq {\mathbf{C}\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{c}}^{5 + \mathbf{z}}\hspace{0pt}d^{z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {2\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 2} - \mathbf{z}}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {2\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 2} - \mathbf{z}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this, (311), and (314) establishes item (ii). The proof of Corollary 3.11 is thus completed. ∎

###### Proposition 3.12.

Assume Setting 3.7, let $`\mathcal{I}`$ be a set, let $`{{\mathfrak{d}} = {({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}_{i \in \mathcal{I}}}:{\mathcal{I}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}}`$ be a function, for every $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`{\langle \cdot , \cdot \rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean scalar product, for every $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm and let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| {}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, let $`{T,r,R} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{{\mathfrak{C}},L,v,w,z,\mathbf{z},\theta} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, $`{\mathfrak{c}} \in {\lbrack 1,\infty)}`$, $`{\mathbf{v},p} \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, let $`\nu_{d}:{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$, $`d \in {{Im}{({\mathfrak{d}})}}`$, be probability measures, assume that

``` math
\begin{matrix}
{{\mathfrak{C}} = \left\lbrack {\mathfrak{c}}\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}\max\left\{ 2{(p - 1)}^{1/2}\exp\left( 3\mathbf{v}{(1 + L^{2}T{(\sqrt{T} + \mathbf{v}p)}^{2})} \right) \right. \right.} \\
{\left. \left. \left( 1 + \sup_{i \in \mathcal{I}}\left\lbrack {({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{- \theta}\left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}} \parallel x \parallel_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\nu_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \right\rbrack \right),R \right\} \right\rbrack^{5 + \mathbf{z}},}
\end{matrix}
```

let $`\varphi_{i}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`i \in \mathcal{I}`$, be continuous functions, let $`\mu_{i}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}`$, $`i \in \mathcal{I}`$, and $`\sigma_{i}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i} \times {\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}`$, $`i \in \mathcal{I}`$, be functions which satisfy for all $`i \in \mathcal{I}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mu_{i}\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```
``` math
{{{\sigma_{i}\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

and $`{{\left\| {\mu_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}} + {\|\left| {\sigma_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}},{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}})}}} \leq {L\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}})}}},`$ and let $`{(\phi_{i,\delta})}_{{i \in \mathcal{I}},{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ satisfy for all $`i \in \mathcal{I}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}`$ that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{i,\delta})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{z}\hspace{0pt}\delta^{- \mathbf{z}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{i,\delta})}} \in \mathcal{M}_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}`$, $`\left| {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{i,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right| \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{v}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}`$, and

``` math
{\left| {{\varphi_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{i,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{w}\hspace{0pt}\delta\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}}.
```

Then

1.  (i)
    there exist unique continuous functions $`u_{i}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`i \in \mathcal{I}`$, which satisfy for all $`i \in \mathcal{I}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}`$ that $`{u_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfy for all $`i \in \mathcal{I}`$ that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}}\frac{|{u_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfy for all $`i \in \mathcal{I}`$ that $`{u_{i}|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{cl}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u_{i}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u_{i}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}} \\
     & {+ {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u_{i}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}}
    \end{array}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    there exist $`{(\psi_{i,\varepsilon})}_{{i \in \mathcal{I}},{\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`i \in \mathcal{I}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}`$ it holds that

    ``` math
    {{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{i,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{{{({5 + \mathbf{z}})}\hspace{0pt}\theta} + z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {4\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 4} - \mathbf{z}}}},
    ```
    ``` math
    {{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{i,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{{{({5 + \mathbf{z}})}\hspace{0pt}\theta} + z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {2\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 2} - \mathbf{z}}}},{{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{i,\varepsilon})}} \in \mathcal{M}_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}},
    ```
    ``` math
    {{\text{and}\qquad\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{i,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```

###### Proof of Proposition 3.12.

Throughout this proof let $`{i \in \mathcal{I}},`$ let $`c_{0} \in {(0,\infty)}`$ be given by

``` math
{c_{0} = {2\hspace{0pt}{({p - 1})}^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{\exp\left( {3\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {L^{2}\hspace{0pt}T\hspace{0pt}{({\sqrt{T} + {\mathbf{v}\hspace{0pt}p}})}^{2}}})}} \right)}}},
```

and let $`\mathcal{C} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ be given by

``` math
{\mathcal{C} = \left\lbrack {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}{\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}}\hspace{0pt}{\max\left\{ R,{c_{0}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}{{\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)} \right\}}} \right\rbrack^{5 + \mathbf{z}}}.
```

Note that the fact that $`0 < {({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{- \theta} \leq 1`$ implies that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\max\left\{ {c_{0}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}{{\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)},R \right\}} \\
 & {\leq {\max\left\{ {c_{0}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{\theta}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{- \theta}} + {{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{\theta}\hspace{0pt}{\sup\limits_{j \in \mathcal{I}}\left\lbrack {{({\mathfrak{d}}_{j})}^{- \theta}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}}{{\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right)^{1/p}} \right\rbrack}}} \right)},R \right\}}} \\
 & {\leq {\max\left\{ {c_{0}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{\theta}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + {\sup\limits_{j \in \mathcal{I}}\left\lbrack {{({\mathfrak{d}}_{j})}^{- \theta}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}}{{\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right)^{1/p}} \right\rbrack}} \right)},{{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{\theta}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{- \theta}\hspace{0pt}R} \right\}}} \\
 & {{\leq {{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{\theta}\hspace{0pt}{\max\left\{ {c_{0}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + {\sup\limits_{j \in \mathcal{I}}\left\lbrack {{({\mathfrak{d}}_{j})}^{- \theta}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}}{{\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right)^{1/p}} \right\rbrack}} \right)},R \right\}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Therefore, (325), (324), and (316) ensure that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\mathcal{C} & {= \left\lbrack {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}{\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}}\hspace{0pt}{\max\left\{ {c_{0}\hspace{0pt}\left( {1 + \left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}{{\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \right)},R \right\}}} \right\rbrack^{5 + \mathbf{z}}} \\
 & {\leq {({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{\theta\hspace{0pt}{({5 + \mathbf{z}})}}\left\lbrack {\mathfrak{c}}\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}} \right.} \\
 & {\left. \max\left\{ c_{0}\left( 1 + \sup\limits_{j \in \mathcal{I}}\left\lbrack {({\mathfrak{d}}_{j})}^{- \theta}\left( \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}} \parallel x \parallel_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\nu_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}{(dx)} \right)^{1/p} \right\rbrack \right),R \right\} \right\rbrack^{5 + \mathbf{z}}} \\
 & {\leq {({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{\theta\hspace{0pt}{({5 + \mathbf{z}})}}\left\lbrack {\mathfrak{c}}\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}\max\left\{ 2{(p - 1)}^{1/2}\exp\left( 3\mathbf{v}{(1 + L^{2}T{(\sqrt{T} + \mathbf{v}p)}^{2})} \right) \right. \right.} \\
 & {\left. \left. \left( 1 + \sup\limits_{j \in \mathcal{I}}\left\lbrack {({\mathfrak{d}}_{j})}^{- \theta}\left( \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}} \parallel x \parallel_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\nu_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{j}}{(dx)} \right)^{1/p} \right\rbrack \right),R \right\} \right\rbrack^{5 + \mathbf{z}}} \\
 & {{= {{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{\theta\hspace{0pt}{({5 + \mathbf{z}})}}\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{C}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, observe that (319) implies that $`\varphi_{i}`$ is an at most polynomially growing function. Combining this, (317), and (318) with item (i) in Corollary 2.24 (with $`d = {\mathfrak{d}}_{i}`$, $`m = {\mathfrak{d}}_{i}`$, $`T = T`$, $`\varphi = \varphi_{i}`$, $`\mu = \mu_{i}`$, $`\sigma = \sigma_{i}`$ in the notation of Corollary 2.24) establishes that there exists a unique continuous function $`u_{i}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}`$ that $`{u_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}}\frac{|{u_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfies that $`{u_{i}|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u_{i}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u_{i}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u_{i}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}`$. This proves item (i). Next observe that Corollary 3.11 (with $`d = {\mathfrak{d}}_{i}`$, $`T = T`$, $`r = r`$, $`R = R`$, $`v = v`$, $`w = w`$, $`z = z`$, $`\mathbf{z} = \mathbf{z}`$, $`{\mathfrak{c}} = {\mathfrak{c}}`$, $`\mathbf{v} = \mathbf{v}`$, $`p = p`$, $`\nu = \nu_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}`$, $`\varphi = \varphi_{i}`$, $`\mu = \mu_{i}`$, $`\sigma = \sigma_{i}`$, $`{(\phi_{\delta})}_{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}} = {(\phi_{i,\delta})}_{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}}`$ in the notation of Corollary 3.11) demonstrates that there exists $`{(\psi_{i,\varepsilon})}_{\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{i,\varepsilon})}} \leq {\mathcal{C}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {4\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 4} - \mathbf{z}}}},
```
``` math
{{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{i,\varepsilon})}} \leq {\mathcal{C}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{d}}_{i})}^{z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {2\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 2} - \mathbf{z}}}},{{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{i,\varepsilon})}} \in \mathcal{M}_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}}},
```
``` math
{{\text{and}\qquad\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{i}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{i,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{{\mathfrak{d}}_{i}}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \leq \varepsilon}.
```

Combining this with (327) establishes item (ii). The proof of Proposition 3.12 is thus completed. ∎

###### Corollary 3.13.

Assume Setting 3.7, let $`{T,r,R} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{{\mathfrak{C}},L,v,w,z,\mathbf{z},\theta} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, $`{\mathfrak{c}} \in {\lbrack 1,\infty)}`$, $`{\mathbf{v},p} \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, for every $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`{\langle \cdot , \cdot \rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean scalar product, for every $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm and let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| {}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, let $`\nu_{d}:{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be probability measures, assume that

``` math
\begin{matrix}
{{\mathfrak{C}} = \left\lbrack {\mathfrak{c}}\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}\max\left\{ 2{(p - 1)}^{1/2}\exp\left( 3\mathbf{v}{(1 + L^{2}T{(\sqrt{T} + \mathbf{v}p)}^{2})} \right) \right. \right.} \\
{\left. \left. \left( 1 + \sup_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}\left\lbrack d^{- \theta}\left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \parallel x \parallel_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\nu_{d}{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \right\rbrack \right),R \right\} \right\rbrack^{5 + \mathbf{z}},}
\end{matrix}
```

let $`\varphi_{d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be continuous functions, let $`\mu_{d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, and $`\sigma_{d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be functions which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```
``` math
{{{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

and $`{{\left\| {\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} + {\|\left| {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}} \leq {L\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}}},`$ and let $`{(\phi_{d,\delta})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{z}\hspace{0pt}\delta^{- \mathbf{z}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}} \in \mathcal{M}_{d}`$, $`\left| {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right| \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{v}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}`$, and

``` math
{\left| {{\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{w}\hspace{0pt}\delta\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}}.
```

Then

1.  (i)
    there exist unique continuous functions $`u_{d}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{u_{d}|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{cl}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}} \\
     & {+ {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}
    \end{array}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    there exist $`{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}`$ it holds that

    ``` math
    {{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{{({5 + \mathbf{z}})}\hspace{0pt}\theta} + z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {4\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 4} - \mathbf{z}}}},
    ```
    ``` math
    {{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{{({5 + \mathbf{z}})}\hspace{0pt}\theta} + z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {2\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 2} - \mathbf{z}}}},{{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \in \mathcal{M}_{d}}},
    ```
    ``` math
    {{\text{and}\qquad\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p}} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```

###### Proof of Corollary 3.13.

Observe that Proposition 3.12 (with $`\mathcal{I} = {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{\mathfrak{d}} = {id}_{\mathbb{N}}`$, $`T = T`$, $`r = r`$, $`R = R`$, $`L = L`$, $`v = v`$, $`w = w`$, $`z = z`$, $`\mathbf{z} = \mathbf{z}`$, $`\theta = \theta`$, $`{\mathfrak{c}} = {\mathfrak{c}}`$, $`\mathbf{v} = \mathbf{v}`$, $`p = p`$, $`{(\nu_{d})}_{d \in {{Im}{({\mathfrak{d}})}}} = {(\nu_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}`$, $`{(\varphi_{i})}_{i \in \mathcal{I}} = {(\varphi_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}`$, $`{(\mu_{i})}_{i \in \mathcal{I}} = {(\mu_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}`$, $`{(\sigma_{i})}_{i \in \mathcal{I}} = {(\sigma_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}`$, $`{(\phi_{i,\delta})}_{{i \in \mathcal{I}},{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}}} = {(\phi_{d,\delta})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}}}`$ in the notation of Proposition 3.12) establishes items (i)–(ii). The proof of Corollary 3.13 is thus completed. ∎

### 3.6 Artificial neural networks with continuous activation functions

In this subsection we establish in Theorem 3.14 below the main result of this article. Theorem 3.14 proves, roughly speaking, that fully-connected artificial neural networks overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of Black-Scholes PDEs (see (348) in item (ii) in Theorem 3.14 for details). In Theorem 3.14 the approximation error between the solution of the PDE and the artificial neural network is measured by means of $`L^{p}`$-norms with respect to the general probability measures $`\nu_{d}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, in Theorem 3.14. To make Theorem 3.14 easier accessible, we derive a simplified and specialized version of Theorem 3.14 in Corollary 3.16 below. In particular, in Corollary 3.16 below we specialize Theorem 3.14 to the case where the general probability measures $`\nu_{d}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, are nothing else but the continuous uniform distribution on $`{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}^{d}`$. Our proof of Corollary 3.16 uses the elementary estimate in Lemma 3.15 below. For the sake of completeness we also present in this subsection a detailed proof of Lemma 3.15.

###### Theorem 3.14.

Let $`{T,r,R} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{v,w,z,\mathbf{z},\theta} \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, $`{\mathfrak{c}} \in {\lbrack 1,\infty)}`$, $`{\mathbf{v},p} \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, for every $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`{\langle \cdot , \cdot \rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean scalar product, for every $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm and let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| {}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, let $`\nu_{d}:{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be probability measures, let $`\varphi_{d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be continuous functions, let $`\mu_{d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, and $`\sigma_{d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ , be functions which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```
``` math
{{{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

and $`{\left\| {\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} + {\|\left| {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}} \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}}`$, let

``` math
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal{N} & {= \cup_{\mathcal{L} \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots\}}} \cup_{{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}})} \in {({{({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{L}})} \times {\{ 1\}}})}}\left( \times_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})} \right),}
\end{aligned}
```

assume that $`{{\sup_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}\left\lbrack {d^{- {\theta\hspace{0pt}p}}\hspace{0pt}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}}} \right\rbrack} < \infty},`$ let $`\mathbf{A}_{d} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, and $`\mathbf{a} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{\mathbf{A}_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {({\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{1})}},{\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{2})}},\ldots,{\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{d})}})}},
```

let $`{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}}:{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}}`$ and $`\mathcal{R}:{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow{\cup_{d = 1}^{\infty}{C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}}}`$ be the functions which satisfy for all $`\mathcal{L} \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots\}}`$, $`{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}})} \in {({{({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{L}})} \times {\{ 1\}}})}`$, $`\Phi = {({(W_{1},B_{1})},\ldots,{(W_{\mathcal{L}},B_{\mathcal{L}})})} = {({(W_{k}^{(i,j)})}_{{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,l_{k}\}}},{j \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,l_{k - 1}\}}}},{(B_{k}^{(i)})}_{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,l_{k}\}}})}_{k \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,\mathcal{L}\}}} \in {( \times_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})})}`$, $`{x_{0} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{0}}},{{x_{1} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{1}},\ldots}},{x_{\mathcal{L} - 1} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{\mathcal{L} - 1}}}}`$ with $`{{\forall k} \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {(0,\mathcal{L})}}}:{x_{k} = {\mathbf{A}_{l_{k}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{k}\hspace{0pt}x_{k - 1}} + B_{k}})}}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{0}},{\mathbb{R}})}}},{{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{0})}} = {{W_{\mathcal{L}}\hspace{0pt}x_{\mathcal{L} - 1}} + B_{\mathcal{L}}}}},
```
``` math
{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{l_{k}}\left( {{\mathbb{1}_{{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\{ 0\}}}\hspace{0pt}{(B_{k}^{(i)})}} + {\sum\limits_{j = 1}^{l_{k - 1}}{\mathbb{1}_{{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\{ 0\}}}\hspace{0pt}{(W_{k}^{(i,j)})}}}} \right)}}},
```

and $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {\sum_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{l_{k}\hspace{0pt}{({l_{k - 1} + 1})}}}`$, and let $`{(\phi_{d,\delta})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{z}\hspace{0pt}\delta^{- \mathbf{z}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, $`\left| {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right| \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{v}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}`$, and

``` math
{\left| {{\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{w}\hspace{0pt}\delta\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}})}}}.
```

Then

1.  (i)
    there exist unique continuous functions $`u_{d}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{u_{d}|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{cl}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}} \\
     & {+ {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}
    \end{array}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    there exist $`{\mathfrak{C}} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{{({5 + \mathbf{z}})}\hspace{0pt}\theta} + z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {4\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 4} - \mathbf{z}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{{({5 + \mathbf{z}})}\hspace{0pt}\theta} + z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {2\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 2} - \mathbf{z}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, and

    ``` math
    {\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```

###### Proof of Theorem 3.14.

Throughout this proof let $`{\mathfrak{C}} \in {(0,\infty)}`$ be given by

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\mathfrak{C}} & {= \left\lbrack {\mathfrak{c}}\max{\{ 1,\frac{R}{r}\}}\max\left\{ R,2{(p - 1)}^{1/2}\exp\left( 3\mathbf{v}{(1 + {\mathfrak{c}}^{2}T{(\sqrt{T} + \mathbf{v}p)}^{2})} \right) \right. \right.} \\
 & {\left. \left. \cdot \left( 1 + \sup_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}\left\lbrack d^{- \theta}\left\lbrack \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \parallel x \parallel_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{p\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{v}}\nu_{d}{(dx)} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \right\rbrack \right) \right\} \right\rbrack^{5 + \mathbf{z}}}
\end{array}
```

and for every $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\mathcal{M}_{d}`$ be the set of all Borel measurable functions from $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ to $`\mathbb{R}`$. Note that Corollary 3.13 (with $`T = T`$, $`r = r`$, $`R = R`$, $`L = {\mathfrak{c}}`$, $`v = v`$, $`w = w`$, $`z = z`$, $`\mathbf{z} = \mathbf{z}`$, $`\theta = \theta`$, $`{\mathfrak{c}} = {\mathfrak{c}}`$, $`\mathbf{v} = \mathbf{v}`$, $`p = p`$, $`{(\nu_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} = {(\nu_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}`$, $`{(\varphi_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} = {(\varphi_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}`$, $`{(\mu_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} = {(\mu_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}`$, $`{(\sigma_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} = {(\sigma_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}`$, $`{(\phi_{d,\delta})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}}} = {(\phi_{d,\delta})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}}}`$ in the notation of Corollary 3.13) demonstrates that there exist unique continuous functions $`u_{d}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{u_{d}|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} + {\langle{{({\nabla_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and that there exist $`{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{{({5 + \mathbf{z}})}\hspace{0pt}\theta} + z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {4\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 4} - \mathbf{z}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{{({5 + \mathbf{z}})}\hspace{0pt}\theta} + z + {w\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{z}} + {2\hspace{0pt}v}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 2} - \mathbf{z}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \in \mathcal{M}_{d}`$, and

``` math
{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
```

The fact that $`{{Im}{(\mathcal{R})}} \subseteq {\cup_{d = 1}^{\infty}{C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}}`$ hence demonstrates that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,R\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}} \subseteq \mathcal{M}_{d}`$. Combining this with (350) and (351) establishes items (i)–(ii). The proof of Theorem 3.14 is thus completed. ∎

###### Lemma 3.15.

Let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`p \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, $`u \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`v \in {(u,\infty)}`$, and let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm. Then

``` math
{{\frac{1}{{({v - u})}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}{\int_{{\lbrack u,v\rbrack}^{d}}{\left\| x \right\|^{p}\hspace{0pt}{dx}}}} \leq {d^{p/2}\hspace{0pt}{\max\left\{ {|u|}^{p},{|v|}^{p} \right\}}}}.
```

###### Proof of Lemma 3.15.

Observe that the Hölder inequality implies that for all $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left\| x \right\|^{2} & {= {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}{|x_{i}|}^{2}} \leq {\left( {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}{|x_{i}|}^{p}} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}1} \right)^{1 - {2/p}}}} \\
 & {{= {\left( {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}{|x_{i}|}^{p}} \right)^{2/p}\hspace{0pt}d^{1 - {2/p}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Next note that the Fubini theorem ensures that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\int_{{\lbrack u,v\rbrack}^{d}}{\left( {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}{|x_{i}|}^{p}} \right)\hspace{0pt}d\hspace{0pt}{(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})}}} = {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}{\int_{{\lbrack u,v\rbrack}^{d}}{{|x_{i}|}^{p}\hspace{0pt}d\hspace{0pt}{(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})}}}}} \\
 & {= {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}{\left( {\int_{u}^{v}{{|x_{i}|}^{p}\hspace{0pt}{dx_{i}}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{u}^{v}{1\hspace{0pt}{dt}}} \right)^{d - 1}}} = {d\hspace{0pt}\left( {\int_{u}^{v}{{|t|}^{p}\hspace{0pt}{dt}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{({v - u})}^{d - 1}}} \\
 & {{\leq {d\hspace{0pt}{({v - u})}^{d}\hspace{0pt}{\sup\limits_{t \in {\lbrack u,v\rbrack}}\left\lbrack {|t|}^{p} \right\rbrack}} = {d\hspace{0pt}{({v - u})}^{d}\hspace{0pt}{\max\left\{ {|u|}^{p},{|v|}^{p} \right\}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this with (353) demonstrates that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\frac{1}{{({v - u})}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}{\int_{{\lbrack u,v\rbrack}^{d}}{\left\| x \right\|^{p}\hspace{0pt}{dx}}}} \\
 & {\leq {\frac{1}{{({v - u})}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{p/2} - 1}\hspace{0pt}{\int_{{\lbrack u,v\rbrack}^{d}}{\left( {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}{|x_{i}|}^{p}} \right)\hspace{0pt}d\hspace{0pt}{(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})}}}}} \\
 & {{\leq {d^{p/2}\hspace{0pt}{\max\left\{ {|u|}^{p},{|v|}^{p} \right\}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The proof of Lemma 3.15 is thus completed. ∎

###### Corollary 3.16.

Let $`{T,r,{\mathfrak{c}},p} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, for every $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm and let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| {}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, let $`\varphi_{d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be continuous functions, let $`\mu_{d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, and $`\sigma_{d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be functions which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```
``` math
{{{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
```

and $`{\left\| {\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} + {\|\left| {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}} \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}}`$, let

``` math
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal{N} & {= \cup_{\mathcal{L} \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots\}}} \cup_{{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}})} \in {({{({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{L}})} \times {\{ 1\}}})}}\left( \times_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})} \right),}
\end{aligned}
```

let $`\mathbf{A}_{d} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, and $`\mathbf{a} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$ be functions which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{\mathbf{A}_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {({\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{1})}},{\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{2})}},\ldots,{\mathbf{a}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{d})}})}},
```

let $`\mathcal{P}:{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}}`$ and $`\mathcal{R}:{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow{\cup_{d = 1}^{\infty}{C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}}}`$ be the functions which satisfy for all $`\mathcal{L} \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots\}}`$, $`{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}})} \in {({{({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{L}})} \times {\{ 1\}}})}`$, $`\Phi = {({(W_{1},B_{1})},\ldots,{(W_{\mathcal{L}},B_{\mathcal{L}})})} = {({(W_{k}^{(i,j)})}_{{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,l_{k}\}}},{j \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,l_{k - 1}\}}}},{(B_{k}^{(i)})}_{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,l_{k}\}}})}_{k \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,\mathcal{L}\}}} \in {( \times_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})})}`$, $`{x_{0} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{0}}},{{x_{1} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{1}},\ldots}},{x_{\mathcal{L} - 1} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{\mathcal{L} - 1}}}}`$ with $`{{\forall k} \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {(0,\mathcal{L})}}}:{x_{k} = {\mathbf{A}_{l_{k}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{k}\hspace{0pt}x_{k - 1}} + B_{k}})}}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{0}},{\mathbb{R}})}}},{{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{0})}} = {{W_{\mathcal{L}}\hspace{0pt}x_{\mathcal{L} - 1}} + B_{\mathcal{L}}}}},
```

and $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {\sum_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{l_{k}\hspace{0pt}{({l_{k - 1} + 1})}}}`$, and let $`{(\phi_{d,\delta})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}\delta^{- {\mathfrak{c}}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, $`\left| {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right| \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathfrak{c}}})}}`$, and

``` math
{\left| {{\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| \leq {{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}d^{\mathfrak{c}}\hspace{0pt}\delta\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{\mathfrak{c}}})}}}.
```

Then

1.  (i)
    there exist unique continuous functions $`u_{d}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{u_{d}|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{cl}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} \\
     & {+ {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\hspace{0pt}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}\hspace{0pt}\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}}
    \end{array}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
