    there exist $`{\mathfrak{C}} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- {\mathfrak{C}}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, and

    ``` math
    {\left\lbrack {\int_{{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}{dx}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```

###### Proof of Corollary 3.16.

Throughout this proof let $`m:{{(0,\infty)}\rightarrow{\lbrack 2,\infty)}}`$ be the function which satisfies for all $`z \in {(0,\infty)}`$ that $`{m\hspace{0pt}{(z)}} = {\max{\{ 2,z\}}}`$ and for every $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\nu_{d}:{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty\rbrack}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Lebesgue measure. Note that Lemma 3.15 (with $`d = d`$, $`p = {m\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{c}})}\hspace{0pt}m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}}`$, $`u = 0`$, $`v = 1`$, $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel = \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \right.`$ in the notation of Lemma 3.15) implies for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that

``` math
{{\int_{{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}^{d}}{{\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{({m\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{c}})}\hspace{0pt}m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}})}\hspace{0pt}{dx}}} \leq d^{{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{({m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}\hspace{0pt}m\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{c}})}})}}}.
```

This ensures that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {\sup\limits_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}\left\lbrack {{{d^{- {{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{({m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}\hspace{0pt}m\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{c}})}})}}}\hspace{0pt}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{({m\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{c}})}\hspace{0pt}m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}})}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}}}}|}_{{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}} \right\rbrack} \\
 & {{= {\sup\limits_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}\left\lbrack {d^{- {{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{({m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}\hspace{0pt}m\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{c}})}})}}}\hspace{0pt}{\int_{{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}^{d}}{{\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{({m\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{c}})}\hspace{0pt}m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}})}\hspace{0pt}{dx}}}} \right\rbrack} \leq 1 < \infty}.}
\end{array}
```

Theorem 3.14 (with $`T = T`$, $`r = r`$, $`R = 1`$, $`v = {\mathfrak{c}}`$, $`w = {\mathfrak{c}}`$, $`z = {\mathfrak{c}}`$, $`\mathbf{z} = {\mathfrak{c}}`$, $`\theta = {{m\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{c}})}}/2}`$, $`{\mathfrak{c}} = {\max{\{{\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{c}}},1\}}}`$, $`\mathbf{v} = {m\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{c}})}}`$, $`p = {m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}}`$, $`{(\nu_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} = {({\nu_{d}|}_{{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}^{d}})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}`$, $`{(\varphi_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} = {(\varphi_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}`$, $`{(\mu_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} = {(\mu_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}`$, $`{(\sigma_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} = {({\sqrt{2}\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{d}})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}`$, $`{(\phi_{d,\delta})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}}} = {(\phi_{d,\delta})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\delta \in {(0,r\rbrack}}}`$ in the notation of Theorem 3.14) hence ensures that

1.  (A)
    there exist unique continuous functions $`u_{d}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{u_{d}|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{cl}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}} \\
     & {+ {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\hspace{0pt}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}\hspace{0pt}\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}}
    \end{array}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (B)
    there exist $`C \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {C\hspace{0pt}d^{{{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{({5 + {\mathfrak{c}}})}\hspace{0pt}m\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{c}})}} + {\mathfrak{c}} + {\mathfrak{c}}^{2} + {4\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{c}}}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 4} - {\mathfrak{c}}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, and

    ``` math
    {\left\lbrack {{{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}}}|}_{{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}} \right\rbrack^{1/{m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}}} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```

This implies item (i). Moreover, note that (B) and the Hölder inequality demonstrate that for all $`{\mathfrak{C}} \in \left\lbrack {\max\left\{ C,{4 + {\mathfrak{c}}},{{{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{({5 + {\mathfrak{c}}})}\hspace{0pt}m\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{c}})}} + {\mathfrak{c}} + {\mathfrak{c}}^{2} + {4\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{c}}}} \right\}},\infty \right)`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {C\hspace{0pt}d^{{{1/2}\hspace{0pt}{({5 + {\mathfrak{c}}})}\hspace{0pt}m\hspace{0pt}{({\mathfrak{c}})}} + {\mathfrak{c}} + {\mathfrak{c}}^{2} + {4\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{c}}}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{{- 4} - {\mathfrak{c}}}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- {({4 + {\mathfrak{c}}})}}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- {\mathfrak{C}}}}
```

and

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left\lbrack {\int_{{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}{dx}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \\
 & {\leq \left\lbrack {\int_{{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}}\hspace{0pt}{dx}}} \right\rbrack^{1/{m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}}}} \\
 & {{= \left\lbrack {{{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}}}|}_{{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}^{d}}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}} \right\rbrack^{1/{m\hspace{0pt}{(p)}}} \leq \varepsilon}.}
\end{array}
```

This establishes item (ii). The proof of Corollary 3.16 is thus completed. ∎

## 4 Artificial neural network approximations for Black-Scholes partial differential equations

### 4.1 Elementary properties of the Black-Scholes model

In this subsection we establish in Lemma 4.2 below a few elementary properties of the coefficient functions in the Black-Scholes model. For the sake of completeness we also provide in this subsection a detailed proof of Lemma 4.2.

###### Setting 4.1.

Let $`p \in {\lbrack 2,\infty)}`$, $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`\theta \in {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$, $`{(\alpha_{d,i})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}}`$, $`{(\beta_{d,i})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}} \subseteq {\mathbb{R}}`$ satisfy that $`{\sup_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}}{({{|\alpha_{d,i}|} + {|\beta_{d,i}|}})}} < \infty`$, for every $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel{}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean norm, for every $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`{\langle \cdot , \cdot \rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the $`d`$-dimensional Euclidean scalar product, for every $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ let $`\left| \middle| \middle| \cdot \middle| \middle| \middle| {}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ be the Hilbert-Schmidt norm on $`{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, let $`e_{d,i} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}`$, satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`e_{d,1} = {(1,0,\ldots,0)}`$, $`e_{d,2} = {(0,1,0,\ldots,0)}`$, $`\ldots,`$ $`e_{d,d} = {(0,\ldots,0,1)}`$, let $`\mathbf{B}_{d} = {(\mathbf{B}_{d}^{(i,j)})}_{{i,j} \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}`$ that $`{\langle e_{d,i},{\mathbf{B}_{d}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{B}_{d}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}e_{d,i}}\rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} = 1`$, let $`\mu_{d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, and $`\sigma_{d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be the functions which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{({\alpha_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}},\ldots,{\alpha_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}})}\quad\text{and}}}\quad{{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{{diag}{({\beta_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}},\ldots,{\beta_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}})}}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{B}_{d}}}},
```

let $`\nu_{d}:{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be probability measures which satisfy for all $`q \in {(0,\infty)}`$ that

``` math
{{\sup\limits_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}\left\lbrack {d^{- {\theta\hspace{0pt}q}}\hspace{0pt}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}}} \right\rbrack} < \infty},
```

let

``` math
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal{N} & {= \cup_{\mathcal{L} \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots\}}} \cup_{{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}})} \in {({{({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{L}})} \times {\{ 1\}}})}}\left( \times_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})} \right),}
\end{aligned}
```

let $`\mathbf{A}_{d} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be the functions which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{\mathbf{A}_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {({\max{\{ x_{1},0\}}},{\max{\{ x_{2},0\}}},\ldots,{\max{\{ x_{d},0\}}})}},
```

and let $`{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}}:{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}}`$ and $`\mathcal{R}:{\mathcal{N}\rightarrow{\cup_{d = 1}^{\infty}{C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}}}`$ be the functions which satisfy for all $`\mathcal{L} \in {\{ 2,3,\ldots\}}`$, $`{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}})} \in {({{({\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{L}})} \times {\{ 1\}}})}`$, $`\Phi = {({(W_{1},B_{1})},\ldots,{(W_{\mathcal{L}},B_{\mathcal{L}})})} = {({(W_{k}^{(i,j)})}_{{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,l_{k}\}}},{j \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,l_{k - 1}\}}}},{(B_{k}^{(i)})}_{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,l_{k}\}}})}_{k \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,\mathcal{L}\}}} \in {( \times_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})})}`$, $`{x_{0} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{0}}},{{x_{1} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{1}},\ldots}},{x_{\mathcal{L} - 1} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{\mathcal{L} - 1}}}}`$ with $`{{\forall k} \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {(0,\mathcal{L})}}}:{x_{k} = {\mathbf{A}_{l_{k}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{k}\hspace{0pt}x_{k - 1}} + B_{k}})}}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{0}},{\mathbb{R}})}}},{{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{0})}} = {{W_{\mathcal{L}}\hspace{0pt}x_{\mathcal{L} - 1}} + B_{\mathcal{L}}}}},
```
``` math
{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {\sum\limits_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{l_{k}}\left( {{\mathbb{1}_{{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\{ 0\}}}\hspace{0pt}{(B_{k}^{(i)})}} + {\sum\limits_{j = 1}^{l_{k - 1}}{\mathbb{1}_{{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\{ 0\}}}\hspace{0pt}{(W_{k}^{(i,j)})}}}} \right)}}},
```

and $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {\sum_{k = 1}^{\mathcal{L}}{l_{k}\hspace{0pt}{({l_{k - 1} + 1})}}}`$.

###### Lemma 4.2.

Assume Setting 4.1. Then

1.  (i)
    it holds for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

    ``` math
    {{{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
    ```
2.  (ii)
    it holds for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

    ``` math
    {{{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} + {\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}},
    ```
    and
3.  (iii)
    for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{cl}
     & {{\left\| {\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} + {\|\left| {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}} \leq {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sup\limits_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}}{({{|\alpha_{d,i}|} + {|\beta_{d,i}|}})}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} \\
     & {{\leq {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sup\limits_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}}{({{|\alpha_{d,i}|} + {|\beta_{d,i}|}})}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}} < \infty}.}
    \end{array}
    ```

###### Proof of Lemma 4.2.

Throughout this proof let $`L \in {(0,\infty)}`$ be given by

``` math
{L = {\sup\limits_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}{\sup\limits_{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}{({{|\alpha_{d,i}|} + {|\beta_{d,i}|}})}}}}.
```

First, note that the fact that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{{diag}{(\alpha_{d,1},\ldots,\alpha_{d,d})}}\hspace{0pt}x}`$ and Lemma 2.7 prove item (i). Moreover, observe that (370) implies that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\lambda \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ it holds that $`{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}} = 0`$ and

``` math
{{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({{\lambda\hspace{0pt}x} + y})}} = {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}}}.
```

This establishes item (ii). In addition, note that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left\| {\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} & {= \left\| {({\alpha_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}},\ldots,{\alpha_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}})} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} = \left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{d}{|{\alpha_{d,i}\hspace{0pt}x_{i}}|}^{2}} \right\rbrack^{1/2}} \\
 & {\leq \left\lbrack {\left. ({\max{\{{|\alpha_{d,1}|},\ldots,{|\alpha_{d,d}|}\}}} \right)^{2}\hspace{0pt}{\sum_{i = 1}^{d}{|x_{i}|}^{2}}} \right\rbrack^{1/2}} \\
 & {= {{\max{\{{|\alpha_{d,1}|},\ldots,{|\alpha_{d,d}|}\}}}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}} \leq {L\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} \\
 & {{\leq {L\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}} < \infty}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, observe that the fact that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {({\beta_{d,i}\hspace{0pt}x_{i}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{B}_{d}^{(i,j)}})}_{{i,j} \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$ assures that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\|\left| {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}^{2} & {= {\sum\limits_{{i,j} = 1}^{d}\left| {\beta_{d,i}\hspace{0pt}x_{i}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{B}_{d}^{(i,j)}} \right|^{2}}} \\
 & {= {\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}\left( {{|x_{i}|}^{2}\hspace{0pt}{|\beta_{d,i}|}^{2}\hspace{0pt}{\sum_{j = 1}^{d}\left| \mathbf{B}_{d}^{(i,j)} \right|^{2}}} \right)}} \\
 & {\leq {\left\lbrack {\max\limits_{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}\left( {{|\beta_{d,i}|}^{2}\hspace{0pt}{\sum_{j = 1}^{d}\left| \mathbf{B}_{d}^{(i,j)} \right|^{2}}} \right)} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\sum\limits_{i = 1}^{d}{|x_{i}|}^{2}}}} \\
 & {{= {\left\lbrack {\max\limits_{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}\left( {{|\beta_{d,i}|}^{2}\hspace{0pt}{\sum_{j = 1}^{d}\left| \mathbf{B}_{d}^{(i,j)} \right|^{2}}} \right)} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{2}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The fact that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{\sum_{j = 1}^{d}\left| \mathbf{B}_{d}^{(i,j)} \right|^{2}} = {\langle{\mathbf{B}_{d}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}e_{d,i}},{\mathbf{B}_{d}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}e_{d,i}}\rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} = {\langle e_{d,i},{\mathbf{B}_{d}\hspace{0pt}\mathbf{B}_{d}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}e_{d,i}}\rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} = 1
```

hence demonstrates that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\|\left| {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}} & {\leq {\left\lbrack {\max\limits_{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}{|\beta_{d,i}|}^{2}} \right\rbrack^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} \\
 & {= {\left\lbrack {\max\limits_{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}{|\beta_{d,i}|}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}} \\
 & {{\leq {L\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}} \leq {L\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}} < \infty}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this and (381) assures that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{{\left\| {\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} + {\|\left| {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right|\|}_{{HS}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}} \leq {2\hspace{0pt}L\hspace{0pt}\left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}} \leq {2\hspace{0pt}L\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}})}} < \infty}.}
\end{array}
```

This establishes item (iii). The proof of Lemma 4.2 is thus completed. ∎

### 4.2 Transformations of viscosity solutions

In this subsection we establish in Proposition 4.3, Corollary 4.4, and Corollary 4.5 a few elementary and essentially well-known transformation results for viscosity solutions of certain second-order PDEs.

###### Proposition 4.3.

Let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{a,\lambda} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`b \in {(a,\infty)}`$, let $`f:{{{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a function which satisfies for all $`t \in {(a,b)}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\alpha \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`\eta \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`{A,B} \in {\{{C \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}:{C^{\ast} = C}\}}`$ with $`A \leq B`$ that

``` math
{{f\hspace{0pt}{(t,x,\alpha,\eta,A)}} \leq {f\hspace{0pt}{(t,x,\alpha,\eta,B)}}},
```

let $`u:{{{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a continuous function which satisfies that $`u`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {f\hspace{0pt}\left( t,x,{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, let $`R:{{\lbrack a,b\rbrack}\rightarrow{\lbrack a,b\rbrack}}`$ be the function which satisfies for all $`t \in {\lbrack a,b\rbrack}`$ that $`{R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}} = {{a + b} - t}`$, let $`U:{{{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the function which satisfies for all $`t \in {(a,b)}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{U\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}},x)}}`$, and let $`F:{{{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the function which satisfies for all $`t \in {(a,b)}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\alpha \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`\eta \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`A \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$ that

``` math
{{F\hspace{0pt}{(t,x,\alpha,\eta,A)}} = {f\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}},x,\alpha,\eta,A)}}}.
```

Then $`U:{{{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ is a continuous function which satisfies that $`U`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{- {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}U})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}} = {F\hspace{0pt}\left( t,x,{U\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({\nabla_{x}U})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}U})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$.

###### Proof of Proposition 4.3.

Throughout this proof let $`{(s,y)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, let

``` math
{{\Psi = {({\Psi\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}})}_{{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}},{\Phi = {({\Phi\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}})}_{{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}}}:{{{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}
```

be twice continuously differentiable functions which satisfy that $`\Phi \geq U`$, $`{\Phi\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}} = {U\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}}`$, $`\Psi \leq U`$, and $`{\Psi\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}} = {U\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}}`$, and let

``` math
{{\psi = {({\psi\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}})}_{{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}},{\varphi = {({\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}})}_{{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}}}:{{{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}
```

be the functions which satisfy for all $`{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\psi\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {{\Psi\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}},x)}}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {\Phi\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}},x)}}}}.
```

Observe that $`R:{{\lbrack a,b\rbrack}\rightarrow{\lbrack a,b\rbrack}}`$ is a bijective function which satisfies that $`{R|}_{(a,b)}:{{(a,b)}\rightarrow{(a,b)}}`$ is twice continuously differentiable and which satisfies for all $`{t \in {\lbrack a,b\rbrack}},`$ $`r \in {(a,b)}`$ that

``` math
{{{R\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}})}} = {t\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{{R'\hspace{0pt}{(r)}} = {- 1}}}.
```

Combining this and (392) ensures for all $`{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ that

``` math
{{{\Psi\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {{\psi\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}},x)}}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{{\Phi\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}},x)}}}}.
```

Therefore, we obtain that (392), (393), and the hypothesis that for all $`{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ it holds that $`{U\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}},x)}}`$ imply that for all $`{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ it holds that $`\psi \in {C^{2}\hspace{0pt}{({{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$,

``` math
{{\psi\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {\Psi\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}},x)}} \leq {U\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}},x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}})}},x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}},
```

and

``` math
{{\psi\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}} = {\Psi\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}} = {U\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}}}.
```

Moreover, observe that (392), (393), (394), and the hypothesis that for all $`{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ it holds that $`{U\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}},x)}}`$ demonstrate that for all $`{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ it holds that $`\varphi \in {C^{2}\hspace{0pt}{({{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$,

``` math
{{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {\Phi\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}},x)}} \geq {U\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}},x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}})}},x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}},
```

and

``` math
{{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}} = {\Phi\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}} = {U\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}}}.
```

Combining this, (395), and (396) with (387) implies that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}\varphi})}\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}} \leq {f\hspace{0pt}\left( {R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y,{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}},{{({\nabla_{x}\varphi})}\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}\varphi})}\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}} \right)}}
\end{array}
```

and

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{{\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}\psi})}\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}} \geq {f\hspace{0pt}\left( {R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y,{\psi\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}},{{({\nabla_{x}\psi})}\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}\psi})}\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}} \right)}}.}
\end{array}
```

This, (388), (393), and (394) ensure that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{- {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}\Phi})}\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}}} = {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}\varphi})}\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}}} \\
 & {\leq {f\hspace{0pt}\left( {R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y,{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}},{{({\nabla_{x}\varphi})}\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}\varphi})}\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}} \right)}} \\
 & {= {f\hspace{0pt}\left( {R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y,{\Phi\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}},{{({\nabla_{x}\Phi})}\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}\Phi})}\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}} \right)}} \\
 & {{= {F\hspace{0pt}\left( s,y,{\Phi\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}},{{({\nabla_{x}\Phi})}\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}\Phi})}\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}} \right)}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, observe that (388), (393), (394), and (400) demonstrate that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{- {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}\Psi})}\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}}} = {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}\psi})}\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}}} \\
 & {\geq {f\hspace{0pt}\left( {R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y,{\psi\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}},{{({\nabla_{x}\psi})}\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}\psi})}\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y)}} \right)}} \\
 & {= {f\hspace{0pt}\left( {R\hspace{0pt}{(s)}},y,{\Psi\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}},{{({\nabla_{x}\Psi})}\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}\Psi})}\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}} \right)}} \\
 & {{= {F\hspace{0pt}\left( s,y,{\Psi\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}},{{({\nabla_{x}\Psi})}\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}\Psi})}\hspace{0pt}{(s,y)}} \right)}}.}
\end{array}
```

Next note that the hypothesis that $`u:{{{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ is a continuous function and the hypothesis that for all $`t \in {(a,b)}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{U\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(t)}},x)}}`$ demonstrate that $`U:{{{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ is a continuous function. Combining this with (401) and (402) assures that $`U:{{{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ is a continuous function which satisfies that $`U`$ is a viscosity subsolution and a viscosity supersolution of

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{- {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}U})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}} = {F\hspace{0pt}\left( t,x,{U\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({\nabla_{x}U})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}U})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$. This proves that $`U:{{{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ is a continuous function which satisfies that $`U`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{- {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}U})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}} = {F\hspace{0pt}\left( t,x,{U\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({\nabla_{x}U})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}U})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(a,b)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$. The proof of Proposition 4.3 is thus completed. ∎

###### Corollary 4.4.

Let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{a,{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}},\lambda} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`b \in {{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\{ a\}}}`$, let $`\nu:{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty\rbrack}}`$ be a measure, let $`\varphi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a continuous function, let $`\Phi:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a $`{{\mathcal{B}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}\backslash\mathcal{B}}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}})}`$-measurable function, let $`f:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a function which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\alpha \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`\eta \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`{A,B} \in {\{{C \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}}:{C^{\ast} = C}\}}`$ with $`A \leq B`$ that

``` math
{{f\hspace{0pt}{(x,\alpha,\eta,A)}} \leq {f\hspace{0pt}{(x,\alpha,\eta,B)}}},
```

assume that $`{\mathfrak{a}} = {\min{\{ a,b\}}}`$ and $`{\mathfrak{b}} = {\max{\{ a,b\}}}`$, and assume that there exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(b,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies that

``` math
{{\inf\limits_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty},
```

and which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {f\hspace{0pt}\left( x,{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and it holds that

``` math
{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(a,x)}} - {\Phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
```

Then there exists a unique continuous function $`v:{{{\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{v\hspace{0pt}{(a,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies that

``` math
{{\inf\limits_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty},
```

which satisfies that $`{v|}_{{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{- {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}} = {f\hspace{0pt}\left( x,{v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({\nabla_{x}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and it holds that

``` math
{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{v\hspace{0pt}{(b,x)}} - {\Phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
```

###### Proof of Corollary 4.4.

Throughout this proof let $`v:{{{\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the function which satisfies for all $`t \in {\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{({{{\mathfrak{a}} + {\mathfrak{b}}} - t},x)}}`$. Note that for all $`t \in {\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{({{{\mathfrak{a}} + {\mathfrak{b}}} - t},x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{({{a + b} - t},x)}}}.
```

This and (408) ensure that

``` math
{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{v\hspace{0pt}{(b,x)}} - {\Phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
```

Next note that (407) and Proposition 4.3 (with $`d = d`$, $`a = {\mathfrak{a}}`$, $`\lambda = \lambda`$, $`b = {\mathfrak{b}}`$, $`{f\hspace{0pt}{(t,x,\alpha,\eta,A)}} = {f\hspace{0pt}{(x,\alpha,\eta,A)}}`$, $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}`$, $`{U\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}`$ for $`t \in {({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\alpha \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`\eta \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`A \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$ in the notation of Proposition 4.3) demonstrate that $`{v|}_{{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{- {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}} = {f\hspace{0pt}\left( x,{v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({\nabla_{x}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$. Furthermore, observe that (406), (412), and the hypothesis that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(b,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$ imply that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{v\hspace{0pt}{(a,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$ and

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\inf\limits_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} & {= {\inf\limits_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{({{{\mathfrak{a}} + {\mathfrak{b}}} - t},x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}}} \\
 & {{= {\inf\limits_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup\limits_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty}.}
\end{array}
```

Next let $`w:{{{\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a continuous function which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{w\hspace{0pt}{(a,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{w\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, which satisfies that $`{w|}_{{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{- {\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}w})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}} = {f\hspace{0pt}\left( x,{w\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({\nabla_{x}w})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}w})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, and which satisfies that

``` math
{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{w\hspace{0pt}{(b,x)}} - {\Phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon},
```

and let $`z:{{{\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be the function which satisfies for all $`t \in {\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{{z\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {w\hspace{0pt}{({{{\mathfrak{a}} + {\mathfrak{b}}} - t},x)}} = {w\hspace{0pt}{({{a + b} - t},x)}}}.
```

Observe that $`z`$ is a continuous function which satisfies that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{z\hspace{0pt}{(b,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{z\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, which satisfies that $`{z|}_{{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\lambda\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}z})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {f\hspace{0pt}\left( x,{z\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({\nabla_{x}z})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{{({{Hess}_{x}z})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ (cf. Proposition 4.3 (with $`d = d`$, $`a = {\mathfrak{a}}`$, $`\lambda = {- \lambda}`$, $`b = {\mathfrak{b}}`$, $`{f\hspace{0pt}{(t,x,\alpha,\eta,A)}} = {f\hspace{0pt}{(x,\alpha,\eta,A)}}`$, $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {w\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}`$, $`{U\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {z\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}`$ for $`t \in {({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}})}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\alpha \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`\eta \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`A \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$ in the notation of Proposition 4.3)), and which satisfies that

``` math
{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{z\hspace{0pt}{(a,x)}} - {\Phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
```

Hence, we obtain that for all $`t \in {\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{z\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}`$. The fact that for all $`t \in {\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{({{{\mathfrak{a}} + {\mathfrak{b}}} - t},x)}}`$ and $`{w\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {z\hspace{0pt}{({{{\mathfrak{a}} + {\mathfrak{b}}} - t},x)}}`$ therefore demonstrates that for all $`t \in {\lbrack{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{w\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {z\hspace{0pt}{({{{\mathfrak{a}} + {\mathfrak{b}}} - t},x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{({{{\mathfrak{a}} + {\mathfrak{b}}} - t},x)}} = {v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}}.
```

Combining this, the fact that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{v\hspace{0pt}{(a,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, (413), (414), and (415) completes the proof of Corollary 4.4. ∎

###### Corollary 4.5.

Assume Setting 4.1, let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{\varepsilon,T} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`\varphi \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, and $`\psi \in \mathcal{N}`$. Then the following two statements are equivalent:

1.  (i)
    There exists a unique continuous function $`u:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, which satisfies that $`{u|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{cl}
     & {{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} + \left\langle {{({\nabla_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\rangle_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}} \\
     & {{+ {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}} = 0}
    \end{array}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, and which satisfies that

    ``` math
    {\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```
2.  (ii)
    There exists a unique continuous function $`v:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ which satisfies for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{v\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfies that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, which satisfies that $`{v|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{cl}
    {{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}} \\
     & {+ \left\langle {{({\nabla_{x}v})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\rangle_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}
    \end{array}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, and which satisfies that

    ``` math
    {\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{v\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```

###### Proof of Corollary 4.5.

Observe that Corollary 4.4 (with $`d = d`$, $`a = 0`$, $`\lambda = {- 1}`$, $`b = T`$, $`\nu = \nu_{d}`$, $`{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, $`{\Phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, $`{f\hspace{0pt}{(x,\alpha,\eta,A)}} = {{\langle\eta,{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} + {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}A} \right)}}}`$, $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}`$ for $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\alpha \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`\eta \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`A \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$ in the notation of Corollary 4.5) proves that item (i) implies item (ii). Next note that Corollary 4.4 (with $`d = d`$, $`a = T`$, $`\lambda = 1`$, $`b = 0`$, $`\nu = \nu_{d}`$, $`{\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\varphi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, $`{\Phi\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, $`{f\hspace{0pt}{(x,\alpha,\eta,A)}} = {{\langle\eta,{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rangle}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} + {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}A} \right)}}}`$, $`{u\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} = {v\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}`$ for $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\alpha \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`\eta \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`A \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d}`$ in the notation of Corollary 4.5) proves that item (ii) implies item (i). The proof of Corollary 4.5 is thus completed. ∎

### 4.3 Artificial neural network approximations for basket call options

In this subsection we establish in Proposition 4.7 below that ANN approximations overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximations of the Black-Scholes model in the case of basket call options. Our proof of Proposition 4.7 employs the elementary ANN representation result for the payoff functions associated to basket call options in Lemma 4.6 below. For the sake of completeness we also provide in this subsection a detailed proof of Lemma 4.6.

###### Lemma 4.6.

Assume Setting 4.1 and let $`{{(c_{d,i})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}},{(K_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}} \subseteq {\mathbb{R}}`$. Then there exists $`{(\phi_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}} \leq {4\hspace{0pt}d}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, and

``` math
{{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\max{\{{{{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}} - K_{d}},0\}}}}.
```

###### Proof of Lemma 4.6.

Throughout this proof let $`{(\phi_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that

``` math
\phi_{d} = \left( {({(c_{d,1},c_{d,2},\ldots,c_{d,d})},{- K_{d}})},{(1,0)} \right) \in {{({{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times 1} \times {\mathbb{R}}})} \times {({{\mathbb{R}} \times {\mathbb{R}}})}}
```

(i.e., $`\phi_{d}`$ corresponds to a fully connected feedforward artificial neural network with 3 layers with dimensions $`(d,1,1)`$). Note that (373) and (374) ensure that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$ and

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} & {= {{1 \cdot {\max\left\{ {{\begin{pmatrix}
c_{d,1} & c_{d,2} & \cdots & c_{d,d}
\end{pmatrix}\hspace{0pt}x} + {({- K_{d}})}},0 \right\}}} + 0}} \\
 & {{= {\max{\{{{{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}} - K_{d}},0\}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, observe that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}} = {{1\hspace{0pt}{({d + 1})}} + {1\hspace{0pt}{({1 + 1})}}} = {d + 3} \leq {4\hspace{0pt}d}}.
```

This and (428) complete the proof of Lemma 4.6. ∎

###### Proposition 4.7.

Assume Setting 4.1, let $`{(c_{d,i})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}} \subseteq {\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}`$, $`{{(K_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} \in {(0,\infty)}},`$ and assume for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\sum_{i = 1}^{d}c_{d,i}} = 1`$. Then

1.  (i)
    there exist unique continuous functions $`u_{d}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} = {\max{\{{{{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}} - K_{d}},0\}}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{u_{d}|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{cl}
     & {{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} + \left\langle {{({\nabla_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\rangle_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}} \\
     & {{+ {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}} = 0}
    \end{array}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    there exist $`{\mathfrak{C}} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{5\hspace{0pt}\theta} + 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 4}}`$, $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{5\hspace{0pt}\theta} + 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, and

    ``` math
    {\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```

###### Proof of Proposition 4.7.

Throughout this proof let $`\varphi_{d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\max{\{{{{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}} - K_{d}},0\}}}
```

and let $`{{(\chi_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{(\phi_{d,\delta})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\chi_{d})}} \leq {4\hspace{0pt}d}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\chi_{d})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, $`{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\chi_{d})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$ (cf. Lemma 4.6), and $`\phi_{d,\delta} = \chi_{d}`$. Note that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}} = {\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\chi_{d})}} = \varphi_{d} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}}.
```

This implies that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{\left| {{\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| = \left| {{\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| = 0 \leq {d^{0}\hspace{0pt}\delta^{0}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{2}})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, observe that (433) and the hypothesis that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}`$ it holds that $`c_{d,i} \geq 0`$ and $`{\sum_{i = 1}^{d}c_{d,i}} = 1`$ assure that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{|{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}|} & {= {|{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\chi_{d})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}|} = {|{\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}|}} \\
 & {= {\max{\{{{{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}} - K_{d}},0\}}}} \\
 & {\leq {{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}{|x_{1}|}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}{|x_{2}|}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}{|x_{d}|}}}} \\
 & {\leq {\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{d}c_{d,i}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\max{\{{|x_{1}|},{|x_{2}|},\ldots,{|x_{d}|}\}}}}} \\
 & {{\leq \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \leq {d^{0}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{2}})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

In addition, observe that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}} = {\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\chi_{d})}} \leq {4\hspace{0pt}d} = {4\hspace{0pt}d^{1}\hspace{0pt}\delta^{- 0}}}.
```

Combining this, (433), (434), (435), the hypothesis that for all $`q \in {(0,\infty)}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\sup\limits_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}\left\lbrack {d^{- {\theta\hspace{0pt}q}}\hspace{0pt}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}}} \right\rbrack} < \infty},
```

and Lemma 4.2 with Theorem 3.14 (with $`T = T`$, $`r = 1`$, $`R = 1`$, $`v = 0`$, $`w = 0`$, $`z = 1`$, $`\mathbf{z} = 0`$, $`\theta = \theta`$, $`{\mathfrak{c}} = {\max{\{ 4,{2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sup_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}}{({{|\alpha_{d,i}|} + {|\beta_{d,i}|}})}} \right\rbrack}\}}}`$, $`\mathbf{v} = 2`$, $`p = p`$, $`\nu_{d} = \nu_{d}`$, $`\varphi_{d} = \varphi_{d}`$, $`\mu_{d} = \mu_{d}`$, $`\sigma_{d} = \sigma_{d}`$, $`{a\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\max{\{ x,0\}}}`$, $`\phi_{d,\delta} = \phi_{d,\delta}`$ for $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ in the notation of Theorem 3.14) demonstrates that there exist unique continuous functions $`v_{d}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{v_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{v_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{v_{d}|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}v_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}v_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}} \\
 & {+ \left\langle {{({\nabla_{x}v_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\rangle_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and that there exist $`{\mathfrak{C}} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{5\hspace{0pt}\theta} + 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 4}}`$, $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{5\hspace{0pt}\theta} + 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, and

``` math
{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{v_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
```

Corollary 4.5 hence assures that there exist unique continuous functions $`u_{d}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} = {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\max{\{{{{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}} - K_{d}},0\}}}},}
\end{array}
```

which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{u_{d}|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} + \left\langle {{({\nabla_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\rangle_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}} \\
 & {{+ {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}} = 0}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and that it holds for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this with the fact that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{5\hspace{0pt}\theta} + 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 4}}`$, $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{5\hspace{0pt}\theta} + 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}}`$, and $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$ establishes items (i)–(ii). The proof of Proposition 4.7 is thus completed. ∎

### 4.4 Artificial neural network approximations for basket put options

In this subsection we establish in Proposition 4.9 below that ANN approximations overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of the Black-Scholes model in the case of basket put options. Our proof of Proposition 4.9 employs the elementary ANN representation result for the payoff functions associated to basket put options in Lemma 4.8 below. For the sake of completeness we also provide in this subsection a detailed proof of Lemma 4.8.

###### Lemma 4.8.

Assume Setting 4.1 and let $`{(c_{d,i})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}} \subseteq {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`K \in {\mathbb{R}}`$. Then there exists $`{(\phi_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}} \leq {4\hspace{0pt}d}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, and

``` math
{{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\max{\{{K - {({{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}})}},0\}}}}.
```

###### Proof of Lemma 4.8.

Note that Lemma 4.6 (with $`c_{d,i} = {- c_{d,i}}`$, $`K_{d} = {- K}`$ for $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}`$ in the notation of Lemma 4.6) demonstrates that there exists $`{(\phi_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}} \leq {4\hspace{0pt}d}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, and

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} & {= {\max{\{{{{- {c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}}} - {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}} - \ldots - {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}} + K},0\}}}} \\
 & {{= {\max{\{{K - {({{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}})}},0\}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The proof of Lemma 4.8 is thus completed. ∎

###### Proposition 4.9.

Assume Setting 4.1 and let $`{(c_{d,i})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}} \subseteq {\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}`$, $`K \in {(0,\infty)}`$ satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\sum_{i = 1}^{d}c_{d,i}} = 1`$. Then

1.  (i)
    there exist unique continuous functions $`u_{d}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} = {\max{\{{K - {({{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}})}},0\}}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{u_{d}|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

    ``` math
    \begin{array}{cl}
     & {{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} + \left\langle {{({\nabla_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\rangle_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}} \\
     & {{+ {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}} = 0}
    \end{array}
    ```
    for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    there exist $`{\mathfrak{C}} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{5\hspace{0pt}\theta} + 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 4}}`$, $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{5\hspace{0pt}\theta} + 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, and

    ``` math
    {\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
    ```

###### Proof of Proposition 4.9.

Throughout this proof let $`\varphi_{d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

``` math
{\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\max{\{{K - {({{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}})}},0\}}}
```

and let $`{{(\chi_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{(\phi_{d,\delta})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\chi_{d})}} \leq {4\hspace{0pt}d}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\chi_{d})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, $`{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\chi_{d})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$ (cf. Lemma 4.8), and $`\phi_{d,\delta} = \chi_{d}`$. Note that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}} = {\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\chi_{d})}} = \varphi_{d} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}}.
```

This and the hypothesis that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}`$ it holds that $`c_{d,i} \in {\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}`$ and $`{\sum_{i = 1}^{d}c_{d,i}} = 1`$ assure that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{|{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}|} & {= {|{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\chi_{d})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}|} = {|{\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}|}} \\
 & {= {\max{\{{K - {({{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}})}},0\}}}} \\
 & {\leq {K + {c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}{|x_{1}|}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}{|x_{2}|}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}{|x_{d}|}}}} \\
 & {\leq {K + {\left\lbrack {\sum_{i = 1}^{d}c_{d,i}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\max{\{{|x_{1}|},{|x_{2}|},\ldots,{|x_{d}|}\}}}}}} \\
 & {\leq {K + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}} \leq {K + 1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{2}}} \\
 & {{\leq {{({K + 1})}\hspace{0pt}d^{0}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{2}})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

In addition, observe that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}} = {\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\chi_{d})}} \leq {4\hspace{0pt}d} = {4\hspace{0pt}d^{1}\hspace{0pt}\delta^{- 0}}}.
```

Furthermore, note that (448) ensures that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left| {{\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d,\delta})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| & {= \left| {{\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\chi_{d})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|} \\
 & {{= \left| {{\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| = 0 \leq {d^{0}\hspace{0pt}\delta^{0}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{2}})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this, (448)–(450), the fact that $`{(\varphi_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}`$ are continuous functions, the hypothesis that for all $`q \in {(0,\infty)}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\sup\limits_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}\left\lbrack {d^{- {\theta\hspace{0pt}q}}\hspace{0pt}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{{\| x\|}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}}} \right\rbrack} < \infty},
```

and Lemma 4.2 with Theorem 3.14 (with $`T = T`$, $`r = 1`$, $`R = 1`$, $`v = 0`$, $`w = 0`$, $`z = 1`$, $`\theta = \theta`$, $`\mathbf{z} = 0`$, $`{\mathfrak{c}} = {\max{\{ 4,{K + 1},{2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sup_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}}{({{|\alpha_{d,i}|} + {|\beta_{d,i}|}})}} \right\rbrack}\}}}`$, $`\mathbf{v} = 2`$, $`p = p`$, $`\nu_{d} = \nu_{d}`$, $`\varphi_{d} = \varphi_{d}`$, $`\mu_{d} = \mu_{d}`$, $`\sigma_{d} = \sigma_{d}`$, $`{a\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\max{\{ x,0\}}}`$, $`\phi_{d,\delta} = \phi_{d,\delta}`$ for $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`\delta \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ in the notation of Theorem 3.14) demonstrate that there exist unique continuous functions $`v_{d}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{v_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} = {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$, which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{v_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{v_{d}|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}v_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} & {= {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}v_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}} \\
 & {+ \left\langle {{({\nabla_{x}v_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\rangle_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and that there exist $`{\mathfrak{C}} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{5\hspace{0pt}\theta} + 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 4}}`$, $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{5\hspace{0pt}\theta} + 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, and

``` math
{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{v_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.
```

Corollary 4.5 hence assures that there exist unique continuous functions $`u_{d}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which satisfy that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(T,x)}} = {\varphi_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\max{\{{K - {({{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}} + {c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}} + \ldots + {c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}})}},0\}}}},}
\end{array}
```

which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{\inf_{q \in {(0,\infty)}}{\sup_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}\frac{|{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}|}{1 + \left\| x \right\|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}^{q}}}} < \infty`$, and which satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{u_{d}|}_{{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ is a viscosity solution of

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{{({\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\hspace{0pt}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} + \left\langle {{({\nabla_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}},{\mu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right\rangle_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}} \\
 & {{+ {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{{Trace}\left( {\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\sigma_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\rbrack}^{\ast}\hspace{0pt}{({{Hess}_{x}u_{d}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}} \right)}}} = 0}
\end{array}
```

for $`{(t,x)} \in {{(0,T)} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ and that it holds for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\left\lbrack {\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}{\left| {{u_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(0,x)}} - {{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right|^{p}\hspace{0pt}\nu_{d}\hspace{0pt}{({d\hspace{0pt}x})}}} \right\rbrack^{1/p} \leq \varepsilon}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this with the fact that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{5\hspace{0pt}\theta} + 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 4}}`$, $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{{5\hspace{0pt}\theta} + 1}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon^{- 2}}`$, and $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\psi_{d,\varepsilon})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$ establishes items (i)–(ii). The proof of Proposition 4.9 is thus completed. ∎

### 4.5 Artificial neural network approximations for call on max options

In this subsection we establish in Proposition 4.13 below that ANN approximations overcome the curse of dimensionality in the numerical approximation of the Black-Scholes model in the case of call on max options. Our proof of Proposition 4.13 employs the ANN representation result for the payoff functions associated to call on max options in Lemma 4.12 below. Our proof of Lemma 4.12, in turn, uses the elementary and essentially well-known facts in Lemmas 4.10–4.11. For the sake of completeness we also provide in this subsection detailed proofs of Lemmas 4.10–4.11.

###### Lemma 4.10.

Let $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ and let $`{( \cdot )}^{+}:{{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)}}`$ be the function which satisfies for all $`q \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that $`{(q)}^{+} = {\max{\{ q,0\}}}`$. Then

1.  (i)
    it holds that $`x = {{(x)}^{+} - {({- x})}^{+}}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    it holds that $`{\max{\{ x,y\}}} = {{({x - y})}^{+} + y}`$.
3.  (iii)
    it holds that $`{\min{\{ x,y\}}} = {{- {({x - y})}^{+}} + x}`$.

###### Proof of Lemma 4.10.

Observe that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{(x)}^{+} - {({- x})}^{+}} & {= {{\max{\{ x,0\}}} - {\max{\{{- x},0\}}}}} \\
 & {{= {{{\lbrack{x - 0}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}\mathbb{1}_{\lbrack 0,\infty)}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + {{\lbrack{0 - {({- x})}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}\mathbb{1}_{({- \infty},0)}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} = x}.}
\end{array}
```

This establishes item (i). Next note that

``` math
{{{({x - y})}^{+} + y} = {{\max{\{{x - y},0\}}} + y} = {\max{\{ x,y\}}}}.
```

This proves item (ii). Moreover, observe that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{- {({x - y})}^{+}} + x} & {= {- {({{\max{\{{x - y},0\}}} - x})}} = {- {\max{\{{- y},{- x}\}}}}} \\
 & {{= {\min{\{ y,x\}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

This establishes item (iii). The proof of Lemma 4.10 is thus completed. ∎

###### Lemma 4.11.

Let $`{(a_{n})}_{n \in {\mathbb{N}}} \subseteq {\lbrack 0,\infty)}`$ be the sequence which satisfies for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
a_{n} & {= {{({{2\hspace{0pt}{({n - 1})}} + 1})}\hspace{0pt}{({n + 1})}}} \\
 & {{{+ \left\lbrack {\sum_{k = 1}^{n - 1}{{({{2\hspace{0pt}{({n - {({k + 1})}})}} + 1})}\hspace{0pt}{({{2\hspace{0pt}{({n - k})}} + 1 + 1})}}} \right\rbrack} + {1\hspace{0pt}{({1 + 1})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Then it holds for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that

``` math
{a_{n} \leq {6\hspace{0pt}n^{3}}}.
```

###### Proof of Lemma 4.11.

Observe that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
a_{n} & {= {{({{2\hspace{0pt}{({n - 1})}} + 1})}\hspace{0pt}{({n + 1})}}} \\
 & {{+ \left\lbrack {\sum_{k = 1}^{n - 1}{{({{2\hspace{0pt}{({n - {({k + 1})}})}} + 1})}\hspace{0pt}{({{2\hspace{0pt}{({n - k})}} + 1 + 1})}}} \right\rbrack} + {1\hspace{0pt}{({1 + 1})}}} \\
 & {= {{{({{2\hspace{0pt}n} - 1})}\hspace{0pt}{({n + 1})}} + \left\lbrack {\sum_{k = 1}^{n - 1}{{({{2\hspace{0pt}{({n - k})}} - 1})}\hspace{0pt}{({{2\hspace{0pt}{({n - k})}} + 2})}}} \right\rbrack + 2}} \\
 & {= {{{{2\hspace{0pt}n^{2}} + n} - 1} + \left\lbrack {{{\sum_{k = 1}^{n - 1}{4\hspace{0pt}{({n - k})}^{2}}} + {2\hspace{0pt}{({n - k})}}} - 2} \right\rbrack + 2}} \\
 & {= {{2\hspace{0pt}n^{2}} + n + 1 + \left\lbrack {{{{\sum_{k = 1}^{n - 1}{4\hspace{0pt}n^{2}}} - {8\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}k}} + {4\hspace{0pt}k^{2}} + {2\hspace{0pt}n}} - {2\hspace{0pt}k} - 2} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {= {{{{2\hspace{0pt}n^{2}} + n + 1 + {4\hspace{0pt}n^{2}\hspace{0pt}{({n - 1})}}} - {8\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{k = 1}^{n - 1}k} \right\rbrack}} + {4\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{k = 1}^{n - 1}k^{2}} \right\rbrack}}} \\
 & {{{+ {2\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}{({n - 1})}}} - {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\sum_{k = 1}^{n - 1}k} \right\rbrack} - {2\hspace{0pt}{({n - 1})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The fact that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

``` math
{{\sum_{k = 1}^{n - 1}k} = {\frac{{({n - 1})}\hspace{0pt}n}{2}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{{\sum_{k = 1}^{n - 1}k^{2}} = \frac{{({n - 1})}\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}{({{2\hspace{0pt}n} - 1})}}{6}}
```

therefore assures for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
a_{n} & {= {{{{2\hspace{0pt}n^{2}} + n + 1 + {4\hspace{0pt}n^{2}\hspace{0pt}{({n - 1})}}} - {8\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \frac{{({n - 1})}\hspace{0pt}n}{2} \right\rbrack}} + {4\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \frac{{({n - 1})}\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}{({{2\hspace{0pt}n} - 1})}}{6} \right\rbrack}}} \\
 & {{+ {2\hspace{0pt}n\hspace{0pt}{({n - 1})}}} - {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \frac{{({n - 1})}\hspace{0pt}n}{2} \right\rbrack} - {2\hspace{0pt}{({n - 1})}}} \\
 & {= {{{2\hspace{0pt}n^{2}} + n + 1 + {4\hspace{0pt}n^{3}}} - {4\hspace{0pt}n^{2}} - \left\lbrack {{4\hspace{0pt}n^{3}} - {4\hspace{0pt}n^{2}}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {{{{+ \left\lbrack {{\frac{8}{6}\hspace{0pt}n^{3}} + {\frac{4\hspace{0pt}{({- 3})}}{6}\hspace{0pt}n^{2}} + {\frac{4}{6}\hspace{0pt}n}} \right\rbrack} + {2\hspace{0pt}n^{2}}} - {2\hspace{0pt}n} - \left\lbrack {n^{2} - n} \right\rbrack - {2\hspace{0pt}n}} + 2} \\
 & {= {{\left( {{4 - 4} + \frac{4}{3}} \right)\hspace{0pt}n^{3}} + {\left( {{{{{2 - 4} + 4} - 2} + 2} - 1} \right)\hspace{0pt}n^{2}}}} \\
 & {{+ {\left( {{{{1 + \frac{2}{3}} - 2} + 1} - 2} \right)\hspace{0pt}n}} + 1 + 2} \\
 & {{\leq {{\frac{4}{3}\hspace{0pt}n^{3}} + n^{2} + 3} \leq {{({\frac{4}{3} + 1 + 3})}\hspace{0pt}n^{3}} \leq {6\hspace{0pt}n^{3}}}.}
\end{array}
```

The proof of Lemma 4.11 is thus completed. ∎

###### Lemma 4.12.

Assume Setting 4.1 and let $`{{(K_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{(c_{d,i})}_{{d \in {\mathbb{N}}},{i \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,d\}}}}} \subseteq {\mathbb{R}}`$. Then there exists $`{(\phi_{d})}_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}} \subseteq \mathcal{N}`$ such that for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}} \leq {6\hspace{0pt}d^{3}}`$, $`{\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, and

``` math
{{{({\mathcal{R}\hspace{0pt}{(\phi_{d})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\max{\{{{\max{\{{c_{d,1}\hspace{0pt}x_{1}},{c_{d,2}\hspace{0pt}x_{2}},\ldots,{c_{d,d}\hspace{0pt}x_{d}}\}}} - K_{d}},0\}}}}.
```

###### Proof of Lemma 4.12.

Throughout this proof let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, let

``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
\phi & {= {({(W_{1},B_{1})},{(W_{2},B_{2})},\ldots,{(W_{d},B_{d})},{(W_{d + 1},B_{d + 1})})}} \\
 & {\in {({{\mathbb{R}}^{{({{2\hspace{0pt}{({d - 1})}} + 1})} \times d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{{2\hspace{0pt}{({d - 1})}} + 1}})}} \\
 & {\times {( \times_{k = 1}^{d - 1}{({\mathbb{R}}^{{({{2\hspace{0pt}{({d - k})}} - 1})} \times {({{2\hspace{0pt}{({d - k})}} + 1})}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{{2\hspace{0pt}{({d - k})}} - 1})})}} \\
 & {\times {({{\mathbb{R}}^{1 \times 1} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{1}})}}
\end{array}
```

(i.e. $`\phi`$ corresponds to fully connected feedforward artificial neural network with $`d + 2`$ layers with dimensions $`(d,{{2\hspace{0pt}{({d - 1})}} + 1},{{2\hspace{0pt}{({d - 2})}} + 1},{{2\hspace{0pt}{({d - 3})}} + 1},\ldots,3,1,1)`$) satisfy for all $`k \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,{d - 2}\}}`$ that

``` math
{W_{1} = \begin{pmatrix}
c_{d,1} & {- c_{d,2}} & 0 & \cdots & 0 \\
0 & c_{d,2} & 0 & \cdots & 0 \\
0 & {- c_{d,2}} & 0 & \cdots & 0 \\
0 & 0 & c_{d,3} & \cdots & 0 \\
0 & 0 & {- c_{d,3}} & \cdots & 0 \\
\vdots & \vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots \\
0 & 0 & 0 & \cdots & c_{d,d} \\
0 & 0 & 0 & \cdots & {- c_{d,d}}
\end{pmatrix} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{{({{2\hspace{0pt}{({d - 1})}} + 1})} \times d}},
```
``` math
\begin{array}{cl}
W_{k + 1} & {= \begin{pmatrix}
1 & 1 & {- 1} & {- 1} & 1 & 0 & 0 & \ldots & 0 & 0 \\
0 & 0 & 0 & 1 & {- 1} & 0 & 0 & \ldots & 0 & 0 \\
0 & 0 & 0 & {- 1} & 1 & 0 & 0 & \ldots & 0 & 0 \\
0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & 1 & {- 1} & \ldots & 0 & 0 \\
0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & {- 1} & 1 & \ldots & 0 & 0 \\
\vdots & \vdots & \vdots & \vdots & \vdots & \vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots & \vdots \\
0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & \ldots & 1 & {- 1} \\
0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & 0 & \ldots & {- 1} & 1
\end{pmatrix}} \\
```
